•	Flow check the well.
•	Pump slug. Use the same slug density and volume on every trip. Record slug details on the IADC report. Let the slug stabilize and confirm that the returned volume is correct
•	Pull out of the hole.  Do not exceed the tested maximum hoisting speed. Monitor the well with the trip tank. Flow check the well if any discrepancy occurs. Shut in immediately if any flow is observed.
•	Flow check prior to pulling the drill pipe across the BOP.
•	Install a safety valve on the string if the trip is interrupted for any reason.  Leave the valve open to monitor the well.
•	When out of the hole, circulate across the hole on the trip tank with the hole fill pump. Keep the shear rams open. The Driller will be on the floor at all times, unless relieved by the Toolpusher. The mudloggers will set level alarms and continuously monitor the trip tank and will notify the Driller of any discrepancy.
•	The time out of the hole should be kept to the minimum, consistent with operational requirements and with regard to the hole stability. The hole should be kept full and monitored continuously

2.19.5.F	TRIPPING PROCEDURES - TRIPPING IN
•	When tripping back into the hole, monitor the well on the trip tank with the hole fill pump running.
•	Limit running speed to the value from the closed-end pipe surge pressure calculations.
•	Stage back in hole by stopping and breaking circulation every 3000 feet.
•	Fill the pipe every 10/20 stands when running in the hole. Flow check the well if there is a volume discrepancy. Shut in immediately if any flow is observed.
•	Always circulate bottoms up when back on bottom.  If gas levels are expected to be high or are increasing, consider closing the annular and circulating through an open choke as the mud from the open hole nears surface while slowly reciprocating the drill string.  Continue circulating until gas levels drop.

2.19.5.G	WELL CONTROL GUIDELINES
Refer to: Well Control Manual
•	Personnel safety is of primary importance.  If the Toolpusher and/or Drilling Supervisor believe that there is danger of losing control of the well, the OIM will take any actions necessary to ensure the safety of all personnel on the rig.
•	The well will be shut in using the hard shut-in procedure with the pipe rams.
•	It will be desirable to keep the drill string moving if conditions permit in order to avoid differential sticking; however, high surface pressure will not permit this and the kill joint must be used.  Procedures to be followed after an influx is detected are as follows.
While drilling:
1	Pick up to position the drill pipe across the pipe ram and the safety valves above the rotary table.  
2	Shut in the well. 
3	Check SIDPP:  
If below 2000 psi:
1	Close the annular
2	Open the rams.
3	Consider reciprocating the drill string as soon as possible.
If above 2000 psi:
1	Shut the safety valve
2	Break out the top drive
3	Make up the kill joint
4	Make up the top drive
5	Make up 2 Lo-Torq valves and kill hose to the pump-in sub side outlet.
6	Pressure test kill hose to 10,000 psi.
7	Equalize pressure across the safety valve and open the safety valve.
While tripping:
1	Position the drill pipe across the pipe ram with tool joint at a working height above the rotary table.  
2	Make up a safety valve and close it.
3	Shut in the well. 
4	Make up the top drive.
5	Equalize pressure across the safety valve and open the safety valve.
6	Check SIDPP:  
If below 2000 psi:
1	Close the annular
2	Open the rams.
3	Consider reciprocating the drill string as soon as possible.
If above 2000 psi
1	Shut the safety valve
2	Break out the top drive
3	Make up the kill joint
4	Make up the top drive
5	Make up 2 Lo-Torq valves and kill hose to the pump-in sub side outlet.
6	Pressure test kill hose to 10000 psi.
7	Equalize pressure across the safety valve and open the safety valve.
Note: It will be necessary to pump open the float to obtain accurate SIDPP.
•	Inform the OIM immediately. The OIM has the responsibility to alert all personnel at his discretion.
•	The person directly in control of the killing operation will be the Senior Toolpusher in full consultation with the Senior Drilling Supervisor and the OIM.  Should unexpected or abnormal conditions develop, shut in the well and reassess the situation.
•	Estimate the maximum surface pressure and maximum pit gain based on the SIDPP and initial pit gain for the first circulation of the Driller's Method to be able to check that surface pressure and pit gains are within the estimated values.
•	Use the Driller's Method to kill the well.  In the event of a large influx and/or high surface pressure, particularly if the drill string must be stripped back to bottom, consider bullheading prior to implementing the Driller's Method.
•	Start injecting glycol upstream of the choke when the influx is nearing surface and continue injecting until the influx has been removed from the well. 
•	The MGS mud seal will be circulated with mud at the mud weight in use at all times during a well kill.
•	Continuously monitor the MGS pressure to ensure that overloading does not occur. Set values for the controller box alarm and the dump controller box signal to automatically vent will be calculated based on the mud weight circulated in the mud seal with the hot loop system as per drilling contractor work instructions.

2.19.5.H	CONTINGENCY PLANS
2.19.5.H.1	LOST CIRCULATION
1	Keep the hole full
2	Minor losses will be treated with LCM.
3	Severe losses will be treated with cement plugs.
4	In the event of a repetitive loss / gain situation, ECD drilling techniques, LCM treatment of the mud system, and, if necessary, cement plugs set through the drilling assembly will be used to drill ahead.  It is possible that the formation could become supercharged in a loss / gain situation.  In this case it will be necessary to bleed back mud.   Attachment C shows an example of the type of procedure to be followed if supercharging is suspected.  Contact office for detailed instructions in the event of suspected supercharging.	
5	If adequate wellbore integrity cannot be achieved by these means, i.e., severe lost circulation or severe loss/gain situation accompanied by high hydrocarbon levels, a liner may be set and drilling continued in smaller hole.  Alternatively, the well may be plugged back and sidetracked to place casing points differently.
2.19.5.H.2	INSUFFICIENT CASING SHOE STRENGTH
1.	The casing/liner shoe will be squeezed with cement if the FIT pressure is insufficient to provide a safe margin to drill to TD.
2.	If the casing/liner shoe FIT pressure remains inadequate even after remedial cementing, the well may be plugged back and sidetracked to place casing points differently.
2.19.5.H.3	CASING PRESSURE TESTS
1.	Test the casing as per the drilling program prior to drilling out the casing / liner shoe
2.	After drilling inside a casing for 30 days, the casing integrity should be checked by re-pressure testing or caliper log,

2.19.5.I	WELL CONTROL EQUIPMENT SPECIFICATIONS
Ensure that the following equipment is available and fit for HPHT operations:
1.	Suitable BOP stack as per well control policy
2.	High Pressure Well Kill Facilities, if mud pumps unable to handle anticipated SIDDP
o	The rated working pressure of the cementing lines shall be the same as the BOP, which will not be less than 10,000psi. A cementing line should be connected to the kill lines.
3.	Hydrate Suppressant Injection Facilities
o	C/K manifold must have antifreeze injection facility.
4.	Low Pressure Gauges 
5.	Mud Gas Separator 
o	The size of the mud-gas separator should be such that it can handle the fluid and gas which is bled off via the choke manifold safely
o	The gas outlet line shall be a minimum of 8". If the vent exists more than 130 ft from the vessel this vent line diameter should be increased to ensure that the backpressure in the vent line does not exceed 2 psi, at a gas flow rate of 7 MMscf/d. No reduction in any vessel dimension is permitted.
o	The mud seal should be at least 10ft high.
o	The mud-gas separator shall have one or more vent lines leading a safe distance downwind from the well, and/or to the top of the derrick. Low places in the vent lines should be avoided in order to prevent liquids being trapped in them
6.	Degasser
7.	Well Effluent Property Monitoring (Mudlogging equipment & drilling contractor equipment)
o	An indicating and recording mud pit level system, capable of providing early visual and audible warning of gain or loss of fluid in the well, shall be installed in those mud tanks, which serve as active mud tanks.
o	A mud return indicator with an audible alarm shall be installed on the flow line.
o	The complete system must be kept in proper working condition at all times
o	An indicator or recording chart must be easily visible to the driller

2.20	INSTALL X'MAS TREE CAP CHECKLIST

2.20.1	PLATFORM OPERATIONS
2.20.1.A	GENERAL
•	Verify whether there is a program.  If so, ensure that the requirements are understood and can be performed in a safe manner.
•	Ensure to identify any heavy lifts.
•	Ensure to hold a Toolbox meeting prior to the job with all relevant personnel to discuss safety and the planned operation.
•	Verify that all the equipment is on board prior to commencement of the job.
•	Ensure to inspect the hanger neck before it's been cleaned to determine the effectiveness of the flushing. Once the hanger neck is cleaned, confirm correct alignment.  Ensure no debris in control line port.
•	Ensure that the tree cap top is level with top of hanger neck.
•	Ensure that the Xmas Tree orientation is correct, check with program or the Project Drilling Engineer.  The general idea is to minimize the offset between the 2 ports and still allow clear access for the SSR port stinger.
•	Ensure to remove any debris sitting on top of the plugs.
•	Check that the 'O' rings in tree cap are OK and not sitting proud.  This is to be done on deck and not immediately prior to installation.
•	Clean groove and install gasket in top groove of tubing spool.
•	Ensure studs and nuts are made up uniformly and in proper sequence to recommended torque.
•	Ensure to inspect the hanger neck before landing the tree cap and repair any score marks.
•	Ensure to sting the SSR test port when pressure testing the DHSV control line and vice versa.
•	Verify any indications of the flapper opening & check for fluid returns.  Check if returns are clean.
•	Confirm access is OK for test port stingers.
•	Ensure pressure test of connection to rated working pressure of equipment.
2.21	LINER CEMENTING CHECKLIST

General Considerations

2.21.1	GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS
•	Review the liner cementing program to ensure requirements are understood and obtainable.
•	Confirm thickening time sufficient for the job requirements.
•	Ensure cement & water samples are forwarded in adequate time for testing. Review results as soon as available w/ engineering & service company
•	Ensure that batch mixing equipment is on location, clean, and in good working order.
•	Ensure that the tanks used for mix water are clean.
•	Ensure actual mix water temperature and salinity is within design parameter 
•	Verify stand back area boards are in good condition to prevent debris from entering DP.
•	Verify rig equipment adequate for the pumping rate & pressure rating.
•	Verify that rig handling capacities, slings, spreader bars, space for tubulars, sufficient Chicksans & lines are adequate and fit for purpose.
•	Ensure that actual BHST as measured from logging is checked against estimated BHST used for design.  If substantial differences, modify cement slurry design.
•	Ensure mix water volumes take dead volume of tanks into account.
•	Check preventive maintenance for permanent equipment.
•	Confirm cementing lines secured & tested.
•	Check back-up equipment (if/when available) for bulk air compressor, mix water lines/pumps, etc.
•	Be sure that dry air is always used to handle cement.
•	Ensure there is a X-over from the casing thread to the drill pipe for the safety sub.
•	Ensure Safety Sub ID is full opening & OD will allow casing to be run to bottom without clearance problems.  Ensure top of X-over is beveled to allow ease of pulling out of hole.
•	Verify that there are proper plans for all related transportation matters, such as cement barge, workboats, and helicopters.
•	Verify sufficient cement & additives for casing job & squeeze work, if required.
•	Be prepared to handle the large mud volume that will be displaced from the hole during the cementing operation.  Plan for disposal or storage.
•	Collect a sample of cement from each compartment of each bulk tanks and sample of the mix water.
•	Verify cement location i.e. silos, permanent tanks, multiple tanks.
•	Evaluate economics for personnel & equipment standby costs vs. rig time.
•	Review hole geometry and mud properties to determine potential for problems.
•	Review prior to the job, additional personnel needed such as toolhands, field engineer, cementing supervisor, helpers, casing crews, mud engineer, etc.
•	Discuss and develop contingencies with well site team: Do you need a wiper trip after logging? What action will be taken if: some of the cement pumps are lost, lost returns, early returns, inadequate density maintenance, plug doesn't bump, stage cementing tools fails to open, liner doesn't reach bottom, floats don't hold, backside flow after job, liner parts or splits, plugged floats, lost foreign material, weather etc.
•	Recheck volume & temperature determinations for the job, using log data for caliper and temperature.
•	Check and understand the type of liquid additive system being used, if required.
•	Confirm correct volumes & sufficient quantity (excess if required) of mix water additives, cement, pit volume, plugs, spacers, and displacement fluids.
•	Visually inspect all tanks that will be used in transfers.
•	Wellsite Supervisor & Cementer must prepare & compare independent mixing & displacement calculations.
•	Verify the following liner cementing calculations:
1.	Weight of liner prior to hang off.
2.	Minimum circulation volume
3.	Preflush additive requirement.
4.	Volume of preflush.
5.	Possible reduction of hydrostatic due to preflush.
6.	Volume of slurry.
7.	Cement, water & additive requirements
8.	Capacity of cement line.
9.	Displacement volume to land pump down plug in liner wiper plug.  Allow for internal joint upsets. 
10.	Displacement volume when cement leaves the liner shoe
11.	Displacement volume to land the wiper volume on landing collar
12.	Displacement volume when cement arrives at previous shoe.
13.	Displacement volume when cement arrives at the liner hanger.
14.	Hydrostatic pressure at previous shoe compared to leak off.
15.	Buoyant running string weight.
16.	Calculate the maximum imposed tension loading on the top of the drill pipe and at the liner hanger when the plug bumps.
•	Confirm the following calculations are available (Cementing program & service company engineer):
1.	Maximum possible returns from the cement job, ignoring circulating losses & maximum displacement volume to catch the "U tube" cement.
2.	Maximum slurry height above liner hanger, assuming gauge hole.
3.	Theoretical differential pressure to bump plug.
4.	Theoretical differential pressure when running tool pulled out of hanger with all excess cement in casing
5.	Maximum allowable circulating & displacing rates to stay within an acceptable ECD for leakoff.
•	Ensure good communications between the rig floor and the cementing unit.  Consider radio headsets with microphones for key personnel.
•	Ensure that displacement tanks are calibrated correctly.
•	If displacing with rig pumps, test pump efficiency prior to the cement job.

2.21.2	JOB EXECUTION
•	Perform Pre Job Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel to review and discuss the operational and safety aspects of the job.
•	Collect dry samples during the job when 15-20%, 45-50%, and 80-85% of the cement has been transferred for a total of 3 samples min.  Collect liquid additive samples prior to job and mix water w/ additives during job.
•	Ensure the use of pressurized balances & that they are calibrated. 
•	Ensure that the pumping simulation schedules are used to determine optimum flow rates and identify downhole reactions (i.e., freefall, U-tubing, ECD caused by restriction between liner hanger & casing, etc.).
•	Ensure minimum overbalance is maintained on the reservoir through out the liner cementation.
•	Verify type of liquid additive being used and understand how it works. (Refer to LAS checklist.)
•	Ensure the use pump down plug cementing head for liners.  Check head for proper operation and possible special bail requirements.
•	Ensure adequate spacer mixing facilities on the rig.
•	If stab-in liner tools are utilized, ensure caution to prevent collapse of casing should the hole bridge off.
•	Ensure that the surface lines are manifolded in advance to facilitate plug release without having to break connections to drop plugs.
•	Use compatible spacer or wash volumes as per job design.
•	Check the bulk delivery rate to ensure sufficient feed rate. 
•	Dryness of supply air is an important factor and ensure that every effort is made to keep the air dry for blowing the cement.
•	Ensure preparations for cement returns and determine in advance where to put it.
•	Do not pull the bulk tank(s) dry.
•	Batch mix all cement slurries for liners if possible.  Mix energy from batch mixing can affect key cementing parameters i.e. reduced thickening time.
•	Warm mix water can reduce TT.  Ensure actual mix water temperature is within design parameter.
•	Liquid additives may be mixed in the cement displacement tanks if pre-job mixing is not possible.
•	Ensure that dry additives are premixed prior to the job.  They do not wet mix satisfactorily in a short period of time.
•	Dryness of supply air is an important factor.
•	Slow down prior to dart latching plug.
•	Pump calculated displacement only. Upon displacement of the top plug, the liner should be tested after the floats have been checked.
•	Consider the following:
1.	Displace pump down plug with cement unit until it lands and shears the liner wiper plug.
2.	Slow the pump down to +/- 2 BPM prior to the pump down plug reaching the liner wiper plug, to observe the shear.
•	Continue displacing until both plugs land on the landing collar or upon reaching calculated displacement. Reduce displacement rate further prior to bump. Record final displacement pressure.
1.	If plugs bump, test the liner to pressure indicated as per the liner cementing program.
2.	If the liner wiper plug shear out was not seen, use calculated displacement volume only. Do not pump excess if plug fails to bump.  If shear was seen, use the calculated liner volume from the time the shear was seen.
3.	Displacement volume must be checked from the displacement tanks.
4.	Record pre-bump static differential at calculated theoretical displacement volume.  Compare with pre-calculated theoretical pressure.
•	Ensure to prepare plan for activities to be done while WOC.
•	During the job check the volumes pumped using material balance computations against the barrel counters on the cementing unit.
•	Ensure cement displacement during liner jobs is stopped on volume.  Do not over displace.
•	After landing the top plug, check for backflow.  If none occurs, leave the casing open.  If the float doesn't hold, close the casing, but monitor pressure buildup until the cement sets up.  Do not let the pressure buildup in the casing exceed the differential pressure required to displace the plug to the float collar (the maximum surface pressure just before the plug bumps).
•	Reversing: The decision to reverse must be clearly decided and communicated in advance of the job.  Reversing should normally be avoided.
•	Upon conclusion, measure & record cement and mix water volumes.  Use material balance calculations to confirm cement was pumped as designed.  Make note of differences on job report.
•	The job is not finished until all reporting has been completed!

2.21.3	REFERENCES
Casing Liner and Tubing Pressure Testing
Testing of Cement Mixing and Pumping Equipment
Operational Requirements for Cement Slurry
Cement Placement

2.22	LINER RUNNING CHECKLIST

2.22.1	LOAD-OUT OF LINER AND EQUIPMENT AT DOCKS
•	Liner inventory at dock is as per the liner program, i.e. thread types, length, size (OD & ID, drift), grade, and weight.
•	Visually inspect all tubulars for condition and integrity and the required inspection documentation is aboard.
•	Verify that the make-up and drifting of the liner hanger equipment in vendor's shop has been witnessed and documented by QA/QC Department.
•	Verify that threads properly treated and protected prior to loading on trucks/boats.
•	Verify that all liner equipment has been pressure tested, drifted and documented. Insist on copies of inspection and test reports.
•	Ensure proper space-out subs and selection of pups, both with appropriate threads for crossovers and handing liner are available.
•	If the liner has flush-joint or other threads that require the use of lift plugs check the plug threads to be sure that they are clean, undamaged, and compatible with the liner threads.
•	Check that the liner hanger equipment is delivered to the rig well in advance to verify that it is "fit for purpose."
•	Follow the racking and handling guidelines as follows:
1.	Handle with nylon or cable slings. 
2.	Do not handle casing in bundles more than 3 joints unless the pipe is packaged for lifting. 
3.	Check that thread protectors are the correct type and properly secured.
4.	Avoid rough handling that may damage the pipe/liner equipment.
5.	Do not stack pipe too high.  The API recommendation that pipe should not be racked more than five tiers, or 10 ft high at the wellsite.
6.	Use 2" X 4" dunnage to separate successive layers of pipe.  
•	Specify pick-up criteria for the wellsite, threads to bow or stern, as required along with any special loading instructions for sandblasted, or externally coated/treated casing, special threads handling.
•	Verify that rig has appropriate drifts for DP and liner equipment to be run.

2.22.2	JOB PREPARATION
•	Review the program with the Toolpusher and OIM for content, clarity, operational and rig equipment limitations. Ask questions if unsure about anything.
•	Ensure that liner service personnel are on location two days prior to running equipment for pre-operational checks of tools and equipment.
•	Check the compatibility of setting ball and shear out seat, and that setting ball goes through the Liner wiper plug system. Also check the compatibility of the Liner wiper plug with the DP dart and the liner casing.
•	Caliper the ID, at least 10% of the Liner and use the average ID for calculating displacement. This has proven to be a lot more accurate than using the API ID.
•	Check the ball seat shear pins in the landing collar.
•	Check and load the Surface Dart Launcher.
1.	Check for proper connections on Launcher.
2.	Ensure darts are compatible with drill pipe.
3.	Ensure that both top and bottom plugs are from the same manufacturer and that they are the correct size and compatible for seating and eventual sealing.
•	Drift all drill pipe crossovers and tools (including the liner setting tool) used to run the liner to ensure passage of the drillpipe wiper plugs, balls or darts.
•	Review the operation of the particular liner hanger being used, know how it is made up, run, set and what can be done in emergency situations.
•	Ensure that the liner is properly tallied, with joints numbered, verify ID's, OD's.
•	The liner hanger's maximum OD should be calipered to ensure passage through the BOP stack, wellhead, wearbushing and heaviest casing
•	Ensure that all handling equipment is fully strapped and calipered, and schematics are available.
•	Visually inspect the drillpipe, hook, elevator, bails, slips, liner hanger, setting tool and all other components in the string prior to running the liner.
•	Ensure that the circulating subs and swages fit the drillpipe and liner.
•	Ensure that drill pipe safety valves with proper connections are available.
•	Verify the requirement to change one set of BOP rams to fit liner OD.
•	Strap, drift / rabbit and number the stands as they are pulled out of the hole to pick up the liner.
•	Rack the drill collars to ensure that they are arranged to allow the maximum amount of space on the floor for picking up the liner.
•	If the liner is flush joint, ensure care is taken to protect the connections. 
•	Confirm shoe track equipment; number of joints, landing collar and type of floats.
•	Premium connections may require torque turning.
•	Use the equipment list to verify that all handling tools are aboard.
•	Ensure that the power tong and associated equipment are "fit for purpose."
•	Check calibration of torque gauges
•	Ensure that the torque gauge is correct for the tong arm length.
•	Ensure that tong line & tong arm is at right angles to each other and horizontal.
•	If liner has chrome content, use canvas slings to handle.
•	Ensure that there is a valid certification package for the liner handling equipment.
•	Ensure that sufficient mud and mud products are available should losses occur while running the casing or cementing.
•	Check the mud pits to be certain that the mud used for filling up the casing is the correct weight and that there is enough room in the pits to accommodate the gain that will result from the casing displacement.  Transfer mud as required.  Make sure the shakers are on and lined up correctly
•	Check the cementer's hook up.  Have the cement unit and manifold on standby, ready for duty.   Flush and pressure test all relevant lines prior to cementing.  Pressure test to 1000 psig above highest anticipated pressure to be encountered during performance of cementing operation.
•	Slip and cut drill line on trip out for casing.
•	Establish DP frictional torque values; ensure that DP Rotational Tests are performed (using the same sizes and combination of DP that will be used for the Liner Running String) at both 10 rpm and 20 rpm with the Drill String at both TD and at the last Casing Shoe on the last Trip out of the hole.
•	Note the total number of liner joints on pipe rack.
•	Ensure that the total number of drill pipe on location is counted.
•	Ensure proper fishing tools are available for this particular operational sequence.

2.22.3	RUNNING LINER
•	Ensure the well is circulated and conditioned as per drilling program prior to POH to running the liner.
•	Review hole stability before tripping to run liner (Refer to Tripping Checklist).
•	Function test BOPE, or test all BOPE as required, prior to running liner.
•	Confirm rig maintenance to ensure cutting and slipping of drill line will not be necessary while running liner.
•	Review liner and drill pipe displacement criteria.
•	Conduct Toolbox meeting for all relevant rig personnel to review and discuss the liner program and safety issues.
•	Discuss the BOP shut-in procedures while running the liner
•	Ensure that proper handling tools are on rig floor and rigged-up as much as possible off the critical operation path.
•	Ensure that the stabbing board is properly in place and safe.
•	Confirm operation and/or condition of trip tanks, mud tank levels and transfer pumps.
•	Verify make-up torque, especially if the liner is to be rotated.
•	Visually ensure all thread initial make-up is proper to eliminate thread damage.
•	Ensure circulation at the last casing shoe.
•	Verify wash down / rotating criteria, if any, along with circulating and conditioning (staging) criteria while running in the hole.
•	Verify landing criteria.
•	Verify circulating and conditioning criteria before cementing.  Refer to liner program and Cementing Liner Checklist.
•	Verify the proper slips for the weight of the liner string. 
•	Use dope from a newly opened pail.  Dope should be the correct kind and well mixed to distribute the solid particles evenly.
•	Thread-Lok the float equipment, especially if the liner is to be drilled out, as per the liner program.
•	Ensure that all joints in the casing-liner overlap are centralized. When there are close clearances, minimum-clearance centralizers should be used.
•	When using lift plugs, be sure that they are securely made up in the box end of the joint before picking the joint up out of the "V" door.
•	Have oversized wiper rubber installed above diverter bag to prevent objects from falling down hole.
•	If liner is slotted, ensure that the crew understands how to drop the liner in the hole in the event of well control procedures.
•	Ensure that on flush-joint pipe, a safety clamp is installed before disconnecting the elevators and removing the lift plug.
•	Make up the liner hanger and visually inspect it to ensure that the slips will operate and that there is no damage to the slips, the J-slot, or other components. A prior check of the OD's on the hanger should indicate the need for special-size tong heads or rotary slips to handle the liner hanger. 
•	Check float operation on initial insertion into the hole.
•	Ensure that any singles which must be picked up to allow tagging bottom on an even stand should be picked up, and stood back if possible, before the liner is run into open hole.
•	Verify that after all of the liner assembly has been picked up, it is possible to circulate through the entire assembly before proceeding to go in the hole.
•	Verify the correct amount of casing run by counting liner left on the deck.
•	Check of the actual liner weight after picking up the first stand of drillpipe.  Be sure that the liner is completely full of mud and take into account the weight of the blocks and the stand of drill pipe.
•	Under no circumstance shall rotation be allowed to the running string; use a back-up tong for connection make-up, and lock the rotary table (mechanical type).
•	Verify with liner program or perform surge/swab calculations, for maximum running speed to avoid unnecessary surges on the formation.
•	Ensure that the bottom is tagged with the cement head high enough above the rotary to allow for the activation of the liner slips.
•	Before the liner is run into the open hole, a cementing standpipe and the cement lines should be rigged up with long enough bails to allow maximum pipe movement or washing down.
•	Ensure that reciprocation of the liner begins immediately. While reciprocating to break the mud gel strength, break circulation as slowly as possible (1/4 to 1/2 bpm). The speed of reciprocation should not exceed that of the recommended lowering speed.
•	Ensure that the place where bottom is to be tagged is marked on the drill pipe.
•	Ensure drill string is spaced out to put the surface cementing equipment as close as possible to the rotary table when shoe is on bottom (keep in mind additional slack off weight required). Drift all pups and singles picked up from pipe deck.
•	Record the exact liner and DP string weights including drag (hook load down and up) to calculate the exact neutral point for the setting tool release (10-15t bearing).
•	Ensure that prior to setting hanger that all drill pipe on surface is counted and matches with correct number from original count.
•	Ensure that before running tool is released, the hanger has had set down weight applied that is equal to or greater than weights applied during the cementing, rotating or packer setting process.
•	Ensure that the Drilling Supervisor or Toolpusher supervise the running of the liner into the open hole.
•	Gradually increase the circulating rate to the maximum rate to be used, as per the liner procedure.

2.23	LIQUID ADDITIVES SYSTEM CHECKLIST

2.23.1	GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS
•	Verify which type of LAS is being used and ensure that the service hand is experienced with its operation.
•	Verify spill containment plans for either system used.
•	Ensure that the service company has proper documentation (i.e., last maintenance records, date of last operation, green tag, stem 1, operations manual, troubleshooting guides, equipment risk assessment etc.) for the equipment on board.
•	Check for visual aspect of the equipment (i.e., general painting condition, cleaning status inside the tanks, valves, connections and hoses).

2.23.2	GRAVITY FEED SYSTEM - OPERATING CHECKLIST
•	Check that the fill process in each additive dose tank has been initialized.
•	Verify that the visual sight gauges indicate the correct volume of additive.
•	Verify that all additive dose tanks empty completely and refill to correct level. Ensure additives mix and blend thoroughly.
•	Repeat additive dump process to verify that additive will flow into the opposite 10 bbl compartment of the mix water tank.
•	Drain system and connect additive supply to the additive dose tanks prior to beginning the job. Verify that the correct additive supply lines are connected to the dose tanks set up for that additive.
•	Storage tanks should be weatherized (heat tracing, etc.) as required w/ large enough lines to assure viscous additives maintain flow during job.

2.23.3	ELECTRONIC SYSTEM - OPERATING CHECKLIST
•	Load additive data required for job.
•	Verify that all additive pumps activated can pump fluid.
•	Visually check that pumps shutoff in correct sequence.
•	Visually check for loss of fluid supply to the pump.
•	Check that all fluid lines from the additive supply to the pump modules are connected properly with no obvious restrictions.
•	Storage tanks should be weatherized (heat tracing, etc.) as required w/ large enough lines to assure viscous additives maintain flow during job.  Ensure gelation does not occur in liquid additive tank walls.
•	Contingency plan should be in place in the event that the LAS electronic system presents any failure.
•	Verify that the visual sight gauges indicate the correct volume of additive 

2.24	LOGGING CHECKLIST
•	Confirm that all the programmed logging requirements are understood and feasible according to current well conditions.
•	Ensure there are no bad wrinkles in screen.
•	A flow line mud sample for the electric logging test must be caught and analyzed just prior to pulling out to log.
•	Ensure proper connection (flanges, x-overs etc.) from rig BOP to wireline riser/lubricator is on location prior to POOH to log.
•	Prior to the logging job have a ToolBox meeting with all relevant personnel to review the planned operations and potential risks.
•	After pulling out of the hole and prior to rigging up loggers, ensure that:
1.	No Crane operations are scheduled which would pass over the wireline while rigged up.
2.	The hole is neither flowing nor losing mud.
3.	The inside blowout preventer and safety valve is readily available.
4.	All crossover subs needed for a fast trip in the hole are conveniently placed.
5.	The BOP has been function-tested.
6.	A proper sized bit is made up on the bottom stand of drill collars.
7.	A rig crew member (normally the driller) is assigned to monitor the trip tank fluid level during the logging operation.
•	Ensure shooting nipple is flange adaptor with ACME o-ring union box.
•	Ensure that a wireline lubricator is installed on a shooting nipple for each logging run, especially if H2S is expected, history of loss circulation, or in a HPHT environment.  The program should include this requirement.
•	Ensure all equipment has proper pressure rating and is accompanied by proper certification.
•	Ensure that all pressure equipment is tested to the maximum anticipated surface pressure + 20% and the tests are recorded on a pressure chart.
•	Ensure grease injectors are utilized on all gas wells.
•	Ensure sufficient lubricator and riser to cover entire tool string is rigged up.
•	Welding machines to be switched off during specific logs
•	Good communications between rig personnel and wireline operator.  On arrival on location the wireline company should be comprehensively briefed by the WSS on the logging program and any requirements for mufax and field prints. The wireline company should also be provided with all relevant data required for the log headings (in writing).
•	Ensure that all equipment and personnel required to carry out logging program are on site and operational.
•	Ensure crews are reminded of possibilities of trapped pressure in tool strings and how to handle.
•	Ensure cable tension load is not transmitted to lubricator.
•	Ensure all fishing/strip over tools are on location prior to start of logging.
•	Ensure proper hole finder is installed on logging tools.
•	Make sure that the well is drilled deep enough so that the top-most sensor in the logging string can reach the bottom-most zone of interest. For this the logging company should provide the measured distance from the sensors to the bottom of the tool string.
•	The Logging Engineer shall be provided with mud, mud-cake immediately prior to logging, in order to obtain resistivity measurements. The mud sample shall always be taken from the flow line. At this time, circulatory temperatures shall be measured in the flow line.
•	There should be restricted access to the cantilever and drill floor whilst making up logging tools to limit exposure to radiation.
•	All shackles and pins must be safety wired.
•	Ensure that the upper sheave hanger (spade) is correctly fitted into the elevators, and that the elevators are secured closed
•	Ensure the brake is chained down once the upper sheave is raised to its final position, and the block line flagged
•	The Wireline Operator will immediately inform the Driller if the tool becomes stuck
•	PA announcement to be made and effected areas cleared prior to commencing to over pull on a stuck tool
•	Secure the upper wireline sheave into the elevators using the "teardrop" bar and install a proper safety sling.
•	No rig chains are to be used as the anchor chain, only chains supplied by the logging company.  Ensure that chains have proper certification.
•	Ensure that the well is covered during all lifting of equipment. Also that the vertical make-up plate used for rigging long logging tool strings is in good condition, and adequately supported in the rotary table.
•	A reminder notice shall be placed on the BOP controls warning that wireline operations are taking place, to avoid accidental closure of the blind rams on logging tools or cable.
•	Check the cable armor regularly for unraveling of armor strands. This applies particularly to operations through a stuffing box
•	All personnel on the wellsite shall defer to the judgment of the contractor's "Logging Engineer", who is a designated "Competent Person" in matters related to operations with sources of Ionizing Radiation.
•	Ensure that the mud level in the trip tank is carefully noted and recorded before any logging tool is run in the hole. By noting the displacement with the tool in the hole and calculating the line displacement it is possible to monitor the status of the hole
•	During the open hole logging operations, ensure that the fluid level in the hole is monitored continuously, by the Driller and Mudloggers, on the trip tank with the trip tank pump running.  
•	Ensure that before running any wireline tool in the hole, each section of the tool must be measured and calipered. It is recommended to draw a sketch of the toolstring with OD and length marked beside each component. Do not rely on published technical data sheets for this. Ensure the logging company has a stocked fishing kit on board with suitable overshots and grapples.
•	Install the wireline wiper prior pulling out of the hole.  Remove the wireline wiper when the tool is at surface.
•	Under normal operating conditions all field prints should be made by the logging engineer on the wellsite immediately following logging.
•	The field prints should be checked by the WSS before the logging report form is signed
Refer to: Procedures for Radioactive Sources, and Barriers

2.25	LOSS CIRCULATION GUIDELINES

2.25.1	INDICATOR OF POTENTIAL LOSS CIRCULATION
•	Decrease in rate of flow.
•	Pit level reduction.
•	Reduction in pump pressure, stroke rate increases.

2.25.2	POTENTIAL CAUSES
•	Natural Fractures.
•	Mud weight too high.
•	Solids build-up, causing high ECD.
•	Bridging, packing-off, balled-up drilling assemblies and stabilizers.
•	Closed in kicks with casing strings too shallow.
•	Abnormally low pore pressure gradients
•	Spudding pipe too fast - surge.
•	Breaking circulation infrequently while running pipe.
•	Poor mud rheology.

2.25.3	POINTS TO CONSIDER
•	Recognize initial indicators of loss circulation and take prompt action.
•	Identify the likely cause of the problem as quickly as possible.
•	Identify if the problem is due to formation properties - determine and report the cause and depth of the problem.
•	Identify If the problem is mechanical, i.e. too high mud weight, high ECD, take and/or propose corrective action.
•	If possible, estimate pressure of any known "thief" or pressure-depleted zones from available information obtained by "listening to the hole".

2.25.4	LOSS PREVENTION MEASURES
•	Prior to drilling into the section, ensure that a Toolbox meeting is held with the relevant personnel to review the following issues as it could apply to the section.
•	Ensure annulus is kept full.
•	Watch ECD's and control mud weight (Refer to ECD Guidelines).
•	Plan for preventive measures upon reviewing drilling program and offset well data, and have resources on board before starting to drill the section.
•	Record and report the amount of losses, if losses are detected.
•	Flow check and keep trend sheets on well flow to distinguish "ballooning" from well control situations.
•	Maintain "trends of the hole" fill-up pressures. If the pump pressure required to fill the hole continues to increase, this is usually a good indicator of solids loading.
•	To prevent losses, align ROP with good hole cleaning practices to minimize unnecessarily high ECD. Note the amount and structure of solids coming over the shakers.
•	When below a casing shoe, the safest place to regain control & returns is on bottom. 
•	Check Cutting Carrying Index (CCI) and maintain laminar flow rates.
•	Avoid excessive mud weight, monitor rheology.
•	Ascertain well bore stability before pulling into casing shoe.

2.25.5	SPECIFIC CONSIDERATIONS TO CURE LOSSES
•	As soon as losses are detected, reduce pump rate and/or consider shutting down the pumps.
•	Keep the hole full with drilling fluid
•	If sudden losses are experienced, fill the annulus with a compatible fluid, such as base oil, to lower the hydrostatic pressure.  KEEP IN MIND THE REQUIRED HYDROSTATIC PRESSURE TO BALANCE PORE PRESSURE.
•	Record circulating mud weight at regular intervals.
•	Pull above the "thief" zone and attempt to regain circulation.
•	Attempt to determine the type of loss circulation, i.e. seeping to massive, localized to large area, to determine suitable loss circulation material.
1.	Application of limited amounts of loss circulation material (LCM) can help; however take care not to inordinately increase solids while bypassing the shakers.
2.	Graded particles work best in "sucrosic" porosity, but poorly in fractured or cavernous porosity.
3.	Fibrous material works best in "fractured" porosity - blocking "tortuous" paths. If porosity is "sucrosic" and "fractured", a combination is often necessary.
4.	Flake material, for example, "mica" is often effective in high mud weights with "sucrosic" porosity.
5.	LCM pills are generally preferable over attempting to maintain LCM in the mud system.  Bypassing the shakers has a self-defeating effect of increasing ECD by loading the system with solids.
6.	For uncontrollable "cavernous" or similar losses, consider "dry" drilling, and letting the cuttings eventually "bridge" or cure the losses or suspend operations.

2.27	PRESSURE TESTING CHECKLIST

2.27.1	PRESSURE TESTING OPERATIONS
•	Identify the various required pressure testing operations for the operations, i.e.:
1.	BOP pressure testing.
2.	Sub-sea control lines.  Refer to Drilling Contractor requirements.
3.	Casing / liners.  Refer to the casing program for requirements.
4.	Cementing lines.  Refer to the cementing program for requirements.
5.	FIT/FLOT.  Refer to FIT/FLOT Checklist for testing requirements.
6.	Tubing / Completion strings. Refer to completion program for requirements
7.	Risers.  Refer to the drilling program for requirements.
•	Ensure that there are detailed testing instructions for all relevant personnel.
•	Ensure the testing objectives are as per the relevant program.
•	Identify the appropriate pump and fluid for the test, i.e.
1.	High / low pressure
2.	High / low rate
3.	Testing fluid (use water unless good reason not to)
•	Hold a Toolbox meeting with all personnel to discuss the program and safety.
•	Ensure that the test results are documented.
•	Ensure that the Drilling Supervisor or Toolpusher is in constant communication with the personnel performing the pressure test.
•	Ensure that all lines, swings, and connections that are used in the pressure testing are adequately secured in place and rated to equal or exceed the test pressures.
•	Before the test calculate the anticipated volume of fluid required to perform the test and compare this to actual pumped while doing the job.  Terminate pressure test if actual volumes differ greatly from the anticipated volumes.
•	Confirm validity of test by comparing volumes pumped to previous tests.
•	Outline safe/unsafe areas.  Use the PA system to announce the commencement and conclusion of testing.
•	Only personnel authorized will go into the test area to inspect for leaks when the equipment involved is under pressure. Unauthorized personnel are to stay clear of the area until the Drilling Supervisor has contacted the pumping unit operator and all have agreed that all pressure has been released and there is no possibility of pressure being trapped.
•	Pressure is to be released only through the pressure release lines that are vented back into the pump unit tanks. The lines are to be clamped down to direct the flow into unit tanks.
•	All pressure tests should be recorded versus time on a pressure chart recorder or hard copy log
•	Never hammer on any lines, unions or other fittings while under pressure.
•	Employees should never straddle or stand around high pressure lines.

2.28	PRODUCTION FLOW TESTING CHECKLIST

General considerations
•	Ensure that the well testing requirements in the program are understood and achievable.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient deck space and variable load for the production testing equipment.
•	Ensure that the OIM is aware of the testing requirement and deck space is made available, if required.
•	Ensure that all required equipment is on board, inspected for readiness and has all the relevant documents. (Maintenance records, test certifications, etc.)
•	Hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel to review the program and safety issues
•	Confirm the requirement for any heavy lifts
•	Prior to start of the test, ensure to open the intervention tree valves & cycle them with well open & flowing to remove any saltwater which could form hydrates in the tree and / or behind the gate valves.  Refer to: Rig-up Intervention_Tree_&_Riser_Checklist.
•	 Ensure that the testing equipment is properly rigged up and pressure tested.  Refer to: Pressure Testing Checklist.
•	Ensure that the required lines of communication are sorted out with everything through the Driller and Control room.
•	Specify & confirm any limits on maximum flowrates, e.g. temperature limits, etc.
•	If there is any wireline work required ensure that there is contingency for pumping methanol.
•	Before any wireline work ensure that the DHSV is open, i.e. before RIH.
•	Verify the best method of flushing out/cleaning the sand traps, if used.
•	During the flow testing, ensure that the Driller keeps a close eye on DHSV & annular pressures, since they could rise quickly.

2.29	REMEDIAL CEMENTING CHECKLIST
