2.29.1	GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS
•	Confirm that the approved daily orders for this remedial work are understood and obtainable.
•	Verify the correct cement recipe is being used and has been tested. Confirm thickening time sufficient for the job requirements.
•	Review results as soon as available w/ engineering & service company.
•	Ensure cement & water samples being used for the testing are for the cement on board. 
•	Verify that standback area boards are in good condition to prevent debris from entering the drill pipe.
•	Verify rig equipment adequate for the pumping rate & pressure rating.
•	Verify sufficient rig handling capacities, slings, sufficient chicksans & lines that are fit for purpose.
•	Confirm pipe handling and efficiencies.
•	Confirm cementing lines secured & tested.
•	Confirm correct stinger, cementing head & plugs, equipment for running retainer, connections for cementing kelly, top drive side swivel, or power swivel are ready.
•	Check back-up equipment (if/when available) for bulk air compressor, mix water lines/pumps, etc.
•	Ensure provisions have been made to handle displaced fluids during the job.
•	Verify sufficient quantities of cement & additives are on board for the job.
•	Verify cement location i.e. silos, tanks, multiple tanks and delivery procedures.
•	Review downhole clearances, hole geometry and mud properties to mitigate any potential problems.
•	Ensure contingency action for cement setting prematurely, perforations are plugged, equipment fails, unexpected pressure is encountered, etc.
•	Prior to the job, determine if additional personnel are needed, such as toolhands, field engineer, cementing supervisor, helpers, casing crews, mud engineer, etc.
•	Evaluate economics for personnel & equipment standby costs verses rig time.
•	Check and understand the type of liquid additive system.  (Refer to: LAS Checklist.)

2.29.2	TUBULARS
•	Ensure swab/surge calculations have been performed and reviewed with rig personnel. Know fracture gradient limitations.
•	Confirm and communicate the pumping pressure limitations due to fracture gradients with all relevant personnel.
•	Clean, tally, drift and inspect tubulars. Verify tubular weight per foot and actual diameters and length for displacement and capacity volume calculations.
•	Ensure to drift the work string and that the drift is hollow and has appropriate length of steel line (approx. 100 ft).
•	Use stingers when laying balanced plugs.  The length of the stinger should exceed the calculated height of the cement plug.
•	Ensure that there is pipe movement by rotating whenever possible. Consider the following;
1.	If pipe movement is not possible, consideration should be given to using mechanical aids (turbulators, etc.)
2.	Begin pipe movement before or during circulation prior to cementing if equipment will allow and continue throughout cementing.
•	Rotation procedures:
1.	Review rotational procedures and ensure requirements are meet.
2.	If possible, begin rotation immediately after workstring is on depth, rotating as per rotational procedures requirements, or 3 to 10 RPM's
3.	String weight will change with changes in fluid density pumped, therefore, it is important not to confuse weight increase with pipe sticking.
4.	Continue pipe movement until plug is in place.

2.29.3	JOB EXECUTION
•	Ensure to perform a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel to discuss the program and the safety issues.
•	Visually inspect all tanks to be used for cleanliness.
•	Confirm correct volumes & sufficient quantities (including contingency) of mix water additives, cement, pit volume, plugs, spacers and displacement fluids.
•	Recheck volume & temperature calculations for the job. It is very important to have accurate displacement calculations.
•	Ensure that the program volumes been adjusted based on anticipated hole volumes, from caliper or field data. 
•	Ensure that the Drilling Supervisor & Cementer independently prepare & compare mixing & displacement calculations.
•	Verify the following squeeze calculations:
1.	Weight of all tubulars before, during and after cementing
2.	Circulation volume.
3.	Volume and density of preflush.
4.	Reduction of hydrostatic head due to preflush. Construct a pumping schedule and ensure overbalance is maintained as appropriate.
5.	Cement slurry volume including excess.
6.	Hydrostatic pressure when cement is fully displaced. Check against expected frac pressure of formation.
7.	Mix water, cement, & additives requirements for all slurries.
8.	Capacity of cement lines & displacement volumes
9.	Theoretical pumping pressures versus slurry & displacement volumes.
10.	Maximum possible returns, ignoring circulating losses, and the maximum displacement volume required to catch up with the "U" tubed cement.
11.	Slurry mixing time.
12.	Displacement time.
13.	Total job time compared to thickening time
14.	Theoretical static differential pressure.
15.	Casing test pressure.
16.	Anticipated TOC.
•	Ensure good communications between the rig floor and the cementing unit.
•	Collect samples of cement, blend, and mixed water.
•	Injection rates and pressures should be determined prior to start of squeeze job.
•	Verify any staging requirements.
•	Become familiar isolation tools prior to running, i.e. permanent or retrievable tools.
•	Beware of casing collapse when using isolation tools on large casing.
•	If a Liquid Additive System is used, identify as gravity feed or electronic control. Guides for checking the LAS are detailed in the ROPE checklist found here: LAS Checklist.
•	Maintain an operations record log of pertinent job information for company reporting (i.e., DIMS).
•	Batch mix, if possible. Avoid using jet mixers. Alternatively, use recirculating type mixers or equivalent continuous mix systems with auto density control
•	Ensure to mix with clean water with salinity and temperature within design parameters.
•	Liquid additives may be mixed in the cement displacement tanks if pre-job mixing is not possible. Dry additives must be premixed prior to the job.
•	Rotation of the drill pipe will improve the chances of obtaining an uncontaminated plug.  Stop the movement when spacer and cement enter annulus.
•	Check the bulk delivery system to ensure sufficient feed rate.  Monitor the ventline to ensure the cement is not being discharged through it.
•	Check to ventilation system since dryness of supply air is an important factor.
•	During the job check the volumes pumped using material balance computations against the barrel counters on the cementing unit.
•	Do not pull the bulk tank(s) dry.
•	Upon conclusion verify cement & mix water volumes using material balance to confirm cement was pumped as designed.
•	Reversing out must be decided in advance since thickening time may need to be increased if circulating out conventionally.
•	Follow the design procedure for waiting on cement. Complete all company & regulatory reporting, immediately after the job.
Note: A high final squeeze pressure does not necessarily indicate a successful squeeze.

2.30	RIG MOVE (JACK-UP & SEMI) CHECKLIST

2.30.1	PRE MOVE FOR SEMI-SUBS & JACK-UP RIGS
•	Conduct a pre-move meeting in town with all concerned parties.
•	Conduct a pre-move meeting at the rig site with all the major players and supervisors.
•	This checklist is to ensure that the rig is ready to move on, spud and drill expeditiously and safely as possible, with the responsibility for the move to be normally under the supervision of the Drilling Contractor Towmaster, OIM, Rig Positioning Personnel and Contractor Marine Personnel.
•	Ensure that mooring analysis is completed and approved.  Provide copy to rig.
•	Ensure that riser analysis is completed and approved.  Provide copy to rig.
•	Check that all required personnel are on board, i.e. Contractor Towmasters, Rig Positioning Personnel & Move Personnel (Insurance Surveyors, etc.)
•	Review Marine Procedure for rig move, noting any special requirements.
•	Ensure that all equipment is secured on rig prior to move.  Inspect hold down points
•	If rig move is relatively short, ensure that relevant service company representatives and equipment are on board to check and prepare their equipment for spudding or kept available for the 1st transport to the rig. Note restrictions on Personnel on Board.
•	Obtain weather forecasts, tide charts, etc from a certified meteorological and oceanography consulting firm.  These shall be provided daily during rig move and operations.
•	Verify that the Toolpusher has prepared the rig for the move.  Review his checklists.
•	Verify equipment requirements for mooring at the new location.  Ensure all mooring equipment aboard anchor handlers.
•	Inspect tow bridle for broken wires.
•	Ensure that the hand held radio battery is charged. Test the hand held radio by calling the tow boat captain immediately prior to the commencement of operations. Also, ensure that the hand held radio can communicate with the VHF radio in the rig's control room. Have one person standing by at the VHF radio throughout the operation, in case there is a failure of the hand held radio. The VHF operator will relay messages to the tow boat captain, if required
•	Check operation of the rig's tow bridle winch.  Ensure that the air supply is turned on and that the brake is able to hold the weight of the rig's tow bridle.  Check the condition of the brake pads
•	Ensure that all tanks are sounded, volumes agreed upon and recorded prior to accepting rig for move.
•	Prior to move, calculate the deck loads, ensuring that items are placed in locations conducive to good stability
•	For a Semi-sub, verify with the Barge Master the variable deck load available for equipment to spud.
•	Ensure that Pre Move Toolbox meeting is conducted with all relevant personnel.
•	Conduct an inspection of all rig equipment as per Drilling Contractor's guidelines.
•	Confirm that legs and / or derrick will pass under any obstructions en route.
•	Check hull and water depth for clearance en route.
•	Confirm that a recent seabed survey has been prepared and that the paperwork is on board.
•	Review the bottom survey and side scan sonar for obstructions (such as pipelines, wellheads, junk) along route & at new location.
•	Verify that all flowlines, wellheads or junk near the new location are identified.
•	Confirm in possession of proper export / import documents, if leaving the country.
•	Verify with the Toolpusher if any work, maintenance or repairs are required and could be done during the rig move
•	Verify requirement for additional personnel to improve "flat time" operations during rig up to spud.
•	Confirm availability of tow bridle with spare personnel baskets (2 ea.).
•	Ensure that a seabed clearance report is done.
•	If rig is coming on contract, ensure that a handover sheet is filled out at tight tow. Note: Tight Tow is where the new AFE will be in effect, start new IADC and DDR from this time.
•	Ensure that an adequate amount of AHV's are ordered and they comply with the Contractor's requirements for towing / anchor handling (i.e. horsepower, bollard pull, etc.)
•	Verify a full safety and operational inspection, once rigged up on location, of all rig equipment as per the Drilling Contractor's and service companies guidelines.
•	Ensure that the spud equipment has been ordered and the PSV has been organized to be on the new location at the same time as the rig arrives.

2.30.2	JACK-UP MOVE REQUIREMENTS
•	If the location is for a Jack-up rig, ensure that the ROV is ready to survey the location where the spud cans will be, usually from the stand off position if going onto a platform or existing sub-sea well.
•	If the move is to or from a producing platform, ensure to understand and comply with the HAZID & HAZOP programs for simultaneous operations.
•	If drilling from a platform, prior to pulling near the platform confirm the readiness to receive the rig.  Hold a Pre Job meeting with the OIM of the production platform to organize the move onto the platform.
•	Confirm weather window for moving onto platform and for heavy lifts.

2.30.3	PREPARATION WHILE UNDER WAY
•	Prepare for spud by reviewing the following with the Rig Team:
1.	Review drilling program while preparing equipment list requirements.
2.	Check welding equipment.
3.	Pre job safety meeting to discuss spudding or re-entering requirements.
4.	Test choke manifold, BOP's, top drive valves, safety valves, standpipe manifold.
5.	Test mud cleaning equipment and correct shaker screens.
6.	Test accumulator (check for size of accumulator).
7.	Test cement unit & lines (cement delivery & discharge).
8.	Review the Drilling Contractor safety program.
9.	Check the Drilling Contractor equipment status against the drilling contract and the drilling equipment required to perform the required operations.
10.	Confirm bottom survey and side scan sonar for obstructions (such as pipelines, wellheads, junk) along route & at new location.
11.	Move GRA to spider deck area
12.	Paint black rings inside GRA funnel 1 foot wide spaced 1 t. apart. In addition, paint letters indicating forward, aft, port, starboard and the well name and number.
13.	Install beacon-mounting frame. Insure beacon is not under shadow of funnel and mounted on stbd, aft side. 
14.	Install slope indicators one forward and one starboard. Ensure that slope indicators are visible with Marcon and clump weight T.V. and are below funnel top.
15.	Install slings on GRA and attach to starboard bridge crane side for moving to spider beams when needed.
16.	Cut 6-4" x 8" square holes in bottom of GRA funnel to allow drainage of cuttings and cement from around 30" housing.
17.	Clean and inspection all 30" csg. box and pin ends.  Also clean and inspection 30" wellhead housing. Ensure adequate "O" rings are on hand
18.	Strap and number all 30" csg.  Install "O" rings on casing
19.	Paint 1 ft wide, 5 ft apart yellow rings on shoe jt. and wellhead housing. Number marks 1 to 30 on shoe, and 20 to 0 on the wellhead housing
20.	Ensure all TV equipment is functioning properly (Marcon, clump wt., breakaway).
21.	Ensure adequate cable is installed on TV winches and guideline system for expected water depth
22.	Check out clump wt. to ensure it is in good shape. Grease sheaves
23.	Check out TV recorder and play back system
24.	Pick up 9 1/2" dc and 8" dc. Clean and inspect box and pins
25.	Calibrate torque indicators on drill floor.
26.	Bring all needed crossovers to drill floor that will be needed for spud assembly.
27.	Check 36" hole opener. Record jet size and number; inspect cutting rollers if fitted with grease fittings grease rollers. Draw picture of hole opener and put in tool book.
28.	Have solid float c/w new seals ready that will fit 36" spud assembly.
29.	Check out all survey equipment (Totco, Sperry Sun).
30.	Check out survey wireline recovery system and sand line.
31.	If mud system is to be used, install surface sensors and other related equipment.
32.	Clean all mud tanks. Install shaker screens.
33.	Change mud pumps to 7" liners. Check valves and all rubber goods in pumps.  Pressure test mud mixing system to 100 psi.
34.	Mix spud.
35.	Mix 400 bbls heavy weight kill mud.
36.	Inspect all drill floor equipment.
37.	Check out all well control equipment; Kelly Omsco valves. Gray valve, stabbing valve, dart sub and dart, short x/o.
•	Review with Rig Team for any other work that can be done while underway, equipment inspections, repairs, etc
•	Prepare a plan with the Drilling Contractor personnel to move on, rig-up and spud in the most efficient and safest manner possible.
•	Ensure that all required reference material is available for the upcoming operations, such as manuals and references, ROPE Checklists, relevant PC files, and any other reference material that may be required during the operation.  Refer to: Project Bridging Document and Information to be Kept on Location
2.31	RIG-UP INTERVENTION TREE & RISER CHECKLIST (PLATFORM OPERATIONS)

2.31.1	GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS
•	Confirm that all equipment delivered to the dock is as required for the job; to include all required technical documentation.
•	Ensure that all inspection and service reports are up to date and correct for the intervention tree.
•	Ensure that the intervention program is understood and that the equipment is fit for purpose, as outlined in the program.
•	Confirm that scaffolding work is needed prior to running intervention tree.
•	Prior to beginning to rig-up the equipment, ensure that a Toolbox meeting is conducted with all relevant personnel.
•	Confirm any heavy lifts and compliance.  Inform OIM of timing of lifts.
•	Ensure that all parties understand required lines of communications.
•	Check that 1/2" NPT plugs and that connections are made up properly.
•	Ensure that hydraulic hoses to be used to Intervention tree run through deck hatch in a safe location.
•	Ensure that a schematic of the rig up is made and is continually updated.
•	Ensure that the all valves on intervention tree are drifted. 
•	Ensure that the intervention tree been greased prior to installation.
•	Ensure that the bore of the intervention tree been checked to make sure there's no grease build-up.

2.31.2	SPECIAL INSTRUCTIONS
•	Confirm hanging weight of each intervention tree component with crane weight indicator and record on DDR.

2.32	RIG-UP SURFACE TEST TREE (STT) CHECKLIST

2.32.1	GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS
•	Confirm that all equipment delivered to the dock is as required for the job, to include all required technical  / temporary documentation.
•	Ensure that all inspection and service reports are up to date and correct for the Surface Test Tree (STT).	
•	Ensure that the program is understood and that the equipment is fit for purpose, as outlined in the operational program.
•	Prior to beginning to rig-up the equipment, ensure that a Toolbox meeting is conducted with all relevant personnel.
•	Confirm any heavy lifts are in compliance. Inform OIM of timing of lifts.
•	Ensure that crossovers are available above STT for wireline / electric line rig-up if required and drifted to largest tool size to be used.
•	Ensure that required lines of communications are followed and posted. All communications must pass through the Driller as required. 
•	Ensure that no valves on STT or Rig floor are operated without the Driller's knowledge.
•	Ensure that STT hoses are sufficient length to operator-required equipment from a safe distance from rotary table.
•	Confirm that scaffolding work is considered prior to rigging up STT.
•	Ensure that the driller makes a schematic of the rig up and that it is continually updated.
•	Ensure that the STT been pressure tested & greased prior to installation. 
•	Functions test the ESD actuator on the flow wing valve. The ESD is a fail-safe valve.
•	Ensure that the bore of the STT been checked to make sure there's no grease build-up.
•	Ensure that the valves are closed and opened a number of times, then drifted a number of times.  With master and swab valves open, drift the flow head to its maximum diameter to accommodate any wireline or coiled tubing tools to be run.

2.32.2	SPECIAL INSTRUCTIONS
Ensure all personnel on rig floor, around flowlines, chiksans and all pressured piping stand clear when pressure testing and flowing operations take place.

2.33	RUN RISER CHECKLIST

Preparation to Run Riser:
•	Refer to the drilling program and Drilling Contractor documentation for any relevant instructions and comments.
•	Check for following materials:
1.	BOP stack
2.	LMRP
3.	Verify and witness stump test of B.O.P. stack to full working pressure.
4.	Verify that all annular elements and ram rubbers are in good condition
5.	Stainless steel VX gasket 
6.	BOP position beacon
7.	Riser
8.	Pod hose clamps
9.	Telescopic joint
10.	SDL ring
11.	SSTV
12.	Diverter control block O-rings
13.	Choke, kill and booster line gooseneck pins
14.	Telescopic joint water spray ring
15.	Telescopic joint packer hose adapter elbows
16.	Diverter ball joint grease line
17.	BOP transporter with bridge
18.	Riser spider
19.	500 ton side-door elevators
20.	500 ton bales
21.	Riser handling tools
22.	Choke and kill line test cap
23.	BOP test pump skid with chart recorder and chart
24.	High pressure test hose assembly
25.	Diverter handling tool
26.	Moonpool 60 ton trolley
27.	Riser handing slings and tag lines
28.	1" impact wrench with 1-1/8" socket
29.	Torque wrench with 1-1/8" socket
30.	Riser bolt make up gauge
31.	Chain tongs
32.	Flashlight
33.	Walkie -Talkies
34.	7/8", 11/16", 3/4" & 1/2" Allen keys
35.	12" Adjustable wrench
36.	Pod hose clamp wrenches
37.	Lock wires
38.	Sledgehammer
39.	Grease
40.	Dope Brush
41.	Soft line
42.	Rags
43.	Riding belts
44.	Inertia reels
45.	Safety harnesses
46.	Work vests
•	Ensure that all equipment is the correct size, in good working order and has the required documentation for inspection and operation.
•	Ensure that a Toolbox meeting is held with the relevant personnel, discussing the planned operation and highlighting the areas that required special attention, especially for a safe operation.
•	Obtain a work permit from the O.I.M. for running the BOP stack and riser including pressure testing the choke and kill lines.
•	Attempt to pressure test Choke and kill manifold and mud manifold prior to running stack and riser.
•	Prepare equipment and labor for heavy lifts under the rig floor in advance of operation as much as possible.
•	Ensure that all tong make up/breakout, snub lines, shackles, slips, elevators and other handling equipment are in good shape and rigged up for tongs to be used.  Check drill floor tuggers.
•	Function spider beams.
•	Service and function all sub sea running tools.
•	Inspect all sheaves in the derrick and riser tensioners.
•	Ensure there are enough ton-miles remaining to run BOP stack and riser prior to slipping and cutting drill line.
•	Install inertia reels in moon pool area as required.
•	Check that the BOP stack is clean and free of all tools.
•	Check that the riser adapter pin, choke, kill and booster line stabs are clean and greased.
•	Check that the riser adapter pin O-ring is in good condition.
•	Check that the riser angle-indicating beacon is securely fastened and operational.
•	Check that the transporter is jacked down as far as possible on the lower dogs.
•	Check that the transporter rails are clean and free of debris.
•	Check that the bridge is removed from the transporter.
•	At the accumulator unit hydraulic control manifold verify that all of the control valves and regulators are in the proper positions
•	At the diverter substructure panel verify that all of the control valves and regulators are in the proper positions.
•	At the active (on-line) pod hose reel, verify that all of the control valves have the handles removed and are in the proper positions
•	Check that the all riser pins and choke, kill booster line male stabs are clean and greased while running riser.
•	Check that all riser pin O-rings are in good condition.
•	Check weather and sea conditions prior to running BOP and riser.
•	Move rig 30 m (100 ft) off location while running the BOP stack and riser unless sufficient water dept and current to no pose hazard to wellhead.
•	Pressure test choke and kill lines every 3-5 joints and prior to slip joint installation.
•	After landing check well connector lock indicator with TV camera.
•	Take over pull test on wellhead connector.
•	Check slope indicator. Record readings.
•	Check over pull on diverter housing after installation.
•	Pressure test wellhead connector against shear rams.

2.34	SLICKLINE INTERVENTION CHECKLIST

2.34.1	PRE-JOB ISSUES
•	Ensure that there is an approved program and its contents are understood.
•	Ensure that there is an approved AFE with costs of job cost control.
•	Verify that all regulatory and government permits are issued and on board.
•	Verify that the third parties services have been notified.
•	Verify any heavy lifts
•	Verify that the OIM and relevant production personnel have been notified to shut in the well and/or facilities. (Permit to Work process)
•	Verify the requirement for support vessels, i.e. barges, supply boats, crew boats, tugs, helicopters.
•	Hold a Toolbox meeting to discuss the operation and safety issues with the relevant personnel.
•	Ensure that the program has been reviewed with the contractor.
•	Ensure a drawing of rig up and lubricator-testing procedures is included in program.
•	Verify the wind direction and how gas will be vented during flow back/bleed-off.
•	Ensure that the contractor personnel have the required experience and training of the operation of their equipment.
•	Ensure that all personnel have the required training certificates (well control, first aid, offshore certification, etc)
•	Verify that the Xmas tree (master valves, swab valve) was inspected and pressure tested prior to arrival.
•	Verify that the master valves hold for the rig up.
•	Confirm the connection on the Xmas tree where the SL unit will rig up.
•	Ensure that the weather forecast prior to rig up is favorable.
•	Verify if there is H2S or CO2 in the formation fluids.
•	Verify the existence or anticipation of any scale, paraffin, asphaltenes, hydrates, or fish in the well bore.
•	Ensure that there is a well history available showing past work and current down-hole status of the well.
•	Verify the pressure is expected at surface and at bottom hole.
•	Verify the rig-up required and confirm whether there is any work in open hole.
•	Confirm the weight and dimensions of the slickline unit and suitable space for its placement.
•	Verify if a centralizer is needed in tool string.
•	Ensure that there is radio communications between the wireline unit and rig floor.
•	Review the program and compare it to the equipment list to ensure that all required equipment is on board prior to start of the job, including the back-ups.
•	Ensure that fishing necks are known and fishing tools are available on location prior to running any tool in hole.
•	Ensure that all of the components of the BOP/lubricator system are on board.  Refer to the equipment list.  Verify the pressure rating of each component and confirm that it has a compatible flange for the wellhead.  Ensure all is pressure tested and recorded prior to use.
•	Check the condition of all the slickline equipment, including the back-up equipment. An inspection of all equipment should be done prior to delivery of equipment to location.
•	Ensure that a gauge to monitor pressure is installed.
•	Ensure a method to bleed of trapped pressure is rigged up.
•	Ensure slickline unit is capable 0f monitoring depth, tension and speed.
•	Know the maximum tension that can be applied to the diameter of slickline in use.
•	Ensure that a go-devil is available if needed.  Do not rely on pulling out of the socket.
•	Ensure that the slickline unit has a braking system with a minimum capacity equal to the cable breaking strength.
•	Ensure that slickline unit has a drive system capable of holding load in neutral.
•	Ensure riser/lubricator is secured for lateral support.
•	Ensure sufficient illumination is available in the work area.
•	Ensure that slickline working area is secure.

2.34.2	SLICK LINE OPERATION
•	Verify the functions that will be done with the slickline to ensure that the program is suitable and that the equipment is fit for purpose.
•	Ensure to hold a Toolbox meeting prior to every slickline run into the well to discuss the previous operation, the planned operation and the relevant safety issues.

2.35	STUCK PIPE AVOIDANCE GUIDELINES

Best reference is Drillers Stuck Pipe Handbook

2.35.1	COMMON MECHANISMS
•	Differential Sticking - Most Common
•	Key Seating
•	Pack-Off / Bridging
•	Reactive Formations (Swelling Shale)

2.35.2	OTHER CAUSES
•	Massive Coal Sections
•	Under Gauge Hole
•	Wellbore geometry, hole restriction in high permeable sections with high fluid loss
•	Collapsed Casing
•	Cement Blocks
•	Junk
•	Green Cement
•	Hole caving, due to tectonics, faulted areas, high angle holes
•	Cuttings beds or build-up, especially in high angle holes
•	Salt, causing plastic flow	

2.35.3	STUCK PIPE IS PREVENTABLE IF
•	All personnel anticipated "trouble" spots with contingency plans for appropriate action.
•	There is effective communication maintained with all team members on the causes of stuck pipe.
•	Everyone is "listening to the hole", for unexpected torque, drag, weight, pressure, geometry or volume of shaker cuttings, trends in mud properties or drilling parameters.
•	Good mud properties are maintained.
•	Pipe movement is maintained, when possible
•	Adequate hole cleaning is maintained.
•	Appropriate action is taken quickly and more importantly, avoid taking the wrong action.

2.35.4	STUCK PIPE PREVENTION

2.35.4.A	BHA CONSIDERATIONS
1.	BHA design: include spiral drill collars, stabilizers and reamers, as required, to minimize wall contact for differential sticking considering directional requirements
2.	Use adequate drilling jars with sufficient jarring capacity.  Verify their operation and running procedures.
3.	Review BHA design to minimize slick drill collars in favor of spiral weights
4.	Record all BHA ID's, OD's and lengths for preparation of BHA diagram

2.35.4.B	DRILLING CONSIDERATIONS
1.	Verify and maximize hole cleaning with the appropriate hydraulic program.
2.	Maintain the lowest solids content in the mud system as is mechanically and economically practical.
3.	Use muds with thin filter cake and low fluid loss properties.
4.	Minimize mud weights while drilling considering well control and hole collapse criteria.
5.	Record and communicate string dynamics and neutral weight pick-up with the Driller.
6.	Make records of torque/drag envelopes and trends from offsets and current hole section.
7.	Confirm procedural and operational aspects for maintaining pipe movement are practically implemented.

2.35.4.C	TRIPPING CONSIDERATIONS
1.	Plan theTrip. Refer to the Tripping Checklist.
2.	Identify and communicate trip trouble spots prior to trip.
3.	Record trip fill volume changes and hole integrity
4.	Conduct Toolbox meeting to ensure rig crew awareness and compliance with trip and well control requirements.
5.	Communicate overall drag limits, reaming/backreaming instructions, pumping and hole cleaning requirements
6.	When the hole is tight on trips, rotate and reciprocate with circulation in the appropriate areas. DO NOT pull hard into a sticky zone.

2.35.5	FREEING STUCK PIPE

Utilize SPADE to assist in determining the stuck pipe mechanism.

2.35.5.A	DIFFERENTIALLY STUCK
1.	IMMEDIATELY, attempt to free string by working the drillpipe. Slack off weight, and then apply torque. Release torque, pick up weight and repeat.  Jarring/work pipe down is often more effective than jarring up.  If free, continue rotation and reciprocation.
2.	If possible, reduce the hydrostatic pressure by spotting a lighter fluid in the hole section.  Ensure NOT to induce a kick.	
3.	Spot oil-base mud or oil containing a surfactant around the fish as quickly as possible. Under-displace enough spotting fluid to pump one barrel an hour for at least 24 hours, keep bit open and prevent settling of solids.
4.	Work pipe at surface while the spotting fluid is allowed to soak. It is better to work the pipe down and/or in torque rather than pulling high-tension loads to free it during the soaking operations.
5.	In areas where wall sticking is a problem, or when drilling with large overbalance through depleted production zones, it is good practice to keep surfactant and oil or non-toxic spotting fluid on hand so that it can be spotted without delay if the drill collars should become stuck.
6.	After the soak solution is spotted, work pipe by putting it in compression. Slack off some pipe weight and then apply torque. Release the torque and pick up the pipe weight. Repeat this cycle every five minutes. Displace 1/2 to 1 barrel of soak solution every half hour to keep the collars covered.
7.	Develop alternative recovery methods, based on factors unique to the situation, current in the area and/or based on personal experience.

2.35.5.B	MECHANICALLY STUCK
1.	Regardless of the cause of sticking, spotting oil-base mud, surfactant oil, or non-toxic spotting fluid around the stuck point is a good practice
2.	In carbonates or chalks, spot acid around the BHA. This usually requires significant planning; therefore attempt other practices while organizing.
3.	When pipe becomes stuck in key seats, always jar down.
4.	If pipe remains stuck, a free point should be run and pipe backed off at least 90 ft above the stuck point and above the top of the suspected key seat.	
5.	Consider running a screw-in sub, one drill collar, bumper sub, oil jars, and drill collars to screw back in fish 
6.	Consider next approach would be to wash over the fish. At this time consideration should also be given to sidetracking the well.

2.35.6	DIAGNOSING PROBABLE CAUSES OF STUCK PIPE

Instructions for use:					
Answer all of the shaded areas (1 to 4) by circling the numbers in the row with the correct answer. Add all of the columns; the column with the highest number indicates the most likely diagnosis.

Diagnosing Probable Causes of Stuck Pipe

Action Plans to Consider

Hole Problems				Freeing Possibilities:

ANOTHER CASES:

Stuck while moving up or with string static	Stuck While Moving Down			
1. Apply low pump pressure (200+/_ PSI). Maintaining pressure even if only restricted circulation is possible	1. Apply low pump pressure and maintaining as much as possible.	1. Circulate at maximum allowable
2. DO NOT JAR UP!! APPLY TORQUE. Slack off to MAXIMUM set down weight a sufficient time for hydraulic jar to "trip". (See "jar" specs.)	2. DO NOT JAR DOWN!!. APPLY TORQUE! Apply MAXIMUM overpull to jar. Allow time for jars to trip (See Jar specs.)	3. Stop or reduce pump speed to slack off to maximum set down weight, cycle jars.
3. If the string odes not come free, DO NOT JAR UP!!  JAR DOWN until the string comes free, or an alternative decision is made.	3. If the string odes not come free, DO NOT JAR DOWN!! Jar Up until the string comes free, or an alternative decision is made.	Secondary Actions:
1. Releasing pills, fresh water in salt, acid in limestone.
2. For all cases, check and maintain mud purpose designed to: Eliminate differential pressure, proper type of loss circulation materials, proper hole cleaning rheology
When Circulation is Established.	When circulation is Established:		
1. Slowly increase pump speed to max circulating rate. When possible, work the string to cleaned the hole.	1. Slowly increase pump speed to max circulating rate. When possible, work the string to cleaned the hole.
2. Ream until the pipe is "free".	2. Ream until the pipe is "free".	When the String is Free:
Circulate and condition, checking mud properties, if off bottom, consider return to bottom and conditioning.
3. If pulling OOH for trip, consider returning to bottom to clean the hole.	3. If pulling OOH for trip, consider returning to bottom to clean the hole.

2.36	SUB-SEA ASSEMBLY CHECKLIST

2.36.1	RETRIEVAL OF THE DRILLING PERMANENT GUIDE BASE (PGB)
•	Refer to the Drilling Program for any special requirements and instructions and that the tree rating is suitable for the well specifications.
•	Verify that all required equipment and tools are available prior to installation.
•	Verify any requirements for heavy lifts and notify the OIM.
•	Hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel to discuss the operation and safety issues.

2.36.2	RUNNING & RETRIEVAL (R & R) TOOL PRE-OPERATIONS CHECKS
•	Visually inspect the following:
1.	Pop off valve body, two shear pins, valve position and stop.
2.	Piston retainer bolts and shear pins.
3.	Retrieval pin retainers and pins, other external parts.
•	Function test retrieval pins.
•	Ensure that all fittings are tight.
•	Inspect seal surfaces, and verify that piston travels freely.
•	Inspect retrieval tool joint.
•	Verify running and retrieval procedures with service representative.

2.36.3	OVER-TRAWLABLE PGB R&R TOOL PRE-OPERATIONS CHECKS
•	Visually inspect the following:
1.	Pop off valve, two shear pins, valve position and stop.
2.	Piston retainer bolts and shear pins.
•	Ensure that the piston is bottomed-out in the "up" position, and that shear pins are in position. Visually inspect retrieval tool joint.

2.36.4	PERMANENT GUIDE BASE (PGB) PRE-OPERATIONS CHECKS
•	Ensure that PGB guideposts are compatible with BOP stack posts.
•	Ensure that adapter bolts are fully made up and tight.
•	Once guideposts are installed, verify fit, and straight, and secure.
•	Once anchor pins are installed, insure that they slide freely and that the latch plate is fully engaged in the slot behind the PGB beam.
•	Verify condition of locking bolt threads and locking holes.
•	Inspect release ring for damage, check for upsets or deformations.
•	Ensure that release rings and lock-rings are greased/lubricated. Rotate release ring to ensure circumferential lubrication.
•	Ensure that all tapped holes are filled with grease.	
•	Ensure that deflector bars are free to hinge outward.

2.36.5	PGB NAKE-UP AND RUNNING
•	Verify guy wire installation in moon pool.  Proper sequence.
•	Lower R&R tool onto PGB and ensure that shear studs align with mating holes in PGB beam.
•	Ensure that the 2-arm utility guide is mounted above the PGB running tool.
•	Install brass shear nuts with proper torque
•	Review running procedures, and retrieval tool procedures with service representative.

2.36.6	TUBING HANGER SYSTEM
•	Ensure tubing hanger stand location is within the reach of a crane which can access the derrick V-doors.
•	Ensure the tubing hanger is inspected and checked as per the manufacturer's operating manual.
•	Ensure that anti-rotation pins are fitted.
•	Check that the hanger bores are clean and undamaged.
•	Check landing nipple profiles where fitted.
•	Check that the correct orientation key is fitted, if applicable.
•	Grease the appropriate parts of the hanger lock-down parts.
•	Fit the control line tubing connectors to the tubing hanger.
•	Inspect electrical cable tails where fitted.

2.36.7	TUBING HANGER RUNNING / ORIENTATION TOOLS
•	Ensure the THRT/THOJ orientation is set up as per the Manufacturer's Operations Manual.
•	Ensure cleanliness of the hydraulic systems meets with the specification, especially the sub-surface safety valve line.
•	Ensure operation of all of the THRT functions.
•	Ensure pressure integrity and operation of all hydraulic lines from the panel to the tubing hanger.

2.36.8	X'MAS TREE
•	Refer to the Drilling Program for any special requirements and instructions and that the tree rating is suitable for the well specifications.
•	Verify that all required equipment and tools are available prior to installation.
•	Verify any requirements for heavy lifts and notify the OIM
•	Hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel to discuss the operation and safety issues

2.36.9	PRE-INSTALLATION TESTING
•	Review manufacturers manual "pre-Installation" tests and checks on Xmas tree system with service representative and appropriate rig crew.
•	Verify weight load requirements, weight of tree and transfer from supply boat to moon pool.
•	Stump function and pressure test the tree.
•	Remove protective hydraulic covers and inspect hydraulic couplings for damage and cleanliness.
•	Ensure that all functions on workover control unit have been vented.
•	Review ROV operational procedures and placement of umbilical, with ROV service representative.

2.36.10	CONTROL INTERFACES
•	Ensure proper guidepost guywire tension.
•	Verify that all valves are fully closed and have not been mechanically over ridden.
•	Function all actuated vales open and closed.
•	Verify operating pressures and volumes; verify successful valve operations by monitoring the position of actuated valve override stems.
•	Once tests are completed, ensure that all actuated valves are returned to the closed position.
•	Check the following:
1.	Hydraulic Production Wing – Closed
2.	Hydraulic Production Master – Closed
3.	Manual Annuls Outlet – Closed
4.	Hydraulic Annular Master – Closed
5.	Hydraulics Crossover – Closed
6.	Hydraulic Service Wing – Closed
7.	SCSSV Line Isolation - Open	
8.	VX Test – Open
9.	Tree Cap Test - Closed.
•	Ensure that all pre-insertion tests and checks on control workover systems have been completed.

2.36.11	PRE-RUN CHECKS
•	Remove TA cap but only when the tree is close to the wellhead.
•	Retract VX gasket retainer mechanism to permit removal of VX gasket.
•	Verify communication through both VX cavity test port function and SCSSV function, by monitoring fluid returns at the bottom of the tree orientation spool.
•	Remove Xmas tree from test stump, then remove used VX gasket.
•	Inspect connector and test stump and endure that they are damage free.
•	Inspect, lubricate/change orientation spool seals as required.
•	Install new VX gasket on test stump, lower tree onto stump. Operate retainer mechanism to secure gasket to connector.
•	Disengage locking key from anti-rotation slot, remove Xmas tree running tool.
•	Pre-operational checks on Xmas tree running/handling tool:
1.	Inspect and replace running tool S-seal if damaged.
2.	Check locking dog.
3.	Verify that locking key is retracted fully and secure.
•	Make up on drill pipe.
•	Lubricate dog surfaces, and external seal. Do not use pipe dope on seal.
•	Install running tool in tree as per procedure.
•	Feed guide wires through slots in funnel guides and re-establish guidewire traps to retainer guidewires. Ensure that rig elevator/blocks are unlocked.
•	Review deployment procedures with service representative and appropriate rig crew.

2.37	SUB-SURFACE BOP CHECKLIST

2.37.1	NIPPLE-UP BOP EQUIPMENT (SEMI-SUB)
•	See requirements for well control for pressure testing criteria.	Refer to Well Control Equipment Testing Requirements
•	Ensure that time breakdown of previous nipple-up operations are analyzed to identify areas for improving nipple-up time.
•	Ensure that the nipple-up operation is documented by time breakdown.
•	Measure the height of each BOP component and include the gap between flanges. Ensure to construct a stack-up drawing of the BOP.
•	Perform a JSA and hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel in preparation for running the BOP stack.
•	Review the BOP equipment specifications to ensure that they comply with the Drilling Contract and meet the wells requirement as per the drilling program.
•	Check annular surge bottles, accumulator pre-charge pressures. Inspect the Accumulator to verify that all equipment is in proper working order.
•	For Vetco H-4 wellhead connector, a pull operated check valve, POVC, should be installed on the lock side of the wellhead connector. Verify proper pressure testing and installation.
•	Inspect stabs between LMRP and BOP.	
•	Secondary unlock functions on both the wellhead and riser connectors should be independent of the primary locking function.	
•	Inspect the Marine Riser- See Marine Riser Section.
•	Verify control back-ups.
•	Verify current inspection certificates for equipment, hoses, etc.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient quantity of BOP spare equipment, i.e. rams rubbers, Bonnet door gaskets, Annular element for annular BOP, Bolts and nuts for BOP stack and wing valves, Ring Gaskets for BOP stack and wing valves.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient quantity of valve repair kits to repair wing valves on the BOP stack and valves in the choke and kill manifold. 
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator is of the appropriate size for the upcoming operation and is properly rigged up. 
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator and all lines are clean and free of debris or blockage. 
•	Ensure that the choke line and all high pressure flow lines are clean and free of debris.
•	Pressure test the upper Kelly cock, lower Kelly cock, safety valves and inside BOP equipment.
•	Record all BOP equipment pressure tests in the daily IADC report.
•	Lifting Equipment:
1.	Inspect choke, kill and hydraulic pins for acceptable surface finish.
2.	Verify proper certifications, testing for lifting equipment.
3.	Visually inspect all rise joints to verify that no obstacles are present to hang-up on diverter housing when running or pulling riser.
4.	Check proper handling tools, spider, adapter, telescopic joint, elevators and bails weight requirement for handling stack.
•	Verify riser tension.
•	Verify automatic release procedures, and that they are properly posted.

2.37.2	NIPPLE-DOWN BOP EQUIPMENT (SEMI SUB)
•	Ensure that there is a plan for nippling down of the BOP equipment to keep as much of it as possible off the critical path, i.e., prepare all equipment and hoists for lifting, all necessary equipment and tools, etc.
•	Ensure that time breakdown of previous nipple-down operations are analyzed to identify areas for improving nipple-down time.
•	Ensure that the nipple-down operation is documented by time breakdown.
•	Perform a JSA and hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel in preparation of the nippling-down operation.
•	Ensure that the nipple-down operation complies with the requirements for a heavy lift.
•	Ensure to jet BOP's & riser before nippling-down.
•	Inspect BOP equipment to verify that they are in good condition.
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator and all lines are clean and free of debris or blockage. 
•	Ensure that the choke line and all high pressure flow lines are clean and free of debris.

2.38	SURFACE BOP JACK-UP

2.38.1	NIPPLE-UP BOP EQUIPMENT (JACK-UP)
•	Ensure that there is a plan for nippling up the BOP equipment to keep as much of it as possible off the critical path, i.e. pressure test as much as possible on a test stump, prepare all equipment and hoists for lifting, all necessary equipment and tools, etc
•	Ensure that time breakdown of previous nipple-up operations are analyzed to identify areas for improving nipple-up time.
•	Ensure that the nipple-up operation is documented by time breakdown.
•	Measure the height of each BOP component to ensure that the stack will fit into the available space; this is to include the gap between flanges.  Construct a stack-up drawing of the BOP. Perform a JSA and hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel in preparation of the nippling-up and BOP testing. The BOP testing area and the area around the BOP stack should be properly secured to insure that unauthorized personnel do not enter the area. Review the BOP equipment specifications to ensure that they comply with the Drilling Contract and meet the wells requirement as per the drilling program.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient quantity of proper size and specification ring gaskets are on site. Spare ring gaskets should be available in the event of a failure or damage during nipple up operations.
•	Inspect the high pressure lines and connections to operate the BOP equipment.
•	Check annular surge bottles, accumulator pre-charge pressures. Inspect the Accumulator to verify that all equipment is in proper working order.
•	Ensure to use the plumb line for proper alignment of the BOP stack.
•	Ensure that the nipple-up complies with the requirements for a heavy lift.
•	Inspect BOP lifting slings and equipment to verify that they are in good condition.
•	Prior to installation, ensure that all the space-out subs and appropriate threads for the crossovers and landing casing are on the rig and have been inspected.
•	Inspect inside of tree to determine the orientation slot.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient quantity of BOP spare equipment, i.e. rams rubbers, Bonnet door gaskets, Annular element for annular BOP, Bolts and nuts for BOP stack and wing valves, Ring Gaskets for BOP stack and wing valves.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient quantity of valve repair kits to repair wing valves on the BOP stack and valves in the choke and kill manifold. 
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator is of the appropriate size for the upcoming operation and is properly rigged up. 
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator and all lines are clean and free of debris or blockage. 
•	Ensure that the choke line and all high pressure flow lines are clean and free of debris.
•	Ensure that all wellhead equipment and BOP equipment are properly pressure to high and low pressure tests. BOP testing should be done with proper testing equipment and recorded on a pressure chart.
•	Pressure test the upper Kelly cock, lower Kelly cock, safety valves and inside BOP equipment.
•	Record all BOP equipment pressure tests in the daily IADC report.

2.38.2	NIPPLE-DOWN BOP EQUIPMENT (JACK-UP)
•	Ensure that there is a plan for nippling down of the BOP equipment to keep as much of it as possible off the critical path, i.e., prepare all equipment and hoists for lifting, all necessary equipment and tools, etc.
•	Ensure that time breakdown of previous nipple-down operations are analyzed to identify areas for improving nipple-down time.
•	Ensure that the nipple-down operation is documented by time breakdown.
•	Perform a JSA and hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel in preparation of the nippling-down operation.
•	Ensure that the nipple-down operation complies with the requirements for a heavy lift.
•	Ensure to jet BOP's & riser before nippling-down.
•	Inspect BOP lifting slings and equipment to verify that they are in good condition.
•	Drain the BOP stack and ensure a skirt is installed around base of the riser to avoid spraying well bay with dirty fluid.
•	If possible, pick-up on the high-pressure riser quickly to avoid jetting well bay with dirty fluid, if required.
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator and all lines are clean and free of debris or blockage. 
•	Ensure that the choke line and all high pressure flow lines are clean and free of debris.

2.39	SURFACE BOP LAND CHECKLIST

2.39.1	NIPPLE-UP BOP EQUIPMENT (LAND)
•	Ensure that there is a plan for nippling up the BOP equipment to keep as much of it as possible off the critical path, i.e. pressure test as much as possible on a test stump, prepare equipment and hoists for lifting, all necessary equipment and tools, etc.
•	Ensure that time breakdown of previous nipple-up operations are analyzed to identify areas for improving nipple-up time.
•	Ensure that the nipple-up operation is documented by time breakdown.
•	Measure the height of each BOP component (Including the Bell Nipple) to ensure that the stack will fit into the available space below the rotary beams; this is to include the gap between flanges.  Construct a stack-up drawing of the BOP equipment.
•	Perform a JSA and hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel in preparation of the nippling-up and testing BOP equipment. The BOP testing area and the area around the BOP stack should be properly secured to insure that unauthorized personnel do not enter the area.
•	Review the BOP equipment specifications to ensure that they comply with the Drilling Contract and meet the well requirement as per the drilling program.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient quantity of proper size and specification ring gaskets are on site. Spare ring gaskets should be available in the event of a failure or damage during nipple up operations.
•	Inspect the high pressure lines and connections to operate the BOP equipment.
•	Check annular surge bottles, accumulator pre-charge pressures. Inspect the Accumulator to verify that all equipment is in proper working order.
•	Ensure to use the plumb line for proper alignment of the BOP stack.
•	Ensure that the nipple-up complies with the requirements for a heavy lift.
•	Inspect BOP lifting slings and equipment to verify that they are in good condition.
•	Prior to installation, ensure that all the space-out subs and appropriate threads for the crossovers and landing casing are on the rig and have been inspected.
•	Inspect inside of tree to determine the orientation slot.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient quantity of BOP spare equipment, i.e. rams rubbers, Bonnet door gaskets, Annular element for annular BOP, Bolts and nuts for BOP stack and wing valves, Ring Gaskets for BOP stack and wing valves, etc.
•	Ensure that there is sufficient quantity of Choke and kill manifold valve repair kits to repair wing valves on the BOP stack and valves in the choke and kill manifold. 
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator and all lines are clean and free of debris or blockage. 
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator is of the appropriate size for the upcoming operation and is properly rigged up. 
•	Ensure that the choke line and all high pressure flow lines are clean and free of debris.
•	Ensure that the choke line and all high pressure flow lines are properly secured and tied down.
•	Ensure that all wellhead equipment and BOP equipment are properly pressure tested to high and low pressure tests. BOP testing should be done with proper testing equipment and recorded on a pressure chart.
•	Pressure test the upper Kelly cock, lower Kelly cock, safety valves and inside BOP equipment.
•	Record all BOP equipment pressure tests in the daily IADC report.

2.39.2	NIPPLE DOWN BOP EQUIPMENT (LAND)
•	Ensure that there is a plan for nippling down of the BOP equipment to keep as much of it as possible off the critical path, i.e. prepare equipment and hoists for lifting, all necessary equipment and tools, etc.
•	Ensure that time breakdown of previous nipple-down operations are analyzed to identify areas for improving nipple-down time.
•	Ensure that the nipple-down operation is documented by time breakdown.
•	Perform a JSA and hold a Toolbox meeting with all relevant personnel in preparation of the nippling-down operation.
•	Ensure that the nipple-down operation complies with the requirements for a heavy lift.
•	Ensure to jet BOP's & Bell Nipple before nippling-down.
•	Inspect BOP lifting slings and equipment to verify that they are in good condition.
•	Drain the BOP stack prior to starting nipple down operations.
•	Ensure that the Mud Gas Separator and all lines are clean and free of debris or blockage. 
•	Ensure that the choke line and all high pressure flow lines are clean and free of debris.

2.40	TRIPPING CHECKLIST

2.40.1	GENERAL TRIPPING
•	Review the reasons for making the trip and to discuss them with the Rig Team and the Drilling Office.
•	Verify the drilling program tripping requirements for each hole section.
•	Develop plans for future flat time operations, reviewing requirement for additional personnel, and for the next trip-in-hole.
•	Review variable deck load and space requirements, may need to lay down pipe.
•	Ensure that proper handling tools are readily available and inspect the condition of the tools, i.e.:
1.	Slips, elevators, tongs, hoists, lay down equipment
2.	Mudsavers, safety clamps, air spinners
3.	Crossovers, handling subs.
•	Perform Toolbox meeting; discuss any special procedures, precautions required, operational highlights, possible unexpected events and review previous tripping in this section, or offset wells.
•	Review the torque and drag information from previous trips and drilling.
•	Ensure that the Driller is aware of maximum overpull allowed before commencing any backreaming. This to be included in guidelines for backreaming such as torque and drag range and for pump rates for optimum hole cleaning, as well as BHA component limitations (Downhole Motors).
•	Function test BOP equipment.
•	Ensure to flush the trip tank at the beginning of each shift.  Keep 15 to 20 bbl in the trip tank at all times.  If there is a float in the tank, ensure that it is cleaned regularly.
•	Review the well kill criteria. Refer to: Kick Tolerance
•	Review the flow checking requirements
•	Review lay down procedures, if required.
•	Verify swab and surge criteria and develop running speed guidelines.
•	Detail hole fill requirements and displacement expectations in preparing a trip sheet.
•	Develop requirements for mudchecks, mud weight and mud properties.
•	Develop criteria for tour change during tripping, remembering that many unscheduled events occur during crew change periods.
•	Ensure proper handover during tower changes.
•	Ensure that the safety valves for DP and DC on the rig floor are in open position.
•	Ensure that bit, motor, MWD/LWD, etc. if required, has been checked and is ready to run back in the hole.
•	Check for proper operation of fill-up measuring equipment.
•	Use a pipe wiper and mud saver bucket, if necessary.  Scrape off any excess mud or gumbo.
•	Have Squeegees, Mudvac and cleanup equipment ready when using OBM 
•	Confirm pipe movement effects, check V-door tail rope for length, defects, etc. Replace if necessary.
•	Calibrate rig floor equipment, torque gauges, weight indicator, etc.
•	Calibrate all monitoring and safety equipment around the entire rig.
•	As required and when possible, ensure that work such as slip and cut drill line and servicing the top drive to be done with the bit near the shoe.
•	Set slips 30” below tool joint.
•	Alternate breaks on every trip.
•	Change out stand of drill pipe above BHA on every trip.

2.40.2	PRE-TRIP
•	Verify and review any hole conditioning requirements per the drilling program, or any other source, i.e. logging program, cementing program, etc.
•	Ensure that before tripping to circulate to fluid across shakers is clean, to condition mud and/or remove any connection gas, as required. 
•	Ensure to reciprocate and rotate the drill pipe while circulating bottoms up.
•	Ensure that there are normal hole conditions (torque and drag) before tripping.  Report on morning report.
•	Prepare for pumping any pills or loss circulation materials.
•	Ensure additional slugs or pills are ready for immediate use, if necessary, to ensure well safety during the trip.
•	With the pumps off, observe the well for flow before pumping the slug and beginning to pull out of the hole.
•	Mud density in and out should not differ by more than 0.2 ppg.
•	Monitor the initial circulating returns with particular attention to the types of solids.
•	Clear rig floor and deck of unnecessary equipment.
•	Verify that the tripping equipment is ready, i.e. mud savers, handling tools, etc.
•	Adjust mud weight for required trip margin plus the loss of ECD's, if necessary.
•	Verify that the trip tank is initially clean and calibrated and then fill as required.
•	Ensure the mud utilized to fill the trip tank is of the correct density.
•	Sand trap and all mud pits should already be cleaned prior to running casing. Do not by-pass desilter, desander, and use all solids control equipment during the hole conditioning until the shale shaker is clean. If necessary sweep the hole with hi-vis pills.
•	Verify that returns are clean and mud properties are correct as per the drilling program or other criteria.
•	Check and record all levels in mud tanks, that valves are properly lined up and that transfer pumps are operational.
•	Well control drills should be held at least once every trip while in cased hole.
•	Consider a pit drill.
•	Highlight prompts for jarring actions. Refer to: Stuck Pipe Avoidance Guidelines.

2.40.3	TRIPPING OUT
•	The Well Site Supervisor or Rig Superintendent must be on the rig floor for the commencement of all trips.  He is to remain on the floor for a minimum of fifteen stands and ensure the well is taking correct fill up volume.  If well is not taking proper fill up volume at any time, pipe will be run back to bottom, and the well circulated bottoms up.
•	Utilize the trip tank to fill the hole and monitor the actual displacement of the drill string.  This should be compared to the calculated displacement to determine whether the hole is taking the proper amount of fluid.  Record the decrease or increase in volume on the trip sheet or hole fill sheet.  Immediately investigate any deviation from the calculated value and be prepared to close-in or divert if the well is kicking.
•	Review pumping and backreaming out criteria and communicate to the Toolpusher / Driller, to include GPM, RPM, hoist speed, overpull allowed, etc.
•	Verify proper placement of slugs. 
•	Again, briefly review requirements for well vigilance during crew changes, proper handover and crew change notes and other pertinent information.
•	Ensure that limits are set to allowable overpull before requiring going back down.
•	Control trip speed to avoid surging or swabbing the hole.
•	Recheck displacement criteria with a trip schedule while monitoring for trends.
•	Ensure that breaks on stands are alternated between trips.
•	Verify any staging or special pill placement criteria.
•	Ensure that driller is to notify of any problem during the trip.
•	Ensure to highlight prompts for jarring.
•	Ensure that a wiper rubber is used.
•	Check floats on the trips
•	Ensure to install a safety valve if the trip is interrupted.  The valve is to be left open to monitor the well while circulating over the hole via the trip tank.
•	Ensure that the drill pipe is moved as much as possible while in open hole.
•	Once out of the hole, confirm that it is filled and the blind rams are open.  Monitor it on the trip tank with the trip tank pump running.  Record on morning report.
•	The Driller must not leave the rig floor during his tour unless properly relieved.  Relief must have valid Industry Recognized Well Control certification.
•	Once out of the hole, ensure that the Mudlogger resets all alarms and continuously monitors the trip tank and notifies the Driller of any discrepancy.
•	Do NOT use the blind rams as a hole cover, if it is desirable to close the blind/shear rams while out of the hole, reduce the operating (manifold) pressure as low as possible to avoid extruding the elastomer in the blind/shear rams

2.40.4	TRIPPING IN
•	Again, briefly review requirements for well vigilance during crew changes, proper handover and crew change notes and other pertinent information.
•	Ensure that all bits and stabilizers are gauged before running back in the hole.  Refer to the BHA Checklist.
•	Ensure that all special equipment is tested i.e. motors, MWD, etc., at the surface and at other dedicated depths such as casing shoes, etc.
•	Ensure that all tong make up/breakout, snub lines, shackles, slips, elevators and other handling equipment are in good shape and rigged up for tongs to be used
•	Ensure to monitor the well on the trip tank.
•	Ensure that the drill pipe is filled and circulation is broken at regular intervals.
•	Ensure that running speed is limited to avoid excessive fluid losses and surging the well.
•	Ensure that a trip schedule is monitored, noting the trends.
•	Ensure that any excessive hold-up/torque is recorded.
•	Control trip speed to avoid surging or swabbing the hole.
•	Ensure that the Driller is to report immediately any problem during the trip.  Any hole problems should be recorded, along with depths in the IADC report.
•	Highlight prompts for jarring actions.
•	Ensure that after reaching bottom, establish normal circulation rate. Read and record the circulation pressure from the standpipe gage. If trip gas causes a pit level gain, it should be handled in the same manner as that used for handling a kick.
•	Ensure that on connections when breaking circulation, or when on bottom, that the pumps are brought on slowly.  If possible, establish rotation before breaking circulation and circulate bottoms up before recommencing drilling.
•	Ensure that slow circulating rates (SCR) are taken before drilling proceeds, with pressures taken from the choke panel.

2.41	WELL CONTROL AVOIDANCE GUIDELINES

LISTEN TO THE WELL - IT IS ALWAYS TALKING!!

2.41.1	FUNDAMENTAL CAUSES OF WELL CONTROL SITUATIONS
•	Failure to insure that the hole is taking the proper amounts of fluid and or keeping the hole full.
1.	Failure to properly fill out trip sheets.
2.	Improper use of the trip tank or non-use of trip tank.
3.	Swabbing well in while pulling the pipe.  Refer to the Tripping Checklist.	
•	Lost circulation and the failure to act quickly and properly.  Refer to the Loss Circulation Guideline.
•	Equivalent mud weight too low.  Refer to the ECD Management Guideline.
•	Improper casing seats with kick margins too low.
1.	Failure to set sufficient amount of conductor casing.
2.	Poor surface casing cement bond.
3.	Improper well design and or improper casing setting depths.
•	Not recognizing and correcting mechanical equipment failures.
•	Unprepared to drill into high pressure and permeable zones.
•	Failure to detect a kick in time.
•	Failure to properly shut the well in after detection of a kick.
•	Failure to run drill pipe float.
•	Improper x-over to stab safety valve or inside BOP.
•	Failure to proper test BOP equipment.
•	Improper installation of BOP equipment.
•	Lack of proper BOP and pit drills.
•	Failure to properly monitor Accumulator pressure.
•	Surge pressures while tripping in hole at excessive speed resulting in break down of formations, creating fluid losses.
•	Excessive penetration rates in porous and permeable formations.
•	Increasing amounts of gas cut mud, coupled with the failure temporarily to degas the mud system at the surface.
•	Failure to replace ECD margin when tripping, or trip margin too low.
•	Excessive annular friction losses in  slim hole drilling applications.
•	Failure to properly recognizing TVD, and/or mud programs not taking TVD into account resulting in calculating  low mud weights.
•	A washout in the drill string resulting in a reduction of the ECD's on bottom.
•	Failure to properly flow check and determine shut-in pressures.
•	Failure to properly circulate the influx out of the hole.
•	Failure to keep sufficient mud weighting material at the wellsite.
•	Underground blowouts.
Refer to Kick Prevention

2.41.2	EARLY DETECTION / RECOGNITION OF A KICK
•	Changes in drilling dynamics: torque spikes, weight on bit, and pump pressure.
•	Changes in drill cuttings - concave or splintered shales - always indicative of external pressure inward to the well bore.
•	Flow rate increases and/or flow line temperature increases.
•	Heaving shales.
•	Decrease in shale density.
•	Increases in salinity in mud system, which can be proceeded by an increase in viscosity of the mud.
•	Fluffy mud.
•	Utilization of special pressures measurement instruments such as "Pressure While Drilling" tools.
•	Logging tools - resistivity decreases in transition zones, as well as sonic transit time.
Refer to: Kick Detection

2.41.3	MONITOR THE FOLLOWING FOR WELL CONTROL AVOIDANCE
•	Pump pressure and strokes, a decrease in pump circulating pressure or increase in pump strokes.
•	Torque and drag trends.
•	Displacements trends.
•	Pit losses and gains.
•	Returns when pump is shut off.
•	Connection and trip gas trends, increase in gas cut mud returns.
•	Increased rates of penetration.
•	"D" exponent information and trends.
•	Clean, calibrated trip tanks are crucial.
•	Maintain trip mud requirements trends.

2.42	WELL CONTROL PROCEDURE

2.42.1	PREPARATION TO DRILL
•	Test all BOP equipment and instruments and train the drilling crew in BOP and pit drills according to the Well Control manual. All testing operations and safety drills shall be recorded on the IADC and Daily Drilling Report.
Refer to: Well Control Manual, Well Control Drills, Information to be Kept at Location
•	The Drilling Supervisor shall also perform the following:
Review the drilling contractor's procedures for handling kicks to ensure that they are compatible to those of the company and that a suitable Bridging Document has been written and reviewed.  
Refer to: Project Bridging Document
Ensure that an adequate quantity of mud materials is on location to handle a well-control situation.
Refer to: Minimum Chemical Stocks
•	The Drilling Supervisor should ensure that the rig and crew are properly trained and prepared to handle a kick

2.42.2	WELL CONTROL CHECKLIST

2.42.2.A	CHECKLIST FOR PRE-SPUD, START OF HOLE SECTION & CREW CHANGE
•	Ensure that all well control duty stations are posted and understood by appropriate personnel.
•	Ensure everyone knows his station and job to be reviewed at well control drills.
•	Ensure all relevant personnel know the choke manifold set up.
•	Ensure that the Toolpusher & Drillers have had practice circulating through the choke.
•	Ensure that the recommended closing pressures of annular preventers for testing on the drill pipe is known and used when testing.
•	Ensure that the crews are instructed as to procedures during trip if swabbing or lost circulation is detected.
•	Ensure "on-bottom", pit & kick drills are held at regular intervals, timed and recorded
•	Ensure that tripping, drill collar and out-of-hole drills are held, timed and recorded  in the IADC and daily drilling report.
•	Ensure that the Superintendents, Toolpushers and Drillers have valid well control certificates. (Refer to: Well Control Certification).
•	Ensure that the trip sheets to be utilized on the well are understood and are properly filled out. This is to include trips in and out of the hole. Trip sheets are to be kept on file at the well site.
•	Ensure that well kill worksheets are readily available.
•	Ensure that the entire crew is familiar with the well control kill methods and procedures to be used on the well.
•	Ensure that SCR's are taken every tour and after mud density changes.
•	Ensure that the "choke and kill" drills are performed prior to drilling out each casing string.
•	Ensure that BOP and valve control handles are clearly labeled and kept in proper position for drilling.
•	Ensure that the maximum allowable annular surface pressure (MAASP) is re-calculated after every mud density change.
•	Ensure that the maximum allowable mud density is posted.
•	Ensure that drill pipe, drill collar; hole volume and mud pump capacity figures are readily available.
•	Ensure that new employees have been trained in well control procedures.
•	Ensure compliance with all routine safety procedures and company policies and standards.
•	Ensure that fire extinguishers are located in suitable places and accessible.
•	Ensure that there is lighting under the rig floor.

2.42.2.B	NECESSARY PRE-RECORDED INFORMATION
•	Ensure that a pre-kick well control worksheet is filled out every 500 ft or when SCR's are taken.
•	Drawings of the complete wellhead, BOP assembly and choke manifold.
•	Ensure have all pertinent safety and environmental regulations on the rig and assessable.
•	Ensure the copies of all company orders and BOP manual is accessible on the well site.

2.42.2.C	CHECKLIST EQUIPMENT TESTED AND INSPECTED

2.42.2.C.1	BOP AND CHOKE MANIFOLD
1.	Ensure that annular preventer and rams are operative from remote locations.
2.	Ensure that at least one automatic choke is remotely operative
3.	Ensure that the choke is functioned daily.
4.	Ensure that the pressure gauges on remotely operated choke agree with those on console and choke manifold.
5.	Ensure that the pressure gauges have been calibrated recently.  (All pressure gauges including the gauges/recorders on the mud logger's shack & on the cementers unit should be crosschecked at each BOP test.)
6.	Ensure that the valves are operated daily.
7.	Ensure that the upper kelly cock, lower kelly valve inside BOP, and the spare kelly valve are been tested to full working pressure. This equipment is to be tested each time the BOP equipment is tested.
8.	Ensure the inside BOP and spare kelly valve have appropriate X-over to fit all sizes of pipe in string. The equipment should be on the rig floor and readily accessible. The valves are to be left in the open position.
9.	Ensure that the operating handles for the above near the driller in a place known to all crewmembers.
10.	Ensure that the locking screws on the ram preventers work easily and are operated each time the BOP's are tested.
11.	Ensure the discharge line from the choke line to the mud gas separator and the discharge line to the mud tanks are clean and free of settled solids
12.	Ensure that the choke lines and choke manifold are clean and free of obstructions
13.	Ensure that all chokes are checked, are in good working order and meet specifications..
14.	Ensure that all choke and kill lines and blow down lines are suitably anchored.
15.	Ensure that the flange bolts are re-torqued at proper intervals.
16.	Ensure the BOP assembly meet contractual obligations and conform to relevant orders and regulations.
17.	Ensure that all items of the BOP meet or exceed the overall pressure rating for the assembly.
18.	Ensure that the hand wheels and shafts are attached to the ram BOP's.
19.	Ensure that the stack has clearance for tool joints between pipe rams so pipe can be stripped through the rams.
20.	Ensure that there are extra ring gaskets, bonnet seal rings, and BOP flange bolts on hand.
21.	Ensure that there is a fill-up line separate from the kill line and it is normally used with the trip tank.
22.	Ensure that there are spare repair kits for the valves and chokes  for the choke manifold available.
23.	Ensure that the choke lines are manifolded so that fluids can be discarded, gas separated safely or mud recovered and de gassed.
24.	Ensure that the mud gas separator is sized to the maximum anticipated flow as per the drilling program for the well. Also refer to the minimum sizing of mud gas separators in the well control manual.
25.	Ensure that BOP's are functioned and that all choke and kill valves are flushed as per the Well Control Manual or as amended in the Bridging Document.
26.	Ensure that the valve handles and wheels are attached.
27.	Ensure that the remote closing unit control panel is function tested during each BOP test.
28.	Ensure the BOP and valve control handles clearly labeled and kept in "open" or "closed" position.  Unless using a sub-sea accumulator unit and isolation of a leak or troubleshooting is required.
29.	Ensure that BOP equipment is tested as per the testing interval set forth in the Well Control Manual or at more frequent interval as identified in the drilling program or as specified by the operator.

2.42.2.C.2	ACCUMULATOR AND RELATED HYDRAULIC SYSTEM
1.	Ensure that there are both a floor mounted and remote accumulator control panel and are clearly labeled.
2.	Ensure that the off floor control panel is effective if the floor unit is destroyed.
3.	Ensure that there is an alternative power source to recharge the accumulator if the main source fails
4.	Ensure that there is light on the driller's console indicating when the accumulator pump is working and functional.
5.	All equipment should meet with RP53 & API RP16E standards.
6.	HPHT wells should also meet all company standards and operator specifications.
7.	Ensure that the control lines are the same pressure rating of the accumulator.
8.	Ensure that the control lines are inspected and are in good working condition.
9.	Ensure that each accumulator bottle has the proper nitrogen charge.	
10.	Ensure that the valves between the accumulator storage bottles and the main operating manifold are open.
11.	Ensure that the control for the blind/shear rams have a cover to prevent accidental or unreasoned closure and are not locked.
12.	Ensure that all the accumulator pumps are in good working order.

2.42.2.C.3	ACCUMULATOR AND RELATED HYDRAULIC SYSTEM
1.	Ensure that the nitrogen back up systems are properly hooked up with the correct number of bottles furnished.
2.	Ensure that all 4-way valves are properly labeled as to their function and positioned in the open or closed position as required.
3.	Ensure that the general maintenance of the accumulator system is in good working order, i.e. no air or hydraulic leaks. If there is any doubt as to the maintenance of the accumulator, the unit should be inspected by a certified service company.
4.	Ensure that all hydraulic functions can be operated from all of the remote stations.

2.42.2.C.4	MUD VOLUME MONITORING SYSTEM
1.	Ensure that there is a pit volume totalizer and recorder mounted in easy view of the driller and is in proper working order.
2.	Ensure that there is a flow indicator on the flow line and is in proper working order.
3.	Ensure that the rig has a  trip tank. Verify that the trip tank is in proper working order. Verify the volume of the trip tank and the calibration increments of the trip tank.
4.	Ensure the high and low alarms on pit volume recorder and flow line flow sensor are set as close as possible together.
5.	Ensure that the high and low alarms on both pit volume recorder and flow line flow sensor are operative at all times.
6.	Ensure that the calibration of the pit level recorder and flow line indicator is accurate.  Cross-check with the mud loggers
7.	Ensure that the pump stroke counters are installed on all pumps and are operative at all times.

2.42.2.C.5	OTHER EQUIPMENT
1.	Ensure that the mud pumps are in proper working condition and meet specifications.
2.	Ensure that the mixing system, including weight material handling system, adequate and in proper working order.
3.	Ensure that the degasser is in good working condition and operated daily.
4.	Ensure that the centrifuge and other solids control equipment are working according to manufacturers specifications.
5.	Ensure that the cement unit is in good shape, pressure tested, gauges calibrated and crosschecked, etc.
6.	Ensure that all high-pressure cement lines are properly secured and that the area in proper roped off during high pressure testing or pumping operations.
7.	Ensure that the mud gas separator conforms, as a minimum, to company standards.
8.	Ensure that the mud gas separator is clean and free of debris. Verify that the input and output lines are clean and free of debris.
9.	If HPHT, ensure that the cement pumps are capable of reaching the desired pressures.  If not, a high-pressure pump must be available.

2.42.2.D	NECESSARY SUPPLIES ON HAND
1.	Ensure that there are sufficient amounts of mud-treating chemicals and weighting material at the well site.
2.	Ensure that there is sufficient lost circulation material at the well site.
3.	Ensure that there is sufficient cement at the well site.
4.	Ensure that there is adequate fresh water or diesel to aid in handling any problems involving plastic viscosity or lost circulation
5.	Ensure that there is enough liquid mud in storage and of suitable properties.
6.	Ensure that there are sufficient spare ram rubbers, spare elements and seals for the annular type preventer on location and other BOP equipment that may be required for the upcoming operation.
7.	Ensure that there are spare choke manifold parts and wrenches properly located for immediate use.
8.	For HPHT wells, refer to: HPHT Checklist and Guidelines
3.	RIG CHECKLISTS

To describe hazards present in the next checklists use the following classification:
H:	High
M:	Medium
L:	Low

3.1	IPM GENERAL REQUIREMENTS

3.1.1	IPM DEFENSIVE DRIVING REQUIREMENTS
1	WELL SITE SUPERVISOR: On-Duty
2	WELL SITE SUPERVISOR: Off-Duty
3	ASSISTANT WELL SITE SUPERVISOR: On-Duty
4	ASSISTANT WELL SITE SUPERVISOR: Off-Duty
5	DRILLING ENGINEER: #A
6	DRILLING ENGINEER: #B
7	PROJECT OFFICE STAFF
8	DO STAFF DISPLAY THE 5 SEEING HABITS?
9	DO STAFF WEAR SEAT BELTS?
10	DRIVE RIGHT MONITORS INSTALLED?
11	TRAINING RECORDS ARE ACCURATE AND IN ORDER?
12	JOURNEY MANAGEMENT SYSTEM IN PLACE?

3.1.2	RECORDS AND TRAINING
1	REGULAR SAFETY INSPECTIONS BY CONTRACTOR; CORRECTION MADE; RECORDS AVAILABLE

3	SAFETY MEETINGS, RECORDS
4	PIT DRILLS / BOP DRILLS; RECORDS
5	FIRE DRILLS & RECORDS
6	ACCIDENTS, INJURY & ILLNESS FORM FILLED OUT; RECORDS; FOLLOW-UP ON ACCIDENTS AND ACTION TAKEN TO PREVENT RECURRENCE
7	FIRST AID LOG FOR MINOR INJURIES - LEGIBLE / UP TO DATE
8	TRAINING FOR PERSONNEL (First Aid, Well Control, H2S, Other) CERTIFICATION ON FILE
9	SAFETY POLICIES ORIENTATION GIVEN TO ALL NEW EMPLOYEES
10	HAZARDOUS / TOXIC SUBSTANCES IDENTIFIED - STORAGE & HANDLING PRECAUTIONS TAKEN; MSDS ON CHEMICALS
11	EMERGENCY TELEPHONE NUMBERS POSTED (CHECKED / TESTED)
12	MAINTENANCE PROGRAM & RECORDS FOR CRITICAL EQUIPMENT

3.1.3	RIG GENERAL REQUIREMENTS
1	SAFETY GEAR AVAILABLE AND WORN
2	APPROPRIATE WARNING AND SAFETY SIGNS DISPLAYED
3	QUALIFIED FIRST AIDER ON EACH CREW
4	FIRST AID SUPPLIES
5	EMERGENCY EYEWASH
6	ELECTRICAL RECEPTACLES, PLUGS, WIRING IN GOOD ORDER
