7	LIGHTING ADEQUATE & PROPERLY PROTECTED
8	FIRE EXTINGUISHERS - ALL MUST BE CHARGED, IN BRACKETS, TAGGED & SEALED
9	FIRE FIGHTING EQUIPMENT AVAILABLE
10	FIRE PUMPS TESTED OUTPUT PRESSURE
11	FIRE PUMPS - LOCAL / REMOTE START FUNCTIONED
12	FIRE PREVENTION - IGNITION HAZARDS PROHIBITED
13	POLLUTION CONTROL - NO FUEL SPILLS, RESERVE PIT LEAKS, RUBBISH IN PITS
14	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING
15	CONFINED SPACE PROCEDURES IN EFFECT (PERMIT SYSTEM)
16	LOCKOUT / TAGOUT PROCEDURES IN EFFECT
17	HIGH PRESSURE LINES IDENTIFIED, SECURED / ANCHORED
18	FLARE LINE(S) SECURED / STAKED - HAZARD SIGNS POSTED
19	SYSTEM OF HAZARDOUS AREA CLASSIFICATION USED
20	SAFE DRILLING PROCEDURES & STANDARDS (COMPLETE / UPDATED / AUTHORIZED)
21	ACCIDENT REPORTING FORMS
22	PROCEDURE FOR REPORTING ACCIDENTS
23	CONTINGENCY PLAN (CRITICAL H2S WELLS) - PROCEDURES KNOWN & USED
24	APPROPRIATE REGULATORY PERMITS
25	WORK PERMIT FORMS - RECORDS KEPT
26	ALARMS - AUDIBLE IN ALL AREAS; DATE OF LAST FUNCTION TEST
27	EMERGENCY LIGHTING / PORTABLE LIGHTS (DATE OF LAST TEST)
28	MUSTER POINT / SAFE AREAS CLEARLY MARKED (ALL PERSONNEL BRIEFED & KNOW THE DRILL)

3.2	SAFETY AND FIRE FIGHTING

3.2.1	PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT
1	HARD HATS & STEEL-TOED BOOTS WORN BY ALL PERSONNEL ON SITE
2	VISITOR'S HARD HATS AVAILABLE
3	EYE PROTECTION
4	GLOVES
5	PROTECTIVE CLOTHING
6	HEARING PROTECTION
7	RESPIRATORY PROTECTION
8	BELTS & SAFETY LINES
(All with adequate stocks & 24 hour accessibility)

3.2.2	DERRICK ESCAPE UNIT
1	INSPECT FOR EXTERIOR CORROSION ON UNIT.
2	VISUALLY INSPECT SPROCKETS AND CHAIN DRIVE.
3	VISUALLY INSPECT BOLTED OR FIXED MOUNTINGS.
4	ROTATE HANDWHEEL AND PAY OUT TEN FEET TO CONFIRM OPERATION. INSPECT CONDITION OF WIRE ROPE FOR DAMAGE OR CORROSION BEFORE REWINDING DRUM.
5	INSPECT SNATCH BLOCK AND HOOK ASSEMBLY FOR FREE OPERATION.
6	INSPECT HARNESS FOR GENERAL CONDITION.
7	INSPECT GUIDELINE WIRE

3.2.3	FIRE PUMP ENGINE
1	CHECK RESULTS OF LAST ANALYSIS REPORT ON ENGINE OIL.
2	START THE ENGINE AND RUN THE UNIT UNDER LOAD FOR APPROXIMATELY 30 MIN.
3	CHECK THE COOLING WATER TEMPERATURE - ENSURE IN ACCORDANCE WITH MANUFACTURERS OPERATIONS MANUAL.
4	CHECK THE LUBE OIL PRESSURE - ENSURE IN ACCORDANCE WITH MANUFACTURERS OPERATIONS MANUAL.
5	CHECK THE ENGINE COOLING WATER AND LUBE OIL SYSTEMS FOR LEAKS.
6	CHECK THE DATE THAT THE CONTENTS OF THE FUEL TANK WERE LAST CHANGED.
7	CHECK THE OPERATION OF THE LOW OIL PRESSURE SHUTDOWN.
8	CHECK THE OPERATION OF THE ENGINE OVERSPEED SHUTDOWN - RECORD RPM'S.

3.2.4	RIG BATTERIES
1	MAKE GENERAL RANDOM CHECK OF PHYSICAL CONDITION OF RIG BATTERIES.
2	ASK FOR RANDOM TEST OF CELL SPECIFIC GRAVITY AND ENSURE BATTERIES ARE BEING KEPT WITHIN SPECIFICATIONS FOR BATTERY TYPE.

3.2.5	GENERAL ALARM SYSTEM
1	CHECK ALL ALARM BELLS/ALARMS AND FLASHING ROTATING LIGHTS ARE WORKING AND ARE IN GOOD CONDITION.

3.2.6	EMERGENCY LIGHTING
1	VISUALLY CHECK LIGHTS FOR LOOSE OR BROKEN MOUNTS, BROKEN OR CRACKED GLASSWARE, BADLY CORRODED ENCLOSURES.
2	FUNCTION TEST A RANDOM SAMPLE OF EMERGENCY LIGHTING FOR PROPER OPERATION.

3.2.7	GAS DETECTION SYSTEM (HYDROCARBON)
1	CHECK CONTROL PANEL AND VERIFY ALL CHANNELS ARE OPERATIONAL.
2	SELECT A RANDOM NUMBER OF SENSORS AND LOCATIONS. ASK RIG PERSONNEL TO APPLY CALIBRATION GAS AND CONFIRM METERS DISPLAY CORRECT PERCENTAGES OF COMBUSTIBLE GAS AS PER CALIBRATION GAS MIXTURES.
3	ENSURE THAT LOW LEVEL ALARM INITIATES AT 10% LEVEL.
4	ENSURE THAT HIGH LEVEL ALARM INITIATES AT 20% LEVEL.
5	INSPECT 3/4 SENSORS TO ENSURE SENSORS ARE FREE FROM CONTAMINATION, PAINT ETC.

3.2.8	GAS DETECTION SYSTEM (H2S)
1	CHECK CONTROL PANEL AND VERIFY THAT ALL CHANNELS ARE OPERATIONAL.
2	SELECT A RANDOM NUMBER OF SENSORS AND LOCATIONS. ASK RIG PERSONNEL TO APPLY CALIBRATION GAS AND CONFIRM METERS DISPLAY CORRECT PPM OF H2S GAS AS PER CALIBRATION GAS MIXTURE.
3	ENSURE THAT LOW LEVEL ALARM INITIATES AT 10 PPM.
4	ENSURE THAT HIGH LEVEL ALARM INITIATES AT 20 PPM.
5	INSPECT 2/3 OF SENSORS TO ENSURE CLEANLINESS AND FREE FROM CONTAMINATION.

3.2.9	H2S REQUIREMENTS
1	COPY OF APPROPRIATE H2S REGULATIONS ON SITE
2	COPY OF WELL CONTINGENCY PLAN ON LOCATION
3	H2S SAFETY TRAINING OF PERSONNEL (TYPE AND FREQUENCY OF TRAINING)
4	MATERIALS & EQUIPMENT SUITABLE FOR H2S SERVICE
5	PROTECTIVE BREATHING EQUIPMENT MAINTAINED AT TWO PLACES ON WELL SITE
6	TWO WIND DIRECTION INDICATORS AT STRATEGIC LOCATIONS
7	AUTOMATIC H2S DETECTION & ALARM SYSTEM - ALARMS SET AT 10 PPM, RESPONSE TESTED
8	CHOKE MANIFOLD, MUD GAS SEPARATOR, FLAME & FLARE IGNITION
9	SECONDARY REMOTE CONTROL OF BOP & CHOKE EQUIPMENT - A SAFE DISTANCE FROM WELLHEAD
10	FULL FUNCTION TEST OF REMOTE PANEL DURING BOP TEST
11	DST OF H2S ZONES ONLY DURING DAYLIGHT
12	CERTIFICATES OF COMPLIANCE AND/OR PERMITS AS REQUIRED
13	SYSTEM OF BREATHING AIR MANIFOLD, HOSES & MASKS ON RIG FLOOR & IN BRIEFING AREAS
14	CASCADE SYSTEM TO REFILL INDIVIDUAL BOTTLES (CAPACITY; NUMBER OF MASKS AVAILABLE AT LOCATIONS)
15	O2 RESUSCITATOR; STOKES FILTER; RETRIEVAL ROPES WITH HARNESS
16	FACIAL HAIR REQUIREMENTS (BEARDS PROHIBITED)
17	CHALKBOARDS; BULLHORNS - FULLY EQUIPPED WITH PENS AND ERASERS
18	WEEKLY H2S DRILLS (FREQUENCY; DEFICIENCIES CORRECTED)
19	SAFE BREATHING AREAS DESIGNATED - MUSTER AREAS IDENTIFIED & KNOWN
20	WATER BASE MUD pH 10.0 OR GREATER; MUD ADDITIVES TO NEUTRALISE H2S AVAILABLE IN SIGNIFICANT QUANTITIES ON SITE
21	FIRST AID TRAINING FOR H2S EXPOSURE PROVIDED IN EMPLOYEE TRAINING

3.2.10	FIRE STATION HOSES
1	CHECK THREADED END OF FIRE HOSES FOR VISIBLE DAMAGE.
2	CHECK FIRE HOSE STORAGE AND DRYNESS.
3	INSPECT FIRE HOSES FOR PHYSICAL DAMAGE.
4	CHECK STATION FOR READINESS AND GOOD HOUSEKEEPING.

3.2.11	FIRE PUMP
1	RUN PUMP FOR A SHORT PERIOD AND CHECK FOR VIBRATION AND OVERHEATING. CHECK MECHANICAL SEAL FOR LEAKAGE.
2	CHECK DATE OF LAST MAJOR OVERHAUL.

3.2.12	PORTABLE FIRE EXTINGUISHERS
1	CHECK DATE OF LAST THIRD PARTY INSPECTION - CHECK THAT ANY DEFECTS RAISED HAVE BEEN ADDRESSED.
2	CHECK EXTINGUISHERS FOR PROPER STORAGE, ACCESSIBILITY, VISIBILITY AND CONDITION OF BOXES.
3	CHECK AND ENSURE THE OPERATING INSTRUCTIONS ARE CLEAR AND LEGIBLE.

3.2.13	EMERGENCY EQUIPMENT BOXES
1	CHECK GENERAL CONDITION OF ALL EQUIPMENT IN SAFETY AND CRASH KIT FOR ANY EVIDENCE OF DAMAGE, DEFORMATION OR DETERIORATION.
2	ENSURE SPARE BREATHING APPARATUS BOTTLES ARE FULLY CHARGED.
3	CHECK THAT THE CONTENTS OF THE BOX MATCH THE INVENTORY LIST.

3.2.14	FIXED CO2 SYSTEM
1	CHECK THE DATE OF LAST THIRD PARTY INSPECTION.
2	MAKE A GENERAL INSPECTION OF THE ENTIRE SYSTEM TO ENSURE THAT THERE IS NOTHING TO INTERFERE WITH THE OPERATION OF THE EQUIPMENT OR ACCESS TO THE RELEASE CONTROLS.
3	INSPECT ALL COMPONENTS OF THE SYSTEM SUCH AS PIPING, NOZZLES, VALVES, SIRENS, REMOTE PULL CONTROLS AND BOXES FOR VISIBLE DAMAGE OR CORROSION.
4	EXAMINE ALL NOZZLES AND PIPING FOR OBSTRUCTIONS FROM PAINT, OIL, GREASE, DIRT OR ANY MATTER THAT WOULD INTERFERE WITH PROPER OPERATION.
5	CHECK CO2 AREA ALARMS.

3.2.15	COMPRESSOR FOR BREATHING APPARATUS
1	INSPECT ALL HOSES FOR CHAFFING, EXCESSIVE WEAR DAMAGE.
2	CHECK CONDITION OF DRIVE BELTS AND ALSO CORRECT TENSION.
3	CHECK CONDITION OF ALL ELECTRICAL CABLE AND PLUG FOR ANY OBVIOUS DAMAGE E.G. CUTS, FLATTENED OR PINCHED SECTIONS.
4	CHECK DATE OF LAST AIR FILTER CHANGE.
5	RUN COMPRESSOR ON TEST FOR SEVERAL MINUTES FOR ANY DEFECTS.

3.2.16	BREATHING APPARATUS
1	MASK AND HOSE: EXAMINE FOR CONTAMINATION, DAMAGE OR CORROSION.
2	CHECK LAST DATE WHEN CYLINDERS WERE SENT FOR INSPECTION/RECERTIFICATION.
3	CHECK CYLINDER GAUGE FOR DAMAGE.
4	CHECK CYLINDER PRESSURE.
5	EXAMINE HARNESS FOR EXCESSIVE WEAR OR DAMAGE.
6	CLOSE CYLINDER VALVE AND NOTE OPERATION OF LOW AIR PRESSURE WARNING WHISTLE.
7	CHECK THAT THE REQUIREMENTS OF THE MANUAL ARE ADHERED TO.

3.2.17	INERTIA REELS
1	INSPECT THE SECURING SHACKLE TO ENSURE IT IS FREE FROM DISTORTION OR DEFECTS. ENSURE THE BOW SHACKLE PINS AND NUT/BOLT ARE TIGHT AND SECURE.
2	CHECK MAIN BODY CASTING OF THE FALL ARRESTOR FOR DEFECTS, CRACKS, DISTORTION ETC.
3	CHECK THE SPRING RETRACTION DEVICE FOR THE CABLE AND ENSURE IT IS FULLY OPERATIONAL.
4	TEST THE LOCKING ACTION BY JERKING ON THE CABLE QUICKLY.
5	INSPECT THE SWIVEL SCREWGATE HOOK FOR EVIDENCE OF DAMAGE OR DETERIORATION.
6	INSPECT GALVANIZED STEEL SAFETY WIRE LINE FOR DAMAGE, KINKS, CORROSION ETC.
7	IF FITTED, CHECK THAT THE RED INDICATOR BUTTON IS RECESSED TO THE CORRECT DEPTH. IF FLUSH OR PROTRUDING, THE UNIT MUST BE WITHDRAWN FROM SERVICE AND RECERTIFIED.
8	CHECK THE RED FERRULE/BOBBIN IS NOT VISIBLE ON CABLE. IF IT IS, THE LAST METER OF CABLE HAS BEEN USED TO ARREST A FALL, THEREFORE OVERHAUL UNIT IMMEDIATELY.
9	CHECK THAT ALL UNITS HAVE CURRENT CERTIFICATION.

3.2.18	WELDING AND BURNING
1	HOTWORK PERMIT PROCEDURE IN PLACE & ENFORCED - HAZARDOUS AREA LIMITS KNOWN, PERMITS USED
2	FIRE WATCH PERSONNEL REQUIRED DURING WELDING / BURNING
3	FIRE EXTINGUISHERS PROVIDED
4	ADEQUATE VENTILATION
5	WELDING MACHINES PROPERLY GROUNDED
6	HOSES, FITTINGS, GAUGES & REGULATORS IN GOOD WORKING ORDER; FRICTION LIGHTERS USED; MATCHES PROHIBITED
7	COMPRESSED GAS CYLINDERS PROPERLY STORED & SECURED, SEPARATED BY DISTANCE / BARRIER
8	PROPER PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT AVAILABLE & USE ENFORCED
9	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING
10	PERFORATING NOTICE FOR WELDING MACHINES AVAILABLE ON-SITE & USED DURING "RADIO SILENCE"

3.3	WELL CONTROL

3.3.1	WELL CONTROL EQUIPMENT TESTING REQUIREMENTS
1	BOP'S TESTED AS PER WELL CONTROL MANUAL AND NOTED ON IADC - LAST TEST DATE RECORDED
2	BOTH LOW AND HIGH PRESSURE TEST DONE (HI TEST / LOW TEST RECORDED)
3	TRIP TANK, PVT EQUIPMENT CALIBRATED

3.3.2	BOP STACK & DIVERTER MINIMUM REQUIREMENTS
1	PROPER PRESSURE RATING / CONFIGURATION FOR BOP EQUIPMENT (RAMS / ANNULAR)
2	ONE SET OF PIPE RAMS FOR EACH PIPE SIZE IN USE
3	TWO FULL BORE VALVES ON KILL AND CHOKE - ONE ON CHOKE HYDRAULIC
4	CASING RAMS IN USE WHEN CASING IS RUN

3.3.3	WELL CONTROL CERTIFICATION
1	ALL COMPANY PERSONNEL WITH CURRENT WELL CONTROL CERTIFICATION
2	LIST CONTRACTOR AND OPERATOR PERSONNEL WITHOUT CERTIFICATION

3.3.4	CONSENSUS OF WELL CONTROL PROCEDURES IN BRIDGING DOCUMENT
1	PLAN FOR DRILLS DOCUMENTED IN BRIDGING DOCUMENT
2	DRILLS BEING DONE (STATE INTERVAL) AND DOCUMENTED IN IADC

3.3.5	CASING PRESSURE TESTING
1	PRESSURE TESTING REQUIREMENTS CONTAINED IN DRILLING PROGRAM
2	CHARTS CONSTRUCTED AT RIG SITE / MAINTAINED IN WELLFILE

3.3.6	MINIMUM CHEMICAL STOCK
1	BARITE ON LOCATION (QUANTITY)
2	CEMENT ON LOCATION (QUANTITY)

3.3.7	CONTINGENCY STRIPPING PROCEDURE
1	STRIPPING CHECKLIST AVAILABLE

3.3.8	KICK TOLERANCE
1	GUIDELINES BEING FOLLOWED
2	DAILY KICK TOLERANCE CALCULATION DONE AT RIGSITE (CURRENT KT RECORDED)

3.3.9	BARRIERS
1	BARRIER GUIDELINES BEING FOLLOWED

3.3.10	AUTHORITY DURING WELL OPERATIONS
1	ORGANIGRAM OF PERSONNEL AND RESPONSIBILITIES AT THE RIGSITE (LOCATION POSTED)

3.3.11	AGREEMENT ON SPECIFIC WELL CONTROL PROCEDURES
1	PRE-WELL CONTROL MEETINGS HELD WHEN APPROPRIATE
2	WRITTEN PROCEDURE FOR WELL CONTROL SIGNED AND POSTED

3.3.12	SHUT-IN PROCEDURE
1	DRILLER HAS AUTHORITY TO SHUT-IN WELL IF NECESSARY
2	SHUT-IN PROCEDURE POSTED AT DRILLER'S POSITION

3.3.13	KICK CONTROL METHOD
1	PERSONNEL FAMILIAR WITH VARIOUS METHODS OF CONTROL

3.3.14	KICK DETECTION
1	DRILLING BREAKS NORMALLY FLOW-CHECKED
2	PVT DATA CONTINUOUSLY MONITORED
3	DRILLER CONSOLE CONTAINS PVT READOUT

3.3.15	KICK PREVENTION
1	HOLE LEVEL MONITORED CONTINUOUSLY
2	TRIP TANK IN USE ON TRIPS
3	TRIP SHEET FILLED OUT ON TRIPS
4	MUD LOGGERS MONITORING TRIPS INDEPENDENTLY
5	OVERBALANCE OF 200 PSI BEING OBSERVED AT ALL TIMES

3.3.16	CONSTANT BOTTOM HOLE PRESSURE
1	PHILOSOPHY BEING OBSERVED

3.3.17	REPORTING OF KICKS
1	COMPLETION OF WELL CONTROL INCIDENT REPORT FORM AS REQUIRED

3.3.18	SHALLOW GAS RISK ASSESSMENT AND CONTINGENCY
1	RISK ASSESSMENT & CONTINGENCY IN PLACE
2	NEVER SHUT-IN (per shallow gas plan)

3.3.19	WELL CONTROL WHILE RUNNING CASING
1	TRIP TANK IN USE WHILE RUNNING CASING
2	SURGE CALCULATIONS / RUNNING SPEED CALCULATED PRIOR TO JOB
3	TWO FLOAT (SHOE AND COLLAR) RUN WHEN REQUIRED
4	CASING RAMS BEING USED
5	PROPERLY THREADED SWAGE ON FLOOR WHILE RUNNING PIPE

3.3.20	LEAK OFF TEST OR SHOE TEST
1	LOT PERFORMED AT SPECIFIED DEPTH WITH SPECIFIED MUD WEIGHT AND PRESSURE - RECORDED
2	FIT PERFORMED AT SPECIFIED DEPTH WITH SPECIFIED MUD WEIGHT AND PRESSURE - RECORDED

3.3.21	SURFACE WELL CONTROL EQUIPMENT FOR LOGGING OPERATIONS
1	INCLUDED IN WELL PROGRAM

3.3.22	KICK DETECTION EQUIPMENT: MINIMUM STANDARDS
1	PIT VOLUME TOTALIZER (PVT SYSTEM) IN USE AND FUNCTIONING PROPERLY
2	FLOW METER ("FLOW SHOW") IN USE AND FUNCTIONING PROPERLY
3	TRIP TANK INDICATOR IN USE AND FUNCTIONING PROPERLY

3.3.23	GAS DETECTION SERVICE & EQUIPMENT: MINIMUM STANDARDS
1	FIXED GAS DETECTION EQUIPMENT: BELL NIPPLE AND SHAKER
2	FIXED H2S DETECTION EQUIPMENT: RIG FLOOR, SHAKER, CELLAR DECK
3	PORTABLE ELECTRONIC GAS DETECTORS (OXYGEN, COMBUSTIBLE GASES, AND H2S)
4	MONTHLY DRILLS CONDUCTED FOR RESPONSE TO COMBUSTIBLE OR TOXIC GASES

3.3.24	FIRE FIGHTING SERVICE AND EQUIPMENT
1	(1) DIESEL OR ELECTRICAL DRIVEN PUMP CAPABLE OF 250 GPM AT 125 PSI
2	(3) LIVE REELED FIRE HOSES EACH 1-1/4" BY 100 FT WITH COMBINATION STREAM/FOG NOZZLES - (1) RIG FLOOR (1) MAIN ENGINES
3	CARBON DIOXIDE FIRE EXTINGUISHERS AS DESCRIBED IN FIREFIGHTING SERVICE AND EQUIPMENT STANDARD

3.3.25	WELL CONTROL MANUAL EXCERPTS
1	WHEN TRIPPING, FLOW CHECKS MADE OFF BOTTOM, LOWEST SHOE, AT HWDP
2	SLOW CIRCULATION PRESSURES AND RATES TAKEN AT BEGINNING OF EACH TOUR, MUD WEIGHT CHANGES, NOZZLES CHANGES, BHA CHANGES - DOCUMENTED IN IADC
3	DISTANCE FROM RKB TO PIPE RAMS POSTED AT DRILLER'S STATION
4	UPON TRIP INTERRUPTION, HAND TIGHT INSTALLATION OF SAFETY VALVE REQUIRED
5	MINIMUM OF ONE SAFETY VALVE (OPEN), ONE INSIDE BOP ON FLOOR WITH CROSSOVERS IF NECESSARY
6	MAASP KNOWN AT ALL TIMES AND POSTED. OPTIONS DURING WELL CONTROL KNOWN BY SUPERVISORS.
7	SPECIAL WELL CONTROL SITUATIONS: OIL BASE MUD
8	CIRCULATION THROUGH CHOKE IF FLOW CHECK PROVES INCONCLUSIVE
9	SPECIAL PRECAUTIONS DURING TRIPPING

3.3.26	PROJECT BRIDGING DOCUMENT
1	SIGNED BY CLIENT AND ALL APPROPRIATED PARTIES

3.3.27	REQUEST FOR DISPENSATION
1	ALL DEVIATIONS FROM POLICIES & STANDARDS DOCUMENTED

3.3.28	WELL CONTROL REQUIREMENTS - ORGANIZATION
1	WELL SITE SUPERVISOR ON-DUTY AND OFF-DUTY
2	ASSISTANT WELL SITE SUPERVISOR ON-DUTY AND OFF-DUTY
3	MUD ENGINEER
4	CEMENT ENGINEER
5	DRILLING CONTRACTOR SENIOR TOOLPUSHER ON-DUTY AND OFF-DUTY
6	DRILLING CONTRACTOR TOURPUSHER ON-DUTY AND OFF-DUTY
7	DRILLING CONTRACTOR DRILLER ON-DUTY AND OFF-DUTY
8	AUDITOR VERIFICATION: DOES THE DRILLER KNOW EXACTLY HOW TO CLOSE IN THE WELL IN CASE OF AN INFLUX?
9	AUDITOR VERIFICATION: IS THE DRILLER EMPOWERED TO CLOSE-IN THE WELL IN CASE OF AN INFLUX WITHOUT PRIOR APPROVAL FROM THE TOOLPUSHER?
10	IS THERE A WORKING ARCHIVE SYSTEM?
11	ARE THERE DAILY KICK SHEETS BEING CALCULATED?
12	IS THE KICK TOLERANCE CALCULATED PER POLICY?
13	IS THERE A COMMUNICATIONS BOARD WITH UP-TO-DATE INFO?
14	WELL CONTROL ROLES AND RESPONSIBILITIES DEFINED?
15	WELL CONTROL KICK REPORTS IN THE FILES?

3.3.29	BOP / ACCUMULATOR / CHOKE MANIFOLD
1	BOP ARRANGEMENT & PRESSURE RATINGS TO MEET OPERATOR SPECIFICATIONS
2	BOP'S TESTED, CLOSING PROCEDURE & CASING PRESSURE LIMIT POSTED (DRILLS REPORTED, FREQUENCY, DATE OF LAST TEST TO FULL RATING)
3	NO MISSING BOLTS; ALL BOLTS TIGHT & SECURE (BOLTS CHECKED WEEKLY & REPORTED)
4	METAL CONTROL LINES IN GOOD CONDITION; NO LEAKS; LINES NOT IN USE BLANKED OFF
5	BOP'S SECURELY BRACED TO SUBSTRUCTURE, ADEQUATE DESIGN
6	ACCUMULATOR UNIT & CONTROLS AT APPROPRIATE DISTANCE FROM WELLHEAD
7	ACCUMULATOR CAPACITY & PRESSURE SUFFICIENT TO OPERATE ALL PREVENTERS TO API RP 53
8	REGULATOR BYPASS VALVE. ALL CONTROL HOSES TESTED TO FULL ACCUMULATOR PRESSURE
9	ACCUMULATOR CONTROLS LABELLED; GAUGES WORKING; HYDRAULIC LINES PROTECTED
10	PRIMARY/REMOTE CONTROLS LABELLED & FUNCTIONING PROPERLY
11	PERSONNEL TRAINED ON HOW TO OPERATE BOP CONTROLS - TRAINING PLAN SCHEDULE AVAILABLE
12	CHOKE MANIFOLD (DEFINE SPECIFICATION)
13	VALVE(S) CHECK ON KILL LINE; VALVE(S) / HCR ON CHOKE LINE
14	CHOKE & KILL LINES SECURE & SNUBBED
15	MUD/GAS SEPARATOR - MEANS OF PREVENTING GAS BLOW-BACK INTO MUD SYSTEM, VENT LINE ADEQUATE, SECURE & PROPERLY DIRECTED
16	BLAST POINTS ON HEADERS PROTECTED BY BULL PLUGS
17	RAM TYPE BOP'S HAVE HANDWHEELS ATTACHED
18	AUTOMATIC CHOKE PANEL IN GOOD CONDITION & WORKING PROPERLY
19	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING - OVERALL CONDITION & MAINTENANCE OF WELL CONTROL EQUIPMENT

3.3.30	PIPE RAMS (UPPER AND LOWER)
1	CHECK AND INSPECT BONNET BOLT HOLES.
2	INSPECT BONNET FLANGES, BONNET SEAL GROOVES, SCRATCHES, BURRS AND OTHER DEFICIENCIES.
3	CHECK FLATNESS OF FLANGES WITH A STRAIGHT EDGE AND DEPTH GAUGE IF REQUIRED.
4	INSPECT RAM CAVITIES FOR PITS, BURRS, UPSETS, GAUGES OR OTHER DEFICIENCIES.
5	INSPECT ALL RAM BODIES FOR WEAR, GOUGES; REMOVE ANY BURRS. CHECK RAM SHAFT MOUNTING SLOTS FOR CRACKS.

3.3.31	PIPE RAMS (SHEAR)
1	CHECK AND INSPECT BONNET BOLT HOLES.
2	INSPECT BONNET FLANGES, BONNET SEAL GROOVES, SCRATCHES, BURRS AND OTHER DEFICIENCIES.
3	CHECK FLATNESS OF FLANGES WITH A STRAIGHT EDGE AND DEPTH GAUGE IF REQUIRED.
4	INSPECT RAM CAVITIES FOR PITS, BURRS, UPSETS, GAUGES OR OTHER DEFICIENCIES.
5	INSPECT ALL RAM BODIES FOR WEAR, GOUGES; REMOVE ANY BURRS. CHECK RAM SHAFT MOUNTING SLOTS FOR CRACKS.
6	INSPECT SHEAR RAM BLADES FOR NICKS AND BURRS. USE DYE PENETRANT ON SHEAR RAM BLADES FOR CRACKS.

3.3.32	BOP SPHERICAL (ANNULAR)
1	INSPECT THE BORE OF THE UPPER HOUSING, PISTON AND LOWER HOUSING FOR EXCESSIVE WEAR AND DAMAGE.
2	INSPECT THE SEALING ELEMENT FOR EXCESSIVE WEAR ON TOPSIDE ID AND OTHER ABNORMALITIES SUCH AS CRACKING, SPLITTING, GOUGING OR LUMPS TORN OUT.
3	PERFORM HYDRAULIC SYSTEM PRESSURE TEST (TEST JOINT INSERTED): CLOSING SIDE AND OPENING SIDE PRESSURES RECORDED.
4	PERFORM FUNCTION TEST - NEVER PERFORM TEST WITHOUT PIPE IN THE BORE. USE PREFERABLY A 5" DP TEST JOINT.
5	RECORD DATE OF LAST MAJOR OVERHAUL.
6	INSPECT ALL "OPEN AND CLOSE" HOSES, CONNECTIONS AND FITTINGS FOR LEAKS AND EXTERNAL DAMAGE. TIE-WRAP HOSES.
7	CHECK SPHERICAL'S ACCUMULATORS FOR CORRECT PRECHARGES (500 PSI + HYDROSTATIC HEAD), LEAKS AND EXTERNAL DAMAGE.
8	VERIFY THAT THE SPHERICAL PREVENTER IS FUNCTION PRESSURE TESTED WEEKLY (OPERATIONS PERMITTING).

3.3.33	FAILSAFE VALVE
1	INSPECT ALL TARGET FLANGES OF ALL ANGLE VALVES. CHECK CONDITION OF THE LEAD TARGETS. ALSO CHECK CAVITIES.
2	VERIFY AND RECORD FLUID QUANTITIES FOR OPEN/CLOSE FUNCTIONS.
3	RECORD DATE OF LAST MAJOR OVERHAUL.
4	VERIFY THAT CHOKE AND KILL VALVES ARE FUNCTION AND PRESSURE TESTED WEEKLY (OPERATIONS PERMITTING).
5	INSPECT ALL OPEN AND CLOSE HOSES, CONNECTIONS AND FITTINGS TO ALL VALVES FOR LEAKS AND EXTERNAL DAMAGE.

3.3.34	BOP CONTROL SYSTEM
1	ENSURE ALL PANEL LIGHTS ARE OPERATIONAL.
2	ENSURE FLOW COUNTER IS OPERATIONAL.
3	FUNCTION TEST THE FOLLOWING ALARMS: LOW MIXED FLUID WARNING, LOW RIG AIR PRESSURE, ACCUMULATOR LOW PRESSURE, AC POWER LOSS WARNING.
4	CHECK DRILLERS AUDIBLE ALARM.
5	TEST VARIOUS FUNCTIONS [WHEN POSSIBLE] TO CONFIRM OPERATION.

3.3.35	ACCUMULATOR UNIT
1	VISUALLY INSPECT ALL PRESSURE GAUGES FOR DAMAGE OR INCORRECT READING.
2	INSPECT ALL PIPING, VALVES AND FITTINGS FOR LEAKS OR DETERIORATION.
3	INSPECT ALL ACCUMULATOR PUMPS AND OPERATION OF PRESSURE SWITCHES.

3.3.36	BOP CONTROL SYSTEM (ACCUMULATOR BOTTLES)
1	RECORD DATE OF LAST PRE-CHARGE CHECK.
2	RECORD DATE OF LAST INSPECTION.

3.3.37	TRIPLEX PUMP
1	CHECK CHAIN DEFLECTION FOR 1" MAXIMUM SLACK.
2	CHECK ALIGNMENT OF CHAIN DRIVE SPROCKETS.
3	CHECK FLUID LEVEL CONTROL SWITCHES.
4	CHECK TWO WAY AND FOUR WAY MANIPULATOR VALVES FOR PROPER OPERATION.
5	SET HIGH AND LOW LIMIT PRESSURE CONTROL SWITCHES TO PROPER SETTINGS.

3.3.38	BOP BRIDGE CRANE
1	CHECK HOISTING WIRE ROPES FOR BROKEN WIRES, ABRASIONS OR KINKS OR EVIDENCE OF NOT SPOOLING PROPERLY ON DRUM.
2	CHECK CRANE HOOK FOR DEFORMATION, CRACKS AND WEAR. CARRY OUT MAGNETIC PARTICLE INSPECTION FOR CRACK DETECTION.
3	CHECK SHEAVES FOR WEAR AND CRACKS.
4	CHECK HIGH PRESSURE HOSES AND PIPING FOR LEAKS, CHAFING, WEAR AND AGE.
5	CHECK AGAINST RECORDS FOR CURRENT CERTIFICATION OF CRANE.
6	CHECK BRIDGE RUNWAY RACK FOR DEFECTS.
7	CHECK FOR WORN OR DISTORTED MECHANICAL PARTS SUCH AS SHAFTS, BEARINGS, PINS, WHEELS, ROLLERS, GEAR AND PINION.
8	CHECK THAT MULTIPLE UNIT VALVE SPOOLS RETURN TO NEUTRAL AUTOMATICALLY.
9	CHECK OPERATION OF CONTROLS FOR INTENDED MOVEMENT OF EACH OF THE CRANE MOTIONS.
NOTE: CRANES WITH IDENTIFIED SAFETY HAZARDS SHOULD BE REMOVED FROM SERVICE UNTIL REPAIRS ARE COMPLETED.

3.3.39	TEST STUMP
1	INSPECT HOSES, FITTINGS, CONNECTIONS AND HYDRAULIC CYLINDER ASSEMBLY FOR LEAKS AND EXTERNAL DAMAGE.
2	FUNCTION TEST HYDRAULIC CYLINDER ASSEMBLY.
3	ENSURE TEST STUMP IS PROPERLY SECURED TO CYLINDER ASSEMBLY.

3.3.40	REMOTE CHOKE PANEL
1	CHECK HYDRAULIC AND PNEUMATIC SYSTEMS FOR LEAKS AND DAMAGE.
2	CHECK OIL LEVEL IN HYDRAULIC RESERVOIR.
3	CHECK ALL GAUGES FOR DAMAGE.
4	VERIFY THAT STROKE COUNTER IS OPERATING CORRECTLY.
5	FUNCTION TEST AND VERIFY THAT THE MANUAL CHOKE POSITIONER, MANUAL SELECTOR AND RELATIVE CHOKE POSITION INDICATOR GAUGE ARE OPERATING CORRECTLY.
6	FUNCTION TEST THE MAXIMUM ALLOWABLE SYSTEM AND CHECK FOR FAULTS.

3.3.41	TRIP TANK PUMP
1	RECORD TOTAL RUN HOURS.
2	REMOVE COUPLING GUARD AND CHECK FOR MISALIGNMENT USING A STRAIGHT EDGE AND FEELERS. MAXIMUM FOR BOTH RADIAL AND AXIAL IS 1/64".
3	HAVE DRILLING DEPARTMENT START AND RUN PUMP, CHECK FOR UNUSUAL NOISE, LEAKS, VIBRATION OR OVERHEATING.
4	IF POSSIBLE, RECORD MOTOR AMPS WHILE CIRCULATING SEA WATER - COMPARE WITH PREVIOUS READINGS.

3.3.42	MUD PIT VOLUME TOTALIZER
1	VISUALLY INSPECT ALL SENSORS FOR DETERIORATION OR DAMAGE.
2	CHECK ALL SENSORS FROM INSTRUMENT PANEL. HAVE DRILLER/DERRICKMAN CONFIRM ACCURATE CALIBRATION.
3	CHECK TRIP TANK SENSOR AND RECORD (IF APPLICABLE). ENSURE ACCURATE CALIBRATION.

3.3.43	STRIPPING TANK PUMP
1	HAVE DRILLING DEPARTMENT START AND RUN PUMP, CHECK FOR UNUSUAL NOISE, LEAKS, VIBRATION OR OVERHEATING.

3.4	POWER SYSTEM

3.4.1	MAIN ENGINE
1	CHECK RECORDS OF STOP PISTON RING LAND CLEARANCE.
2	CARRY OUT AIRBOX INSPECTION (ON AT LEAST ONE ENGINE). CHECK PM RECORDS FOR OTHERS.
3	CHECK GOVERNOR CONTROL LINKAGE FOR BINDING OR EXCESS PLAY.
4	USE A FLASHLIGHT TO THOROUGHLY INSPECT THE TRAP BOX FOR DEBRIS.
5	CHECK OPERATION OF CRANKCASE PRESSURE DETECTOR.
6	LAST 4200 HOUR INSPECTION RECORDED FOR ALL ENGINES.
7	CHECK HIGH COOLING TEMPERATURE ALARM.
8	CHECK HIGH OIL TEMPERATURE ALARM.
9	CHECK LOW LUBE OIL PRESSURE SHUT DOWN.
10	CHECK "IN SERVICE" HOURS OF MAJOR COMPONENTS WITH REPLACEMENT/INSPECTION RECOMMENDATIONS.
11	CHECK RESULTS OF LAST OIL ANALYSIS - ALL ENGINES.

3.4.2	AC GENERATORS
1	CHECK VENTILATION DUCTS AND PASSAGE FOR OBSTRUCTION AND BUILDUP OF DIRT; VISUALLY INSPECT COOLING FAN BLADES.
2	CHECK INSULATION OF THE STATOR AND REVOLVING FIELDS FOR EVIDENCE OF OVERHEATING, CRACKED OR BRITTLE INSULATION.
3	CHECK THE STATOR AND REVOLVING FIELDS FOR EVIDENCE OF MOVEMENT BY INSPECTING WEDGES, FILLERS, TIES AND TAPE SEPARATION.
4	BEARING INSULATING SLEEVE - MEASURE WITH A MILLIVOLT METER BETWEEN THE BEARING HOUSING AND GENERATOR SHAFT.
5	MEGGER TEST (1000 V) GENERATOR STATOR; RECORD READING TAKEN 10 SECONDS AFTER STARTING THE CHECK AND AGAIN AFTER 5 MINUTES. THE RATIO OF INSULATION RESISTANCE AT 5 MINUTES SHOULD NOT BE LESS THAN 1-1/2 TIMES THE 10 SECOND READING.
6	MEGGER TEST GENERATOR REVOLVING FIELD (INTERCONNECTED) (TEST VOLTAGE 500 V). IF READING IS BELOW ONE MEGOHM, SEPARATE AND TEST SEPARATELY.
7	OPEN AND INSPECT CONTROL PANEL AND J-BOXES. INSPECT ALL MAIN AND CONTROL TERMINAL BOARD CONNECTIONS, RELAYS, STARTERS AND WIRING FOR DEFECTS AND TIGHTNESS.
8	HIGH VOLTAGE SIGNS VISIBLE
9	HEARING PROTECTION AVAILABLE & SIGNS VISIBLE
10	FIRE EXTINGUISHERS
11	NON-CONDUCTIVE MATERIALS IN PLACE
12	ALL SWITCHES LABELLED
13	WIRING RECEPTACLES & LIGHTING IN GOOD ORDER
14	SWITCHES & CONNECTOR BOXES HAVE COVERS IN PLACE
15	GENERATOR GROUNDED
16	DOORS PROPERLY PROPPED & SPINNED
17	ALL MOVING MACHINERY PROPERLY GUARDED
18	LOCK-OUT PROCEDURE INCORPORATED & USED
19	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING - NO OIL IN FLOOR; COMBUSTIBLES CONTAINED OR ELIMINATED
20	LOCAL & REMOTE EMERGENCY STOP BUTTONS FUNCTION TESTED (DATE OF LAST TEST)

3.4.3	MAIN SWITCHBOARD - INSTRUMENTS
1	CHECK SWITCHBOARD INSTRUMENTS AND DETERMINE IF VOLTMETERS, AMMETERS, WATTMETERS, VARMETERS, POWER FACTOR METERS ETC ARE OPERATIONAL AND ACCURATE.

3.4.4	MAIN SWITCHBOARD - PROTECTIVE RELAYS
1	CHECK PM RECORDS TO ENSURE ALL PROTECTIVE DEVICES HAVE BEEN TESTED DURING THE LAST YEAR.
2	RANDOMLY CHECK SAFETY DEVICES WHERE POSSIBLE: REVERSE POWER, OVERCURRENT, UNDERVOLTAGE, UNDER/OVER FREQUENCY, TEMPERATURE.

3.4.5	MAIN SWITCHBOARD
1	INSPECT MAIN BUS-BARS AND INSPECT CABINETS WHERE POSSIBLE.
2	SURVEYOR SHOULD SATISFY HIMSELF THAT COMPARTMENTS ARE CLEAN AND HOUSEKEEPING IS IN ORDER.
3	ALL CONTROL FUSE INDICATORS SHOULD BE CHECKED TO ENSURE INTEGRITY OF DEVICES.
4	HAVE CHIEF ELECTRICIAN REMOVE 3 OR 4 CIRCUIT BREAKERS AND INSPECT CONDITION OF ARC CHUTES, STATIONARY AND MOVING CONTACTS.
5	WHERE APPLICABLE, CHECK VACUUM BREAKER CONTACT AIR GAPS.
6	CHECK ALL SWITCHBOARD CELLS ARE COCKED AND ONLY ABLE TO BE OPENED USING SPECIAL TOOLS/KEYS.
7	RECORD DATE OF LAST INFRA RED SURVEY (ANNUAL INSPECTION).
8	RECORD DATE OF LAST CONDUCTOR TEST ON MAIN BUSSBAR.

3.4.6	480 V SWITCHBOARD
1	INSPECT ALL TERMINAL AND CONNECTION HARDWARE FOR TIGHTNESS. KEEP INSULATION FREE OF DIRT, PAINT OR OTHER CONDUCTING MATERIAL.
2	INSPECT CONTROL AND FEEDER WIRING FOR FRAYING AND DETERIORATION.
3	INSPECT CONTROL AND METERING TRANSFORMERS FOR DAMAGED INSULATION AND TIGHTNESS. REPAIR ANY FAULTS FOUND.
4	CHECK ALL BREAKER TRIP DEVICES FOR GENERAL CONDITION.
5	HAVE CHIEF ELECTRICIAN REMOVE 1 OR 2 CIRCUIT BREAKERS AND INSPECT CONDITION OF ARC CHUTES, STATIONARY AND MOVING CONTACTS. CHECK INSULATOR RESISTANCE BETWEEN CB POLES.

3.4.7	MOTOR CONTROL CENTERS
1	INSPECT MAIN INCOMING POWER CABLE AND CONNECTING LUGS FOR DEFECTIVE CONNECTIONS AND GENERAL CONDITION.
2	MEGGER TEST POWER CABLE AND BUS TO GROUND (1000 V), RECORD AS FOUND.
3	INSPECT ALL MAIN AND CONTROL TERMINAL BOARD CONNECTIONS AND WIRING FOR DEFECTS AND TIGHTNESS.
4	CHECK ALL MAGNETIC LINE STARTERS FOR LOOSE, MISSING, BROKEN OR CORRODED HARDWARE.
5	RANDOMLY INSPECT MOVING AND STATIONARY CONTACTS.
6	CHECK ARC BOXES FOR EXCESSIVE COLLECTION OF CONTACT MATERIAL OR OTHER FOREIGN MATERIAL.
7	CHECK RECORDS OF INSULATION RESISTANCE TEST TO GROUND (1000 V) OF EACH MOTOR CONTROLLED FROM THE MCC.
8	CHECK RECORDS OF RESISTANCE BETWEEN MOTOR HOUSING(S) AND SOLID HULL GROUND; RECORD IF MORE THAN 1 OHM.
9	CHECK ALL UNIT INDICATING LIGHTS AND REPLACE AS REQUIRED.
10	RANDOMLY CHECK BEARINGS OF LISTED MOTORS (WHILE RUNNING) FOR EXCESSIVE NOISE, VIBRATION AND HEAT.
11	RECORD DATE OF LAST OVERLOAD SURVEY (ONCE EVERY TWO YEARS).

3.4.8	HAZARDOUS AREAS ENCLOSURES
1	CHECK HAZARDOUS AREA ENCLOSURE REGISTER AND ENSURE THAT ANNUAL INSPECTION IS BEING CARRIED OUT.

3.4.9	480 VOLT POWER DISTRIBUTION PANELS
1	IF POSSIBLE, INSPECT CONDITION OF PANEL AND ASSOCIATED WIRING WITH POWER OFF. IF IT IS IMPOSSIBLE TO ISOLATE, INSPECT WITH PANEL LIVE - ALL NECESSARY SAFETY PRECAUTIONS TAKEN.
2	CHECK ENCLOSURE FOR LOOSE OR DAMAGED WIRING AND CONNECTIONS.
3	ENSURE ALL TRUNKS ARE IN PLACE, WIRING IS SECURED AND PROTECTED.
4	ENSURE PANEL DOOR LOCKING MECHANISM OR SCREWS ARE IN GOOD CONDITION AND PREVENT PANEL FROM BEING OPENED BY UNAUTHORIZED PERSONNEL.

3.4.10	220 VOLT POWER DISTRIBUTION PANELS
1	IF POSSIBLE, INSPECT CONDITION OF PANEL AND ASSOCIATED WIRING WITH POWER OFF.
2	CHECK ENCLOSURE FOR LOOSE OR DAMAGED WIRING AND CONNECTIONS.
3	ENSURE ALL TRUNKING IS IN PLACE, WIRING IS SECURED AND PROTECTED.
4	ENSURE PANEL DOOR LOCKING MECHANISM OR SCREWS ARE IN GOOD CONDITION.

3.4.11	110 VOLT POWER DISTRIBUTION PANELS
1	IF POSSIBLE, INSPECT CONDITION OF PANEL AND ASSOCIATED WIRING WITH POWER OFF.
2	CHECK ENCLOSURE FOR LOOSE OR DAMAGED WIRING AND CONNECTIONS.
3	ENSURE ALL TRUNKING IS IN PLACE, WIRING IS SECURED AND PROTECTED.
4	ENSURE PANEL DOOR LOCKING MECHANISM OR SCREWS ARE IN GOOD CONDITION.

3.4.12	TRANSFER PANEL
1	INSPECT THE ROTARY/TOPDRIVE TRANSFER PANEL FOR DAMAGED METERS AND INDICATOR LIGHTS ON DRILLERS CONSOLE PANEL.
2	OBSERVE CORRECT CYCLING OF PURGE SYSTEM FOR TRANSFER PANEL AND INDICATOR LIGHTS ON DRILLERS CONSOLE PANEL.

3.4.13	SCR SYSTEM
1	CHECK THE FOLLOWING LED STATUS/ALARM INDICATORS: BAY NUMBER, SCR AVAILABLE, SCR ENABLE, FUSE FAILURE, OVERCURRENT, CURRENT LIMIT, HIGH TEMPERATURE.
2	VISUALLY INSPECT PC BOARDS FOR SIGNS OF COMPONENT DETERIORATION.

3.4.14	SCR BAYS
1	CHECK CABINET DOOR HINGE AND LOCKS FOR DAMAGE.
2	CHECK BRIDGE ASSEMBLIES.
3	EXAMINE BAY FOR BAD CONNECTIONS, LOOSE COMPONENTS AND DISCOLORED CONNECTIONS.
4	EXAMINE BREAKER (CB) CUBICLE FOR LOOSE CONNECTIONS.
5	EXAMINE AUXILIARY MOTOR CONTROL BREAKERS, CONTACTORS AND OVERLOAD RELAYS.
6	CHECK SCR COOLING MOTOR FOR EXCESSIVE NOISE.

3.4.15	TRANSFORMERS
1	INSPECT FOR ACCUMULATION OF DIRT ON INSULATING SURFACES AND AIRFLOW PASSAGEWAYS, FOR LOOSE CONNECTIONS AND FOR GENERAL CONDITION. OBSERVE FOR SIGNS OF OVERHEATING AND VOLTAGE CREEPAGE.
2	CHECK FOR LOOSE IRON, LOOSE COIL BLOCKING AND CRACKED OR BROKEN INSULATORS.
3	IF POSSIBLE, PERFORM INSULATION RESISTANCE TESTS: HV WINDING TO GROUND, LV WINDING TO GROUND, AND BOTH WINDINGS TO GROUND. RECORD AS FOUND.
4	CHECK AND RECORD TRANSFORMER TEMPERATURES FOR REFERENCE. A MINIMUM OF 8 MEGOHMS (ONE MINUTE READING AT 25 DEG C) IS THE LOWEST ACCEPTABLE READING.
5	REFER TO PM RECORDS FOR MOST RECENT I.R. MEASUREMENT IF ABOVE CANNOT BE PERFORMED.

3.4.16	UPS, BATTERY CHARGERS AND BATTERIES
1	INSPECT CIRCUIT BOARD PARTS FOR EVIDENCE OF OVERHEATING AND PHYSICAL DAMAGE.
2	INSPECT FRONT PANEL MOUNTED COMPONENTS FOR LOOSE OR BROKEN CONNECTIONS.
3	INSPECT TRANSFORMERS FOR EVIDENCE OF OVERHEATING SUCH AS DISCOLORED INSULATION.
4	INSPECT POWER TERMINAL BLOCKS FOR TIGHTNESS, CRACKED INSULATION OR LOOSE CONNECTIONS.
5	INSPECT BATTERIES, TAKE SPECIFIC GRAVITY READING (IF POSSIBLE) AND RECORD.
6	SWITCH OFF AC INPUT POWER AND OBSERVE OPERATION OF UPS OR BACK UP BATTERIES.

3.4.17	L.P. COMPRESSOR
1	CHECK AND RECORD: OIL TEMPERATURE, AIR PRESSURE, PERCENTAGE LOAD, OPERATION OF CONDENSATE DUMP, LOAD/UNLOAD OPERATION, RESULTS OF LAST OIL ANALYSIS REPORT, DATE OF RELIEF VALVE CERTIFICATION.

3.4.18	COMPRESSOR (COLD START)
1	CHECK PULLEY ALIGNMENT AND V-BELTS FOR CORRECT TENSION AND WEAR.
2	START UP THE COMPRESSOR AND CHECK FOR PROPER LOADING/UNLOADING.

3.4.19	DIESEL ENGINE - COLD START COMPRESSOR
1	INSPECT ENGINE GUARDS FOR LOOSE BOLTS OR DAMAGE.
2	INSPECT ENGINE EXHAUST SYSTEM FOR LEAKS AND DAMAGE DUE TO VIBRATION; ENSURE SYSTEM IS WELL SUPPORTED; NO FLAMMABLE MATERIALS NEARBY.
3	START ENGINE, ENGAGE CLUTCH AND CHECK FOR PROPER LOADING AND UNLOADING.

3.4.20	AIR DRYER
1	CHECK OPERATING CONDITIONS: INLET FLOW, INLET PRESSURE, TEMPERATURE AND PURGE RATES FOR BOTH TOWERS.
2	CHECK PREFILTER AND AFTERFILTER (IF PRESSURE DROP EXCEEDS 10-15 PSI REPLACE CARTRIDGES).
3	CHECK OPERATION OF AUTOMATIC DRAIN VALVES.
4	CHECK SYSTEM FOR LEAKS.
5	CHECK THAT PRESSURIZATION VALVE OPERATION IS SATISFACTORY.
6	CHECK CONTROL CIRCUIT TIMER AND SOLENOID OPERATION.
7	CHECK DATE OF LAST DESICCANT CHANGE.
8	CHECK DATE OF RELIEF VALVE CERTIFICATION.
9	CHECK OUTLET DEWPOINT AND RECORD.

3.4.21	PURGE AIR SYSTEM
1	CHECK THAT AIR IS OF SUITABLE QUALITY - OPEN INSTRUMENT ENCLOSURES AT RANDOM, CHECK FOR ANY MOISTURE/OIL.
2	CHECK FOR CORRECT AIR PURGE PRESSURE.

3.4.22	CENTRIFUGAL PUMP
1	RUN PUMP, CHECK MOTOR AMPS AND RECORD.
2	IF CIRCULATING SALT WATER, THEN CHECK DISCHARGE PRESSURE AND RECORD.
3	CHECK FOR ANY LEAKS, VIBRATION OR OVERHEATING.
4	RECORD DATE OF LAST MAJOR OVERHAUL.

3.4.23	FUEL OIL TANK
1	INSPECT FUEL TANK VENT FLAME ARRESTOR SCREENS FOR THEIR CONDITION. ENSURE CONTAINMENT WELLS BELOW THE VENTS ARE EMPTY AND DRAIN PLUGS ARE IN PLACE.
2	CHECK LAST ANALYSIS REPORT ON TANK CONTENTS.

3.4.24	FUEL CENTRIFUGE
1	INSPECT ENTIRE CENTRIFUGE SYSTEM FOR ABNORMAL CONDITIONS.

3.4.25	FUEL STRAINER
1	CARRY OUT CHECKS ON THE MANUAL VALVES ASSOCIATED WITH THE STRAINER AND LOADING SYSTEM.
2	CHECK HANDLE, STEM AND BODY FOR DAMAGE OR CORROSION.
3	CHECK THE STEM PACKING (IF APPLICABLE) FOR LEAKS. ENSURE THE PACKING DOES NOT INHIBIT MOVEMENT OF THE VALVE STEM.
4	OPERATE THE VALVE TO THE FULLY OPEN/CLOSE POSITION IF POSSIBLE.
5	CHECK THAT THE LEGEND PLATE IS CLEAN AND LEGIBLE.

3.4.26	OIL TRANSFER PUMP
1	TEST RUN PUMP AND CHECK FOR VIBRATION, OVERHEATING OR LEAKS.
2	CHECK MOTOR AMPS.

3.4.27	STEAM GENERATOR
1	TEST ALL SAFETY SHUT DOWNS, AUXILIARY THERMOSTAT, BURNER SAFETY CONTROL, MAIN THERMOSTAT AND PUMP LEVEL SHUT DOWN. CHECK RELIEF VALVE CERTIFICATION.
2	START UNIT AND CARRY OUT CHECKS.
3	CHECK FOR ANY OIL, FUEL OR WATER LEAKS AT THE UNIT.
4	CHECK FOR EXCESSIVE STACK SMOKE.
5	RECORD STEAM DISCHARGE PRESSURE AND TEMPERATURE, FEED PRESSURE, FUEL PRESSURE.
6	SHUT DOWN UNIT (DRY SHUT DOWN METHOD) AND CARRY OUT ISOLATION PROCEDURE.
7	EXERCISE ALL REMOTE FUEL SHUT OFF VALVES AND ALL STEAM STOP VALVES.

3.4.28	HOT WATER BOILER
1	RUN BOILER AND OBSERVE LOW FIRE, HIGH FIRE AND MODULATION IN 'AUTOMATIC' OPERATION. CHECK FOR EXCESSIVE STACK SMOKE.
2	CHECK CONDITION OF BOILER REFRACTORY LINING.
3	CHECK THE OPERATION OF THE 'LOW WATER SHUTDOWN'.
4	CHECK RELIEF VALVE CERTIFICATION.
5	CHECK FOR FUEL LEAKS.
6	CHECK INHIBITOR CONCENTRATION OF BOILER WATER.

3.4.29	FUEL TANKS / FUEL TRANSFER
1	CONTENTS LABELLED - LABELS "FIRE HAZARD", "NO SMOKING", "NO HOT WORK" UNDERSTOOD
2	TANKS VENTED - VENTS OPEN
3	FIRE EXTINGUISHERS PROVIDED - WITHIN EASY REACH / SUITABLE TYPE
4	FUEL TANKS SAFELY LOCATED
5	FUEL TRANSFER EQUIPMENT IN GOOD CONDITION - STATIC GROUND DEVICE; "DEADMAN CONTROL NOZZLE"
6	FUEL TRANSFER PROCEDURES POSTED & ENFORCED
7	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING - NO VISIBLE LEAKS
8	SPILL PREVENTION EQUIPMENT PROVIDED; CURBS, DRIP PANS, SUMPS
9	LADDER SECURED & IN GOOD CONDITION
10	PRESSURISED TANKS RATED FOR PRESSURE (CERTIFICATION EXPIRY DATE)
11	OVERFILL PROVISIONS (WHAT HAPPENS TO SPILL)
12	FLAMMABLE LIQUIDS STORED PROPERLY & LABELLED, AWAY FROM HEAT, OXYGEN BOTTLES, OXIDISING AGENTS
13	FIRE EXTINGUISHERS PROVIDED - AVAILABLE NEARBY
14	"NO SMOKING" SIGNS - CLEARLY VISIBLE
15	ITEMS PREVENTED FROM FALLING - TOE PLATE, CAGES, OTHERWISE SECURED
16	SUPPLIES STACKED NEATLY ON SHELVES
17	WIRING AND OUTLETS IN GOOD ORDER
18	RUBBISH CONTAINERS; COVERED FOR OIL SATURATED RAGS
19	WALKWAYS CLEAN
20	COMBUSTIBLES AWAY FROM LIGHT FIXTURES - KEPT SEPARATE & CLEARLY LABELLED / HAZARD SIGNS

3.4.30	STORAGE AREAS
1	PAINT & PAINT THINNER CONTAINERS COVERED
2	EXPLOSION PROOF LIGHTING & VENTILATION PROVIDED FOR PAINT STORAGE
3	SAFETY CANS AVAILABLE FOR TRANSPORTING OF PAINT THINNER
4	SPILL PREVENTION EQUIPMENT PROVIDED; CURBS, DRIP PANS, SUMPS
5	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING

3.5	HOISTING & ROTATING SYSTEM

3.5.1	GENERAL
1	DERRICK PINS & KEEPERS - ALL IN PLACE AND IN GOOD CONDITION
2	ESCAPE LINE WITH GERONIMO - PROPERLY RIGGED
3	CLIMBING ASSIST INSTALLED, ADJUSTED AND USED
4	SAFETY BELTS AND LINES IN GOOD CONDITION
5	DERRICK LADDER SAFE TO USE - NO RUNGS BENT OR MISSING
6	PIPE FINGERS SNUBBED WITH SAFETY CHAIN
7	ADEQUATE DERRICK LIGHTING - EXPLOSION PROOF, SECURED
8	SILLS / BUMPER BOARD TO PREVENT RUNNING INTO CROWN
9	CROWN BLOCK REGULARLY MAINTAINED
10	FLASHING RED LIGHT IN CROWN
11	ANY TOOLS USED IN DERRICK PROPERLY SECURED
12	STAND PIPE SECURED; MUD HOSE SNUBBED AT BOTH ENDS
13	ALL WIRE ROPE IN GOOD CONDITION; NO WIRE ROPE RUBBING AGAINST STRUCTURAL MEMBERS
14	HYDRAULIC SYSTEM & CYLINDERS - NO LEAKS VISIBLE
15	MANUAL LATCH DOGS
16	CONDITION OF DERRICK (CRACKS / CORROSION)
17	GAS BURNER VENT LINE - ROUTED TO A POSITION COMMENSURATE WITH MAXIMUM DILUTION, SECURELY STAKED
18	PROPERLY GUYED, ANCHORED & TENSIONED - GUY WIRES IN GOOD CONDITION

3.5.2	DRAWWORKS & HOISTING GEAR
1	"CROWN-O-MATIC" PROPERLY INSTALLED; SETTING MAINTAINED; CHECKED EACH TRIP (RECORDED IN SHIFT REPORT); RESET AFTER SLIP/CUT.
2	BAND BRAKE MECHANISM - FOLLOW MANUFACTURER RECOMMENDED PROCEDURES.
3	CONDITION OF ALL COTTER PINS, SNAP RINGS, BRAKE SHAFTS, EQUALIZER AND LINKAGE PIVOTS INCLUDING ALL BUSHES AND PINS FOR WEAR AND DAMAGE.
4	MAIN DRUM FLANGE WEAR (MANUFACTURERS SPECIFICATIONS) - RECORD AS FOUND ON BOTH SIDES.
5	MAKE UP AND BREAK DOWN CATHEADS CORRECTLY OPERATING.
6	CHECK THE WELD AREAS WHICH JOIN THE BAND END LUGS TO THE BAND (DYE PENETRANT NDT). ALSO CHECK BAND BRAKE EYE BOLTS, RETAINING RINGS, BRAKE SHAFT LEVER PIN AND CONTROL LEVER.
7	PHYSICAL CONDITION OF DRAWWORKS OIL - CHECK LAST ANALYSIS REPORT.
8	DRILL LINE IN GOOD CONDITION; TON MILEAGE UP TO DATE; SLIP / CUT AS REQUIRED (DATE LAST VISUALLY INSPECTED).
9	MPI DEAD LINE ANCHOR FRAME BASE AND ALL PIN AREAS (WEIGHT SENSOR SHEAVE PIN). CHECK PREVIOUS REPORTS.
10	ALL BOLTS IN PLACE & TIGHTENED ON DRILL LINE ANCHOR.
11	BRAKES IN GOOD CONDITION; ADJUSTED & WORKING PROPERLY (LAST INSPECTION & ADJUSTMENT RECORDED).
12	WEIGHT INDICATOR FUNCTIONING CORRECTLY WITH COMPATIBLE UNITS.
13	TRAVELLING BLOCK & HOOK IN GOOD CONDITION, REGULARLY MAINTAINED (DATE LAST NDT TESTED / OVERHAULED).
14	OVERALL MAINTENANCE & APPEARANCE OF DRAWWORKS.
15	SPINNING CHAIN (IF USED, CHECK FOR DEFECTS / DAMAGE).
16	AFTER TASK HAS BEEN COMPLETED, RUN DRAWWORKS AND CHECK AUXILIARY LUBE OIL PUMP PRESSURE AND CHECK FOR VIBRATION AND NOISE.
17	ELEVATORS, BAILS (DATE OF LAST MPI CRACK DETECTION TEST).

3.5.3	AUXILIARY BRAKE
1	GOOD CONDITION OF FLAME PLATE.
2	MEASURE AIR GAP OF COIL ASSEMBLY (WITH FEELER GAUGE) AT THE THREE INSPECTION POINTS (PORT SIDE - STBD SIDE). ENSURE AIR GAPS ARE CLEAN AND UNOBSTRUCTED.
3	CHECK OPERATION OF BOTH FLOW METERS (MINIMUM FLOW 300 LT/MIN, CLEAN GLASS COVER, FLOW ALARM FUNCTIONAL).
4	DISCONNECT BRAKE COIL LEADS AND MEASURE AND RECORD RESISTANCE OF ALL COILS. OBSERVE LEADS MARKINGS FOR CORRECT INTER-MAGNET POLARITY.
5	WHILE LEADS ARE DISCONNECTED, CARRY OUT A RESISTANCE READING TO THE GROUND OF ALL COILS (NOTIFY WHEN READING IS BELOW 0.5 MEGOHM).
6	PLACE DRILLERS CONTROL TO "OFF" POSITION. CONNECT DVM POWER TERMINAL STRIP AND RECORD VOLTAGE (SHOULD BE ZERO).
7	WITH DRILLERS CONTROL TO FULL "ON" POSITION REPEAT READING AND RECORD (NOT LESS THAN 225 VOLTS).
8	CHECK EMERGENCY BRAKE BATTERY BACK UP SYSTEM: SWITCH OFF MAIN AC POWER TO BRAKE; VERIFY DRILLER GOT AUDIBLE ALARM WARNING AND BRAKE OPERATED ON EMERGENCY POWER.
9	CHECK MAINTENANCE HISTORY FOR LATEST ALIGNMENT READINGS (AXIAL AND RADIAL).
10	RAISE AND LOWER THE BLOCKS AND CHECK FOR ABNORMAL NOISE OR VIBRATION.

3.5.4	DRAWWORKS MOTORS
1	CHECK COMMUTATOR SURFACE FOR HEAVY FILM, STREAKING, THREADING, GROOVING, COPPER DRAG, PITCH BAR MARKING, SLOT BAR MARKING, HIGH MICA AND FLAT SPOTS.
2	TAKE A MEGGER TEST OF MOTOR FIELD AND ARMATURE. RECORD AS FOUND FOR BOTH MOTORS.
3	WHILE MOTOR IS OPERATIONAL CHECK MOTOR BEARINGS FOR EXCESSIVE NOISE AND TEMPERATURE.

3.5.5	ROTARY TABLE
1	CHECK MAIN SKID HOLD DOWN BOLTS ARE SECURE. IF NOT TORQUE TO 955 FT/LBS TOTAL 4 EA.
2	CHECK TABLE LOCK PAWL FOR PROPER ENGAGEMENT/DISENGAGEMENT (WHEN TOP DRIVE IS USED FOR DRILLING - THE ROTARY LOCK PAWL SHOULD BE ENGAGED).
3	CHECK PHYSICAL CONDITION OF ROTARY AND TRANSMISSION OIL.
4	HAVE DRILLER OPERATE ROTARY AT VARIOUS SPEEDS - CHECK FOR ANY UNUSUAL NOISE/VIBRATION.
5	TAKE A MEASUREMENT OF THE ROTARY TABLE BEARING CLEARANCE. THE LOCATIONS ARE STAMPED UNDER THE ROTARY COVER. RECORD.

3.5.6	ROTARY DRILLING UNIT
1	CHECK FRICTION SHOES ON AIR BRAKE. REPLACE IF WORN DOWN TO A THICKNESS OF 5/32".

3.5.7	RIG FLOOR - CATHEADS / HOISTS
1	CATHEADS SMOOTH, UNGROOVED, GUARDED; ROPE DIVIDER IN GOOD CONDITION; EMERGENCY STOP EFFECTIVE & USED
2	CATLINE IN GOOD CONDITION; TIED BACK SAFELY; PROPERLY STORED
3	AUTOMATIC CATHEAD IN PROPER WORKING ORDER
4	HEADACHE POST IN GOOD CONDITION & PROPERLY SECURED
5	AIR HOIST LINE IN GOOD CONDITION; PROPERLY SPOOLED WITH LINE GUIDE; GUARDED ANCHORED
6	HOOKS IN GOOD CONDITION
7	WIRE ROPE LOOPS SECURED WITH 3 CLAMPS OR PRESSED FITTINGS; CLAMPS ON DEAD END
8	OVERALL CONDITION OF ALL ROPE, WIRE ROPE, CHAINS, SLINGS & HOOKS USED FOR LIFTING
9	MAN-RIDING WINCH - FAIL SAFE BRAKE FITTED, ADEQUATE PROCEDURES

3.5.8	KELLY BUSHINGS
1	CHECK MAINTENANCE RECORDS - MOST UNITS ARE NOT IN USE BUT IF ON BOARD, MAINTENANCE SHOULD BE PERFORMED.

3.5.9	MASTER BUSHING
1	CHECK THE BOWL LOCK ASSEMBLY FOR DEFECTS AND INSURE IT FUNCTIONS PROPERLY.
2	CHECK TAPER OF THE BOWL WITH A STRAIGHT-EDGE. BE SURE BOWLS DO NOT HAVE TWO DIFFERENT TAPERS.
3	TAKE MEASUREMENTS OF THE BOWL AND RECORD ON THE SHEET.
4	TAKE MEASUREMENTS OF THE MASTER BUSHING WITH GUIDE LINES AND RECORD. REFER TO MANUFACTURERS MANUAL FOR DIMENSIONS.
5	DYE PENETRANT INSPECT (DPI) PINS, BOWL LOCKS, BOWL AREA, THROAT AND DRIVE PIN HOLE AREAS FOR CRACKS. IF CRACKING EXCEEDS RECOMMENDED DEPTH, RETURN TO PROPER FACILITY FOR EVALUATION.

3.5.10	ROTARY TABLE MOTOR
1	CHECK COMMUTATOR SURFACE FOR FILM, STRIPPING, BAR BURNING, HIGH AND LOW BARS, HIGH MICA AND FLAT SPOTS.
2	TAKE A FINAL MEGGER TEST OF MOTOR FIELD AND ARMATURE, RECORD AS FOUND.
3	CHECK DUCTING FOR AIR LEAKS OR DAMAGE.
4	MEGGER TEST AUXILIARY MOTORS AND RECORD READINGS (RT BLOWER, RT OIL CIRC PUMP).

3.5.11	TOP DRIVE
1	CHECK DATE OF LAST JARRING ACTIVITY - ENSURE THAT AFTER JARRING PREVENTIVE MAINTENANCE WAS CARRIED OUT.
2	CHECK THE REPORT OF THE LAST THIRD PARTY INSPECTION FOR MPI OR UT OF TOP DRIVE COMPONENTS - ENSURE ALL NON CONFORMANCES ADDRESSED.
3	ENSURE ALL PROTECTIVE GUARDS ON THE TOP DRIVE ARE INTACT AND PROPERLY SECURED. VISUALLY INSPECT AND CONFIRM ALL BOLT SAFETY WIRES ARE INTACT.
4	INSPECT LINK TILT ASSEMBLY. CONFIRM ALL BOLTS ARE INTACT, TIGHT AND SAFELY WIRED. INSPECT BLADDER FOR DAMAGE, CHECK INTERMEDIATE STOPS.
5	VISUALLY INSPECT TORQUE ARRESTOR SECURING PINS TO ROTATING HEADS. INSPECT SINGLE POINT SECURING PIN AND PAD-EYE ON PIPE HANDLER HANGER.
6	CHECK DC MOTOR 'MAKE-UP' AMPS/TORQUE AGAINST BACK-UP TONG TORQUE GAUGE.
7	CHECK OPERATION OF RBS ALARM (SHOULD BE CONNECTED FOR FAILSAFE OPERATION).
8	VISUALLY INSPECT THE INERTIA BRAKE PADS FOR WEAR OR DAMAGE.
9	CHECK THE OIL FOR SIGNS OF WEAR METALS OR CONTAMINATION.
10	TEST THE LOW OIL PRESSURE CUT-OUT BY SIMULATION.
11	INSPECT THE TORQUE WRENCH STABBING GUIDE FOR EXCESSIVE WEAR OR DAMAGE. INSPECT SPLINES FOR NICKS AND GOUGES.
12	VISUALLY INSPECT ALL HYDRAULIC SYSTEM COMPONENTS FOR LEAKS. CHECK THE ROTATING HEAD FOR SEAL LEAKAGE.
13	INSPECT THE COUNTER BALANCE CYLINDERS, ACCUMULATORS AND MANIFOLD PIPING FOR LEAKS AND DAMAGE.
14	ON THE RBS SYSTEM, INSPECT ALL HYDRAULIC CYLINDERS AND PIPING FOR LEAKS, WEAR AND DAMAGE.
15	ON THE RBS SYSTEM, INSPECT ALL BEARINGS FOR SIGNS OF WEAR AND DAMAGE.
16	ON THE RBS SYSTEM, VISUALLY INSPECT THE RBS GUIDE RAILS FOR CORROSION, WEAR AND DAMAGE.
17	INSPECT THE DUAL HYDRAULIC POWER CONTROL STATIONS FOR DAMAGE, WEAR AND LEAKS.
18	VISUALLY INSPECT THE BULL GEAR TEETH FOR WEAR AND DAMAGE.
19	CLEAN AND INSPECT THE TOP QUILL SHAFT BEARING HOUSING SEAL FOR DAMAGE AND DETERIORATION.
20	INSPECT THE ALIGNMENT BLOCKS ON THE GUIDE DOLLY ASSEMBLY FOR WEAR AND DAMAGE.
21	CHECK THE LAST DATE OF CALIBRATION FOR TORQUE READOUT GAUGE.

3.5.12	TOP DRIVE BLOWER MOTOR
1	MEGGER TEST MOTOR WINDINGS TO GROUND AND RECORD.
2	CHECK CABLE GLAND FOR PROPER SEALING.
3	CHECK DUCTING FOR LEAKS.
4	CHECK BEARINGS FOR EXCESSIVE NOISE/TEMPERATURE.
5	CHECK PRESSURE SWITCH AND ASSOCIATED ALARMS.

3.5.13	TOP DRIVE DC MOTOR
1	REMOVE INSPECTION COVERS AND VISUALLY INSPECT INTERIOR FOR DEFECTS. CLEAN AIR INLET AND OUTLET COVERS OR FILTERS. ENSURE COVERS FIT AND LATCH PROPERLY.
2	PERFORM AN INSULATION RESISTANCE TEST AT 1.2 TIMES RATED OPERATING VOLTAGE AND RECORD VALUE FOR ARMATURE AND FIELD.
3	MEASURE RESISTANCE OF FIELD WINDINGS AND RECORD. COMPARE WITH MANUFACTURERS SPECIFICATION. MEASURE RESISTANCE BETWEEN MOTOR HOUSING AND SOLID GROUND.
4	CHECK RECORD OF MOTOR BEARING GREASING INTERVALS: DATE - RUNNING HOURS.

3.5.14	SWIVEL
1	CHECK THE RESULTS OF THE LAST OIL ANALYSIS.
2	REMOVE DRAIN PLUG AND CHECK BAR MAGNETS FOR METAL PARTICLES.
3	INSPECT ALL GREASE POINTS FOR DAMAGE. ENSURE GREASE IS BEING PUMPED THROUGH ALL GREASE NIPPLES.
4	CHECK TO ENSURE SWIVEL ROTATES FREELY. WHILE ROTATING THE SWIVEL, FEEL FOR ROUGHNESS OF THE BEARINGS.
5	INSPECT THE SWIVEL BOX AND SUB PINS FOR SIGNS OF WEAR AND DAMAGE.
6	CHECK THAT THE BAIL PIN KEEPERS ARE IN POSITION AND SECURE.

3.5.15	MAST
1	CHECK CROWN SAFETY PLATFORM FOR DAMAGE.
2	CHECK CROWN ASSEMBLY HANDRAILS FOR CRACKED WELDS AND/OR DAMAGE.
3	CHECK CROWN FRAME FOR BENT BEAM FLANGES AND BEAM WEBS. ENSURE THERE ARE NO CRACKED WELDS.
4	CHECK CROWN SUPPORT BEAM FOR BENT BEAM FLANGES AND BEAM WEBS. ENSURE THERE ARE NO CRACKED WELDS.
5	CHECK FOR BOWING, CRACKED WELDS, PIN CONNECTION CONDITION AND THAT SAFETY PINS ARE INSTALLED: FRONT LEGS DRILLERS AND OFF-DRILLERS SIDE, REAR LEGS.
6	CHECK GIRTS AND BRACING FOR DAMAGE AND CRACKED WELDS.
7	CHECK RACKING PLATFORM FRAME FOR DAMAGE, CRACKED WELDS, WORN PIN CONNECTIONS AND MISSING SAFETY PINS.
8	CHECK FOR DAMAGE AND/OR CRACKED WELDS: RACKING PLATFORM FINGERS, WORKING PLATFORM, HANDRAILS, LADDERS, LADDER RETAINING CAGES.

3.5.16	CROWN BLOCK
1	INSPECT ALL GREASE POINTS FOR DAMAGE. ENSURE GREASE IS BEING PUMPED THROUGH ALL GREASE NIPPLES/LINES.
2	VISUALLY CHECK SHEAVE BEARINGS FOR PROPER GREASING.
3	VISUALLY INSPECT THE BLOCK ASSEMBLY FOR SIGNS OF WEAR, CRACKS, DEFORMATION OR DAMAGE.
4	USING A BAR, CHECK EACH BEARING FOR EXCESSIVE LOOSENESS.
5	USE GROOVE GAUGE TO CHECK WEAR OF SHEAVE GROOVES. THE GROOVES SHOULD BE SMOOTH AND FREE OF WIRELINE IMPRINTS. RECORD.
6	INSPECT SHEAVE GROOVES FOR CORRUGATIONS OR SCOURING (WIRELINE IMPRINTS).
7	RECORD DATE WHEN ALL SHEAVES WERE LAST GIVEN 100% MPI.

3.5.17	TRAVELING BLOCK
1	VISUALLY CHECK SHEAVE BEARINGS FOR PROPER GREASING.
2	VISUALLY CHECK THE BLOCK ASSEMBLY FOR SIGNS OF WEAR, CRACKS, DEFORMATION OR LOOSE FASTENERS AND RETAINERS.
3	USING A BAR, CHECK EACH SHEAVE BEARING FOR EXCESSIVE LOOSENESS.
4	CHECK THE SHEAVE GROUP FOR WEAR AND CORRECT PROFILE USING API SHEAVE GROOVE WEAR GAUGE AND RECORD.
5	INSPECT SHEAVE GROOVE FOR CORRUGATIONS OR SCOURING (WIRELINE IMPRINT).
6	RECORD DATE OF LAST CLEVIS PIN MPI INSPECTION.
7	RECORD DATE OF LAST MAJOR (MANUFACTURER) OVERHAUL OF BLOCK.

3.5.18	TRAVELING BLOCK GUIDE DOLLY
1	INSPECT ALL GREASE POINTS FOR DAMAGE. ENSURE GREASE IS ABLE TO BE PUMPED THROUGH ALL GREASE NIPPLES.
2	CHECK THE DOLLY WHEELS FOR ANY FLAT SPOTS ON THE FACE, THIS WOULD INDICATE BEARING SEIZURE.
3	CHECK FOR DOLLY WHEEL MOVEMENT TO DETERMINE IF BEARING REPLACEMENT IS REQUIRED.
4	PRY EACH DOLLY WHEEL AND GIVE AN ESTIMATED PERCENTAGE OF WEAR. WHEELS SHOULD ROTATE FREELY BUT HAVE LESS THAN 1/16" AXIAL OR RADIAL PLAY.
5	CHECK THE DOLLY TRACK FOR SIGNS OF WEAR OR DEFORMATION.
6	VISUALLY INSPECT THE DOLLY ASSEMBLY FOR SIGNS OF WEAR, CRACKS, DEFORMATION OR DAMAGE OR LOOSE FASTENERS.

3.5.19	DRILL STRING COMPENSATOR
1	CHECK ALL ROLLERS TO ENSURE THAT THEY ARE LUBRICATED AND FREE RUNNING.
2	CHECK THE PISTON RODS FOR PITTING.
3	CHECK HIGH-PRESSURE HOSES FOR AGE, DAMAGE AND LEAKING CONNECTIONS.
4	CHECK PISTON ROD PACKING FOR EVIDENCE OF HYDRAULIC LEAKS.
5	CHECK ALL PIPING FOR LEAKS AND PHYSICAL DAMAGE.
6	CHECK ALL BOLTS FOR TIGHTNESS AND CORROSION.
7	CHECK SYSTEM OPERATION OF POSITION INDICATOR AND LIGHTS "LOCK-UNLOCK".
8	CHECK ALL CONNECTING CABLES AND STUFFING TUBES FOR DAMAGE AND CORROSION.
9	CHECK POSITION SWITCHES FOR PHYSICAL DAMAGE AND CORROSION.
10	CHECK POSITION SENSOR AND VERIFY WITH ACTUAL PISTON POSITION.
11	CHECK TERMINAL BOARDS FOR LOOSE CONNECTIONS AND OXIDATION OF WIRES AND TERMINALS.
12	CHECK HYDRAULIC OIL RESERVOIR FOR CLEANLINESS OF FLUID (REFER TO LAST FLUID ANALYSIS REPORT). RE-FILTER OR REPLACE AS REQUIRED.
13	CHECK ALL GAUGES FOR DAMAGE OR MALFUNCTION.
14	CHECK PRE-CHARGE IN ACCUMULATORS (350 PSI NITROGEN).
15	CHECK HYDRAULIC LOCK DECLARATION VALVE OPERATION ON MAST TRAVELLING BLOCK.

3.5.20	BRAKE COOL WATER SYSTEM
1	CHECK DATE WHEN ZINC ANODES WERE LAST REPLACED.
2	CHECK AND INSPECT ALL PIPING AND NIPPLES FOR CORROSION, ESPECIALLY AROUND THE END HOUSING.
3	CHECK FRESH WATER INHIBITOR CONCENTRATION.
4	CHECK ENTIRE SYSTEM FOR LEAKAGE.
5	CHECK ELMAGCO BRAKE INLET WATER FLOW - APPROXIMATELY 75 GPM (300 LT/MIN) SHOULD BE MEASURED ON EACH SIDE OF THE BRAKE. RECORD INBOARD AND OUTBOARD.
6	CHECK FRICTION BRAKE OUTLET WATER FLOW - APPROXIMATELY 50 GPM AT THE BRAKE STUFFING BOX. RECORD DISCHARGE SIDE.

3.5.21	BRAKE COOLING SYSTEM
1	RUN PUMP FOR A SHORT PERIOD AND CHECK FOR LEAKS, VIBRATION.
2	CHECK MAXIMUM DISCHARGE PRESSURE, RECORD.
3	CHECK ACTUAL MOTOR LOAD AMPERES, RECORD AS FOUND.
4	BRAKE COOLING WATER PUMPS TO BE MEGGER TESTED. MCC'S TO BE INSPECTED. REMOTE START/STOP TO BE CHECKED. FLOW/TEMP SENSOR TO BE CHECKED.

3.5.22	DRILL WATER PUMP
1	CHECK PUMP SHAFT MECHANICAL SEAL FOR LEAKAGE.
2	CHECK BEARING OIL SEALS FOR LEAKAGE.
3	RUN PUMP FOR A SHORT PERIOD AND CHECK FOR VIBRATION AND OVERHEATING (130 DEGF TO 140 DEGF IS NORMAL).
4	CHECK MAXIMUM DISCHARGE PRESSURE AND RECORD.
5	CHECK ACTUAL MOTOR LOAD AMPERES AND RECORD.

3.5.23	WEIGHT INDICATOR
1	CHECK GAP ON THE SENSATOR, SHOULD BE 5/8" WITH BLOCKS EMPTY.
2	INSPECT WEIGHT INDICATOR GAUGE - RECORD LAST CALIBRATION DATE.
3	INSPECT HOSE FROM SENSATOR TO INDICATOR FOR LEAKS AND DAMAGE.
4	ENSURE FREEDOM OF MOVEMENT OF SENSATOR PINS.

3.5.24	DRILLERS CONTROL CONSOLE
1	CARRY OUT LAMP TEST - REPORT ANY LAMP OUTAGE.
2	CHECK FOR ANY AIR/HYDRAULIC LEAKS.
3	CHECK ALL GAUGES FOR DAMAGE.
4	ENSURE THAT THE CONSOLE IS CLEAN AND THAT ALL INSTRUCTIONS ARE LEGIBLE.
5	CONFIRM CALIBRATION INTEGRITY OF ALL INSTRUMENTS ON DRILLERS CONSOLE.

3.5.25	DRILLING RECORDER
1	CHECK RECORDER COMPONENTS FOR OBVIOUS DAMAGE, WEAR AND INSECURITY OF PARTS.
2	CHECK INTERNAL HYDRAULIC AND PNEUMATIC TUBING FOR DAMAGE AND DETERIORATION. VERIFY THAT ALL CONNECTIONS ARE TIGHT.

3.5.26	WIRELINE RETRIEVING UNIT
1	CHECK THAT THE MAINTENANCE IS BEING CARRIED OUT ACCORDING TO STANDARD.

3.5.27	RIG FLOOR GENERAL
1	EMERGENCY POWER SHUTDOWN AT DRILLER'S CONSOLE (REGULARLY TESTED & CHECKED)
2	BACKUP TO ELMAGCO BRAKE PROVIDED
3	HAND RAILS AND TOE BOARDS COMPLETELY AROUND RIG FLOOR
4	V-DOOR GUARDED (ROUTINELY USED)
5	STAIRS & STEPS NON-SKID, PROPERLY SECURED WITH STANDARD RAILINGS
6	FLOOR / ROTARY FLOOR & RACKING AREA IN GOOD CONDITION
7	NON-SKID MATERIAL USED ON FLOOR/AROUND ROTARY FLOOR IN GOOD CONDITION & EFFECTIVE
8	FLOORS FREE OF GREASE & TRIPPING HAZARDS
9	SMOKING OR OPEN FLAMES PROHIBITED - SIGNS VISIBLE
10	FIRE EXTINGUISHERS IN AREA
11	OPENINGS IN FLOOR COVERED / GUARDED
12	MOVING MACHINERY (DRIVE BELTS, CHAINS, FANS, GEARS, SHAFTS) PROPERLY GUARDED
13	LIGHTING ADEQUATE, GUARDED & EXPLOSION PROOF
14	WIRING & OUTLETS IN GOOD CONDITION
15	TOOLS, HAND TOOLS, SUBS, BITS ETC. IN GOOD CONDITION & PROPERLY STORED
16	STAB-ON KELLY COCK WRENCH ACCESSIBLE & READY FOR INSTALLATION
17	MUD BOX IN GOOD CONDITION, PROPERLY RIGGED
18	HOSE & LINES FOR CIRCULATING IN SATISFACTORY CONDITION & SNUBBED-OFF
19	ROTARY HOSE AND KELLY SPINNER HOSE IN SATISFACTORY CONDITION & SNUBBED OFF
20	BOP CONTROLS LABELLED & OPERATING PROPERLY (LAST FUNCTION CHECKED: FREQUENCY)
21	CHOKE PANEL, GAUGES & CONTROLS FUNCTION TESTED (DATE)
22	GEOLOGRAPH FUNCTIONING
23	FLOW SHOW FUNCTIONING
24	PVT FUNCTIONING
25	TWO MEANS OF EGRESS FROM DOGHOUSE TO RIG FLOOR
26	DOGHOUSE HEATERS NOT AN IGNITION SOURCE (FLAME PROOF)
27	COMMON DRINKING CUPS PROHIBITED
28	FIRST AID KIT & EYEWASH IN DOGHOUSE (OR READILY ACCESSIBLE), PRIMED / READY FOR USE
29	BULLETIN BOARDS; SAFETY SIGNS; REQUIRED NOTICES: BOP PROCEDURES, MAX ALLOWABLE CASING PRESSURE, FILLUP INFO
30	COMMUNICATIONS (APPROPRIATE FOR LOCATION)

3.5.28	COLUMN VOID SPACES
1	ENSURE ALL SPACES ARE PROPERLY VENTILATED BEFORE ENTRY. CHECK WITH AN OXYGEN LEVEL INDICATOR.
2	WHILE IN VOID, EXERCISE ALL BILGE VALVES. VERIFY CORRECTLY MARKED REACH ROD OPERATES THE APPROPRIATE VALVE.
3	CHECK FOR PROPER LUBRICATION OF ALL BEVEL GEARS, VALVE STEMS AND GLAND PACKING.
4	CHECK BILGE WELLS AND ROSE BOXES.
5	INSPECT MANHOLE COVERS FOR DISTORTION. CHECK CONDITION OF STUD THREADS, NUTS, RUBBER GASKET ETC.

3.5.29	SUBSTRUCTURE / CELLAR / WELLBAY / TEXAS DECK
1	ASSEMBLY PINS IN PLACE & SECURE WITH KEEPERS
2	BEAMS & BRACES IN GOOD CONDITION
3	ADEQUATE LIGHTING; GUARDED; EXPLOSION PROOF
4	PROPER SCAFFOLDING
5	CELLAR AREA FREE OF DEBRIS
6	FIRE EXTINGUISHERS IN AREA
7	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING
8	LOWER & UPPER SUBSTRUCTURE BOLTED OR PINNED TOGETHER
9	DRILLING LINE PROPERLY INSTALLED ON ANCHOR & KEYED
10	AIR TANK DRAINED DAILY; RELIEF VALVES (REPORTED/LAST CHECKED & TESTED)
11	LADDERS ON LOCATION IN GOOD CONDITION; SAFETY LINES/INERTIA BRAKES USED
12	WORK VESTS AVAILABLE & USED

3.5.30	CATHODIC PROTECTION
1	CHECK TRANSFORMER AND REACTORS FOR GROUND RESISTANCE WITH AN OHMMETER, RECORD READINGS AS FOUND.
2	MEASURE CONNECTION RESISTANCE OF GROUND CABLE TO MAIN STRUCTURE AND RECORD.
3	DISCONNECT REFERENCE CELL CABLES AND MEASURE OPEN CIRCUIT POTENTIAL OF EACH CELL TO HULL. RECORD.
4	DISCONNECT ANODE FUSES AND MEASURE POTENTIAL DIFFERENCE OF EACH ANODE TO HULL. RECORD. RECONNECT.
5	CHECK SYSTEM LOG SHEET ON POTENTIAL VOLT READINGS (LOOK FOR ANY CHANGES OVER THE PAST PERIOD).
6	CHECK AND VERIFY CORRECT READOUT ON REMOTE PANEL (IN CONTROL ROOM).

3.5.31	ANTI-FOULING STRUCTURE
1	CHECK TRANSFORMERS AND RESISTORS FOR GROUND RESISTANCE WITH AN OHMMETER, RECORD READINGS AS FOUND.
2	CHECK SYSTEM LOG SHEETS ON CURRENT READINGS. LOOK FOR ANY CHANGES OVER THE PAST PERIOD.
3	CHECK RIG HISTORY FILE REGARDING INSPECTION AND REPLACEMENT OF ANODES.

3.5.32	WATERTIGHT DOORS
1	CHECK THAT DOOR SEAL RUBBERS ARE IN GOOD CONDITION.
2	WITH DOOR IN CLOSED POSITION, CHECK THAT THERE ARE NO GAPS AROUND THE SEALING AREAS.
3	CHECK DOGS AND ACTUATING LINKAGES FOR DAMAGE.
4	CHECK FOR ANY HYDRAULIC OIL LEAKS AT CYLINDERS OR PIPING.
5	CHECK OPERATION OF ALL MANUAL OPEN/CLOSE HAND WHEELS.
6	CHECK OIL LEVEL IN HYDRAULIC TANK AT MAIN DECK LEVEL.
7	CHECK OPERATION OF EMERGENCY (CLOSE) HAND WHEEL AT DECK LEVEL.
8	CHECK BOTH MANUAL AND REMOTE OPERATION OF ALL DOORS.

3.5.33	MISCELLANEOUS VALVES
1	OPERATE THE VALVE TO THE FULLY OPEN/CLOSED POSITION IF POSSIBLE.
2	CHECK THAT THE LEGEND PLATE IS CLEAN AND LEGIBLE.
3.6	CIRCULATING SYSTEM

3.6.1	MUD STORAGE TANK / CEMENT TANKS
1	PALLETS OF SACK MATERIAL & MUD PROPERLY STACKED IN AN ORDERLY FASHION
2	TORN SACKS CLEANED UP; KEPT TO A MINIMUM
3	CAUSTIC SODA & OTHER HAZARDOUS CHEMICALS PROPERLY LABELLED, STORED & HANDLED
4	NO ASBESTOS PRODUCTS ON LOCATION
5	CHEMICAL WARNING SIGNS
6	ADEQUATE VENTILATION
7	EYEWASH & PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT AVAILABLE
8	FORKLIFT PROPERLY MAINTAINED & OPERATED SAFELY
9	BULK & CEMENT TANKS EQUIPPED WITH RUPTURE DISKS AND/OR PRESSURE RELIEF DEVICES THAT FUNCTION PROPERLY (DATE LAST TESTED / CERTIFIED)
10	FUNCTION TEST EACH REMOTE CONTROLLED VALVE (DRILLING OPERATION PERMITTED)
11	CHECK AND VERIFY LIGHTS AND POSITION INDICATORS ARE INDICATING CORRECTLY
12	VERIFY THAT PRESSURE INDICATORS AND TRANSMITTERS ARE OPERATING CORRECTLY
13	CHECK PURGE AIR SYSTEM ON CONTROL PANELS AND RECORD INPUT AND OUTPUT PRESSURES
14	VERIFY THAT WEIGHT INDICATORS ARE INDICATING CORRECTLY

3.6.2	MUD PUMPS
1	RELIEF VALVES (POP-OFFS) PINNED, SET & COVERED (DATE TESTED / PRESSURE SETTING)
2	RELIEF LINE PROPERLY VENTED & SECURED
3	VIBRATOR HOSE SNUBBED AT BOTH ENDS
4	MOVING MACHINERY PROPERLY GUARDED
5	PROPER TOOLS & EQUIPMENT FOR PUMP MAINTENANCE
6	FIRE EXTINGUISHERS IN AREA
7	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING
8	VALVES & CONTROLS PROPERLY LABELLED
9	LOCKOUT SWITCH ON EACH PUMP
10	PULSATION DAMPENERS CHECKED
11	CHECK THE MAIN AND PINION GEAR FOR CONDITION AND UNIFORM WEAR
12	CHECK PINION DRIVE CHAIN AND SPROCKETS FOR WEAR
13	CHECK LUBE OIL PRESSURE - CHAIN LUBRICATION SYSTEM AND RECORD
14	CHECK LUBE OIL PRESSURE - GEARBOX
15	CHECK AND RECORD DATE OF LAST LUBE OIL ANALYSIS (CRANKCASE OIL)

3.6.3	MUD PUMP BLOWER MOTORS
1	MEGGER TEST MOTOR WINDINGS TO GROUND, RECORD AS FOUND FOR ALL MOTORS.
2	MEASURE RESISTANCE BETWEEN MOTOR HOUSING(S) AND SOLID GROUND, RECORD IF MORE THAN ONE OHM.
3	CHECK MOTOR BEARINGS FOR EXCESSIVE NOISE AND TEMPERATURE.

3.6.4	MUD PUMP MOTOR
1	CHECK COMMUTATOR SURFACE FOR FILM, STRIPPING, BAR BURNING, HIGH AND LOW BARS, HIGH MICA AND FLAT SPOTS. IF ANY FOUND, FIND CAUSE AND CORRECT.
2	TAKE A FINAL MEGGER TEST OF MOTOR FIELD AND ARMATURE, RECORD AS FOUND.
3	MEASURE RESISTANCE BETWEEN MOTOR HOUSING(S) AND SOLID HULL GROUND, RECORD IF MORE THAN 1 OHM.
4	WHILE MOTOR IS OPERATIONAL, CHECK MOTOR BEARINGS FOR EXCESSIVE NOISE AND TEMPERATURE AND MOUNTING BOLTS FOR TIGHTNESS.
5	CHECK DUCTING FOR AIR LEAKS OR DAMAGE.
6	MEGGER TEST AUXILIARY MOTORS AND RECORD READINGS (BLOWER, CHAIN OILER, LINER WASH).

3.6.5	PULSATION DAMPENER
1	CHECK PRESSURE GAUGE FOR CORRECT CALIBRATION.
2	CHECK CHARGE PIPING AND PURGE/CHARGE VALVES AND EQUIPMENT FOR DEFECTS.
3	CHECK DATE AND RESULTS OF LAST ULTRASONIC WALL THICKNESS TEST.

3.6.6	AGITATOR
1	CHECK ALL AGITATORS WHILE OPERATING FOR MOTOR BEARING OR GEARBOX DEFECTS.
2	CHECK CONDITION OF IMPELLERS.

3.6.7	CHICKSAN SWIVEL
1	CARRY OUT A PHYSICAL INSPECTION OF ALL CHICKSAN SWIVELS. CHECK RECORDS TO ENSURE THAT THE CONTENT OF PM TASKS HAS BEEN COMPLIED WITH.

3.6.8	BULK VALVE CONTROL PANEL
1	CHECK IF PURGE AIR IS FREE OF OIL AND MOISTURE.
2	VERIFY PURGE AIR SYSTEM AND AIRFLOW MONITOR ARE OPERATING CORRECTLY.
3	TURN OFF PURGE AIR SUPPLY (PURGE AIR FAILURE LIGHT ON DRILL FLOOR MUST COME ON).
4	CHECK AND VERIFY THAT LIGHTS AND POSITION INDICATORS ARE INDICATING CORRECTLY.

3.6.9	MUD HOPPER / MIXING BARREL
1	MUD - SAFE MEANS OF ACCESS & EGRESS
2	CHEMICAL BARREL SAFELY CONSTRUCTED AND LOCATED
3	CHEMICAL WARNING SIGNS
4	PERSONNEL INSTRUCTED IN SAFE HANDLING OF CHEMICALS (PROCEDURE IN PLACE AND ENFORCED)
5	PERSONAL SAFETY EQUIPMENT - RUBBER GLOVES, RUBBER APRON, GOGGLES, DUST MASK - AVAILABLE & USED AS REQUIRED
6	EMERGENCY EYEWASH STATION NEARBY - PRIMED & READY FOR USE. DELUGE SHOWER REQUIRED
7	MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS READILY AVAILABLE, COPIES WITH RIG MEDIC & TOOLPUSHER
8	ADEQUATE BARITE & CHEMICAL MIXING/ROUTING SYSTEM (TESTED)

3.6.10	CEMENTING UNIT
1	MOVING MACHINERY PROPERLY GUARDED
2	ADEQUATE VENTILATION - DUST MASKS AVAILABLE
3	FIRE EXTINGUISHER PROVIDED
4	PIPING & CONNECTION ADEQUATE FOR ANTICIPATED PRESSURES; PROPERLY SECURED
5	SPILL PREVENTION EQUIPMENT PROVIDED; CURBS, DRIP PANS, DRAINS ETC.
6	PRESSURE RELIEF VALVE FITTED

3.6.11	MUD TANKS / SOLIDS CONTROL EQUIPMENT AREA
1	PASSAGEWAYS - SAFE CLEARANCE; NO OBSTRUCTION; NO MISSING OR UNSAFE GRATING
2	STAIRS IN GOOD CONDITION; NON-SKID; PROPERLY SECURED
3	HAND RAILS ON ALL PASSAGEWAYS & STAIRS - IN GOOD CONDITION; SECURE; TOE BOARDS
4	LIGHTING - ADEQUATE FOR AREA, EXPLOSION PROOF
5	MOVING MACHINERY PROPERLY GUARDED
6	ELECTRICAL WIRING - IN ACCORDANCE WITH HAZARDOUS AREA CLASSIFICATION
7	PVT EQUIPMENT - SECURE & WORKING PROPERLY
8	FUNCTION TEST PVT EQUIPMENT & CHECK CALIBRATION
9	PRESSURE RELIEF LINES PROPERLY SECURED, SLOPING AWAY FROM RELIEF VALVE
10	PERSONAL PROTECTIVE GEAR - APRONS / GOGGLES / MASKS / EYEWASH - AVAILABLE & USED
11	VALVES ACCESSIBLE, IN GOOD CONDITION; HANDLES SECURED
12	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING
13	SMOKING PROHIBITED
14	ADEQUATE PIPING & VALVES FOR KILL MUD PREPARATION AND PUMPING (INCLUDING ACCESS FOR CEMENT UNIT)
15	ADEQUATE TRIP TANK ARRANGEMENT WITH MANUAL GAUGING SYSTEM VISIBLE FROM DRILLING CONSOLE

3.6.12	SHALE SHAKER
1	VISUALLY EXAMINE THE UNIT FOR SIGNS OF WEAR OR DAMAGE.
2	CHECK FOR ANY BUILD UP OF SOLIDS FROM FAN END OF ELECTRIC DRIVE MOTORS.
3	CHECK BEARINGS AND ROLLER ASSEMBLIES ON BELT TENSIONERS FOR WEAR.
4	VISUALLY EXAMINE MAIN DRIVE SHAFT BEARINGS FOR WEAR AND/OR DAMAGE.

3.6.13	SHALE SHAKER MOTORS
1	CHECK ALL INDICATING LIGHTS FOR CORRECT OPERATION.
2	VISUALLY INSPECT ALL ELECTRICAL CABLES FOR DAMAGE.
3	VISUALLY INSPECT ELECTRIC MOTORS FOR DAMAGE AND MUD BUILD UP. KEEP COOLING FANS CLEAN.
4	CHECK AND RECORD MOTOR CURRENT IN EACH PHASE. A RISE IN AMPS ON ANY PHASE WITH CONSTANT VOLTAGE CAN INDICATE A PROBLEM IN THE VIBRATOR BEARINGS.
5	CHECK THE FLEX CONDUIT ON THE POWER SUPPLY TO THE VIBRATING MOTORS FOR DAMAGE AND CRACKING.

3.6.14	SHALE SHAKER AREA
1	MOVING MACHINERY PROPERLY GUARDED, GUARDS SECURE
2	STAIRS IN GOOD CONDITION; NON-SKID; PROPERLY SECURED
3	HAND RAILS FITTED ON ALL PASSAGEWAYS & STAIRS; IN GOOD CONDITION; SECURE; TOEBOARDS
4	PASSAGEWAYS - SAFE CLEARANCE; NO OBSTRUCTIONS; NO MISSING, LOOSE OR UNSAFE GRATING
5	ELECTRICAL EQUIPMENT EXPLOSION PROOF (STATE STANDARD / CODE)
6	EYEWASH STATION NEARBY
7	PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT AVAILABLE
8	VENTILATION (SPECIFICATION HAZARDOUS AREA CLASSIFICATION)

3.6.15	DEGASSER
1	OPEN LARGE HATCH CONTAINING THE VACUUM BREAKER VALVE TO GET ACCESS TO INTERIOR OF CYCLONE SEPARATOR.
2	CHECK VACUUM CONTROL LINE - 1 1/2" PIPE CONNECTING THE VENTURI THROUGH THE FLOAT VALVE TO VACUUM BREAKER.
3	ENSURE FLOAT IS NOT LEAKING OR ARM BROKEN.
4	ENSURE FLOAT, TRUNNION AND CONTROL VALVE OPERATE FREELY.
5	CHECK GAUGES (0-100 PSI AT JET NOZZLE AND 0-30" VACUUM GAUGE TOP OF SEPARATOR) FOR DAMAGE.
6	CHECK ALL FLANGES, CONNECTIONS AND OPENINGS FOR VACUUM LEAKS WHILE UNIT IS IN OPERATION.

3.6.16	DEGASSER PUMP
1	START AND RUN PUMP, CHECK FOR UNUSUAL NOISE, LEAKS, VIBRATION OR OVERHEATING.
2	CHECK AND RECORD DETAILS OF LAST MAJOR OVERHAUL (IMPELLER, BEARINGS, SEAL REPLACEMENT).

3.6.17	DESANDER PUMP
1	START AND RUN PUMP, CHECK FOR UNUSUAL NOISE, LEAKS, VIBRATION OR OVERHEATING.
2	CHECK DATE AND CONTENT OF LAST MAJOR OVERHAUL.

3.6.18	MUD CLEANER
1	INSPECT HYDRAULIC HOSES AND CONNECTIONS FOR DETERIORATION AND LEAKS.
2	CHECK THE VARIABLE SPEED RANGE OF SCREEN.
3	CHECK HYDRAULIC DISCHARGE PRESSURE AND RECORD (1200 PSI MAX).

3.6.19	MUD CLEANER PUMP
1	START AND RUN PUMP, CHECK FOR UNUSUAL NOISE, LEAKS, VIBRATION OR OVERHEATING.
2	CHECK DATE AND CONTENT OF LAST MAJOR OVERHAUL.

3.7	LIFTING AND HANDLING

3.7.1	CRANES
1	CONTROLS PROPERLY LABELLED
2	LOAD RATING CHART & WEIGHT INDICATOR
3	HAND SIGNALS POSTED
4	MOVING MACHINERY PROPERLY GUARDED & GUARDS SECURE
5	BOOM ANGLE INDICATOR & STOPS PROVIDED
6	BOOM STRUCTURE IN GOOD CONDITION
7	LIMIT SWITCH DEVICE PROVIDED
8	WIRE ROPES IN GOOD CONDITION, ANTI-TWIST
9	HOOKS PROVIDED WITH SAFETY CLIP
10	TAG LINES AVAILABLE & THEIR USE REQUIRED
11	PERSONNEL BASKETS IN GOOD CONDITION & PROCEDURES ADEQUATE
12	BACKUP SAFETY WIRE PROVIDED BETWEEN BASKET & HOOK
13	DRIP PAN PROVIDED
14	CRANES TEST (RECORD OF TEST)
15	COMMUNICATIONS APPROPRIATE TO INSTALLATION
16	OPERATING MANUAL COMPLIES WITH MODU CODE WHERE APPLICABLE

3.7.2	FORKLIFT
1	CHECK OPERATION OF ALL CONTROLS, BRAKES, THROTTLE, FORWARD/REVERSE GEARS, HOIST, LOWER AND TILT OF FORKS. CHECK FOR MOTION ALARM.
2	CHECK FOR OIL LEAKS.
3	CHECK ENGINE OVERSPEED SHUTDOWN AND PYROBAN SYSTEM IF FITTED.
4	CHECK REVERSING MIRROR AND MANUAL HORN ARE IN PLACE AND OPERATIONAL.

3.7.3	AIR HOIST
1	OPEN AIR SUPPLY VALVE SLOWLY AND CHECK OPERATION OF AIR THROTTLE AND BRAKE.
2	CHECK WIRE ROPE FOR BROKEN WIRES, ABRASIONS, KINKS AND CORROSION.
3	CHECK BRAKE ASSEMBLY FOR WEAR OF BRAKE BANDS AND LINKAGE SEIZURE.
4	CHECK OPERATION OF AUTOMATIC BRAKE (IF FITTED).
5	ENSURE DRUM GUARDS ARE IN PLACE.
6	ENSURE EXHAUST IS VENTED TO AN AREA THAT DOES NOT CREATE A SAFETY HAZARD.
7	USING A LOAD CELL, CHECK THAT HOIST CAN PULL AND HOLD ON ITS BRAKE THE SWL (ENSURE SWL DATA PLATE IS IN PLACE ON HOIST).

3.7.4	PIPE RACK AREA
1	PIPE RACKS LEVEL
2	ENDS PINNED & LAYERS CHOCKED
3	V-DOOR RAMP & CATWALK - LEVEL & IN GOOD CONDITION
4	PIPE STOPS PROVIDED
5	STACKED MATERIAL SECURED AGAINST FALLING
6	GENERAL HOUSEKEEPING - AREA FREE FROM OBSTRUCTION / DEBRIS

3.8	DOWNHOLE / FISHING / CASING EQUIPMENT

3.8.1	KELLY
1	VISUALLY INSPECT EXTERNAL SURFACE FOR DAMAGE I.E. PITTING, CORROSION, EROSION, GOUGES ETC.
2	VISUALLY INSPECT CONNECTORS FOR PITTING, CORROSION, EROSION, BELLED BOX, SHOULDER/SEAL DAMAGE. ALSO INSPECT FOR STRETCHED THREADS - THREAD PROFILE GAUGE TO BE USED.

3.8.2	X-OVER SUBS
1	VISUALLY INSPECT EXTERNAL SURFACE FOR DAMAGE I.E. PITTING, CORROSION, EROSION, GOUGES ETC.
2	VISUALLY INSPECT CONNECTORS FOR PITTING, CORROSION, EROSION, BELLED BOX, SHOULDER/SEAL DAMAGE. ALSO INSPECT FOR STRETCHED THREADS.

3.8.3	RACKING ARM
1	CHECK HYDRAULIC OIL RESERVOIR LEVEL.
2	CHECK OIL LEVEL IN SWING GEAR REDUCER.
3	CHECK ALL HYDRAULIC HOSES FOR TIGHTNESS, LEAKS, PINCHING AND AGING.
4	CHECK HYDRAULIC CYLINDERS FOR LEAKAGE AROUND ROD PACKING.
5	REMOVE REAR COVER PLATE ON CONTROL CONSOLE AND EXAMINE ALL HOSES AND CONNECTIONS FOR LEAKS.
6	RUN UNIT THROUGH ALL FIVE MAJOR FUNCTIONS (BOOM LIFT/LOWER, SWING, ETC). CHECK FOR EASE OF CONTROLS AND THAT MULTIPLE UNIT VALVE SPOOLS RETURN TO NEUTRAL.
7	CHECK OPERATOR BOOM CLAWS FOR DEFORMATION AND DAMAGE.
8	CHECK HYDRAULIC PUMP FOR VIBRATION, LEAKAGE AND ABNORMAL TEMPERATURE WHILE RUNNING.

3.8.4	CASING STABBING BOARD
1	VISUALLY INSPECT STRUCTURAL COMPONENTS, HANDRAILS, CLAMPS, BRACKETS, TRACKS, PADEYES. REPORT DEFECTS.
2	VISUALLY INSPECT: ROLLERS, SHACKLES, HOISTING ASSEMBLY SAFETY LOCK DEVICE AND INERTIA REEL SAFETY BLOCK. CHECK AND RECORD CERTIFICATION DATE.
3	CARRY OUT DROP TEST.

3.8.5	DRILL COLLAR SLIPS
1	CHECK RECORDS TO ENSURE THAT MPI OF SLIPS BY THIRD PARTY HAS BEEN DONE. RECORD DATE.
2	VISUALLY CHECK THE WEB AREA OF EACH SEGMENT FOR CRACKS. CRACKS INDICATE INCORRECT SUPPORT OR WRONG SIZE DRILL COLLAR.

3.8.6	DRILL COLLAR SAFETY CLAMP
1	CHECK GRIPPING DIES FOR WEAR.
2	CHECK FOR ANY WORN PARTS.

3.8.7	DRILL PIPE SLIPS
1	CHECK DATE OF LAST THIRD PARTY MPI.
2	PERFORM "SLIP TEST" PROCEDURE TO DETERMINE THE DEGREE OF WEAR IN THE SLIPS, BOWLS AND MASTER BUSHING.

3.8.8	CASING ELEVATORS
1	RECORD LAST 100% MAGNETIC PARTICLE INSPECTION FOR EACH SET OF ELEVATORS.

3.8.9	5" DP ELEVATORS
1	RECORD TYPE, PART NUMBER AND SERIAL NUMBER.
2	RECORD LAST 100% MPI INSPECTION FOR EACH SET OF DP ELEVATORS.

3.8.10	DP ELEVATORS
1	CHECK OPERATION OF LATCH AND SAFETY CATCH; ENSURE THAT LATCHES RETURN PROPERLY AND THAT SPRINGS ARE IN GOOD CONDITION.
2	CHECK CONDITION OF LINK BLOCKS AND PINS. CHECK DATE OF LAST THIRD PARTY MPI.

3.8.11	PIPE SPINNER
1	OPERATE SPINNER, CHECK OPERATING PRESSURE. CHECK CONDITION OF HOSES.

3.8.12	KELLY SPINNER
1	IF IN STORAGE, CHECK THAT MAINTENANCE IS BEING CARRIED OUT AND THAT THE UNIT IS READY FOR SERVICE.

3.8.13	CASING TONGS
1	CHECK FOR VERTICAL MOVEMENT OF LONG JAW, SHORT JAW, LUG JAW AND LATCH. EXCESSIVE MOVEMENT INDICATES WEAR OF HOLES AND/OR HINGE PINS.
2	CHECK CONDITION OF DIES AND MAKE SURE COTTER PIN OR ROLL PIN IS IN PLACE (ONE ON TOP, ONE AT BOTTOM).
3	CHECK LATCH ENGAGEMENT FOR PROPER FUNCTIONING OF MECHANISM.
4	CHECK HANGER, FASTENERS AND BALANCING SCREW FOR WEAR OR DAMAGE.

3.8.14	DRILL COLLAR TONGS
1	CHECK FOR VERTICAL MOVEMENT OF LONG JAW, SHORT JAW, LUG JAW AND LATCH.
2	CHECK CONDITION OF DIES AND MAKE SURE COTTER PIN OR ROLL PIN IS IN PLACE.
3	CHECK LATCH ENGAGEMENT FOR PROPER FUNCTIONING OF MECHANISM.
4	CHECK HANGER, FASTENERS AND BALANCING SCREW FOR WEAR OR DAMAGE.

3.8.15	EZY TORQ
1	CHECK PM RECORDS FOR ANNUAL THIRD PARTY DISASSEMBLY AND INSPECTION. RECORD DATE.

3.8.16	IRON ROUGHNECK
1	CHECK HYDRAULIC FLUID RESERVOIR LEVEL.
2	CHECK HYDRAULIC HOSES FOR CHAFFING, WEAR AND AGING.
3	CHECK THAT CONTROL LEVERS RETURN TO NEUTRAL AUTOMATICALLY WHEN CONTROLS ARE RELEASED.
4	CHECK HYDRAULIC CYLINDERS FOR LEAKAGE AROUND THE ROD PACKING.
5	CHECK ALL PRESSURE GAUGES FOR DAMAGE OR MALFUNCTION.
6	INSPECT TONG DIES FOR WEAR; DIES SHOULD BE A TIGHT FIT IN THE SLOTS (ANY MOVEMENT IS UNACCEPTABLE).
7	INSPECT ALL SENSORS, CABLES, JUNCTION BOX ENCLOSURES ETC [IF FITTED].
8	REFER TO PM RECORDS; ENSURE ANNUAL STRIP DOWN AND MPI HAS BEEN SATISFACTORILY CARRIED OUT. RECORD DATE.
9	FUNCTION IRON ROUGHNECK IN AUTOMATIC MODE [IF AUTO FUNCTION EXISTS].

3.8.17	POWER TONG
1	CHECK TONG DOOR LATCH ASSEMBLY FOR PROPER OPERATION AND SPRING TENSION.
2	CHECK HYDRAULIC MOTOR AND CONTROL VALVE HOLD-DOWN BOLTS FOR TIGHTNESS.
3	CHECK TORQUE GAUGE ASSEMBLY TO ASSURE ACCURATE TORQUE READING. THE LOAD CELL PISTON SHOULD EXTEND A MAXIMUM OF 1/4" FROM THE CELL WITH SYSTEM FILLED UNDER NO LOAD.
4	CHECK ALL HYDRAULIC HOSES FOR CRACKED, COLLAPSED, OR WORN CONDITION.
5	CONNECT TO THE HYDRAULIC SYSTEM; CHECK TONG UNIT FOR HYDRAULIC OIL LEAKS AND ENSURE ALL FUNCTIONS ARE OPERATING PROPERLY.
6	STIFF ARM, SAFETY GATE, BACKUP POSITIONER, HOSES & FITTINGS IN GOOD CONDITION.
7	SNUB LINES & JERK LINES IN GOOD CONDITION; PROPERLY SECURED AT BOTH ENDS - 3 CLAMPS OR PRESSED FITTINGS; CLAMPS ON DEAD END.
8	JAGGED ENDS OF WIRE ROPE ON TONG LINES CUT & TAPED TO PREVENT INJURY. NO FRAYED ROPE.
9	BODY AND JAWS IN GOOD CONDITION; DIES SHARP & KEEPERS IN POSITION. HANDLE SAFETY PIN.
10	COUNTER WEIGHTS ADEQUATELY SECURED; ALL WEIGHTS INSIDE COUNTERWEIGHT BUCKET; COUNTERWEIGHT ALLOWS OVERHEAD CLEARANCE.
11	GUARDED & IN GOOD CONDITION.

3.9	TRANSPORT – MOVE – POSITION

3.9.1	MOBILE CRANE
1	START ENGINE AND CARRY OUT A CHECK OF ALL FUNCTIONS IN CAB: ENGINE WATER TEMPERATURE GAUGE, ENGINE OIL PRESSURE GAUGE, WINDSHIELD WIPERS, HEATER, HORN AND LIGHTS. ON HYDRAULIC CRANES CHECK CONTROL PRESSURE AND RECORD.
2	CHECK ALL CRANE OPERATIONS WITH DRIVER. ENSURE HE IS SATISFIED WITH COMPLETE CONTROL SYSTEM.
3	CHECK REAR MOTION ALARM.
4	CHECK OPERATION OF BOOM SAVER LIMIT SWITCHES.
5	THOROUGHLY INSPECT ALL WIRE ROPE AND PENDANT LINES FOR DETERIORATION: REDUCTION OF ROPE DIAMETER, BROKEN OR WORN WIRES, CORRODED OR CRACKED END CONNECTIONS OR SEVERE KINKING.
6	CHECK THE CONDITION OF THE RUBBING PLATES BETWEEN THE BOOM TELESCOPING SECTIONS.
7	CHECK THE CONDITION OF ALL HYDRAULIC CYLINDERS AND ASSOCIATED FLEXIBLES AND FIXED PIPE-WORK.
8	CHECK THE CONDITION OF ALL ELECTRICAL WIRING.
9	REVIEW ALL OIL ANALYSIS RESULTS FROM LAST 12 MONTHS LOOKING FOR INCREASED METAL CONTENT.
10	REVIEW THE LIFTING GEAR INSPECTION FOR THE CRANE AND ENSURE THAT ALL CRITICAL COMPONENTS WERE NDT INSPECTED.

3.9.2	CRANE LOAD WATCHER
1	VERIFY CALIBRATION OF BOOM AND WHIP LINE BY LIFTING A SERIES OF KNOWN WEIGHTS.
2	CHECK THAT CAUTION AND OVERLOAD ALARMS ARE FUNCTIONAL.

3.9.3	VEHICLE (CAR, BUS, PICK-UP)
1	CHECK ALL LEVELS: OIL, WATER, STEERING AND BRAKE LIQUIDS. START THE ENGINE AND CHECK FOR ABNORMAL NOISE OR VIBRATIONS.
2	CHECK: LIGHTS, HORN, MIRRORS, WIND-SCREEN WIPERS, PRESSURE AND WEAR OF TYRES.
3	PERFORM A ROAD TEST AND VERIFY: BRAKES, STEERING, SHOCK ABSORBERS, AIR CONDITIONER.
4	CHECK THE CALIBRATION OF THE DRIVE-RIGHT SYSTEM.
5	VERIFY THE SAFETY EQUIPMENT: SEAT-BELTS, EXTINGUISHER, FIRST AID KIT, SPARE WHEEL, ROLL-OVER BARS, SAFETY NET, SHOVEL.

3.9.4	DEPTH SOUNDER
1	CHECK THAT ALL CONTROLS ARE FUNCTIONAL.

3.9.5	LIGHTS: NAVIGATION
1	VISUALLY CHECK NAVIGATION LIGHT RUNNING PANEL FOR LOOSE OR BROKEN SOCKETS, INSECURE OR BROKEN MOUNTS, AND LOOSE ELECTRICAL CONNECTIONS.
2	VISUALLY CHECK EXTERIOR LIGHT FIXTURES FOR LOOSE OR BROKEN SOCKETS, DAMAGED REFLECTORS, BROKEN OR CRACKED GLASSWARE, AND CORRODED OR DAMAGED FITTINGS.
3	USING A LOW VOLTAGE MEGGER (100V), CHECK LIGHT CIRCUITS FOR GROUND. RECORD VALUES BELOW .5 MEGOHMS.
4	TEST NAVIGATION RUNNING LIGHT PANEL LAMP OUTAGE ALARM CIRCUIT.

3.9.6	OBSTRUCTION LIGHTS
1	VISUALLY CHECK EXTERIOR LIGHT FIXTURES FOR LOOSE OR BROKEN SOCKETS, DAMAGED REFLECTORS, BROKEN OR CRACKED GLASSWARE.
2	CHECK BATTERIES FOR DAMAGE AND DETERIORATION. RECORD VOLTAGE.
3	USING LOW VOLT MEGGER (250V), CHECK MAIN LIGHT CIRCUITS FOR GROUND.
4	CHECK OPERATION OF AUTO CHANGEOVER DEVICES IN LIGHT FIXTURES.
5	CHECK THAT EACH LAMP FAILURE ALARM STATION IS OPERATIONAL.
6	ENSURE LIGHTS ARE FLASHING THE CORRECT MORSE CODE 'U'.
7	TIME THE PERIOD BETWEEN EACH SIGNAL AND RECORD.
8	CHECK MAIN/STANDBY LIGHT CHANGEOVER CIRCUIT IS FUNCTIONING.
9	CHECK THAT ALL LIGHT COOLING FANS ARE OPERATIONAL.
10	MEASURE VOLTAGE OF 15 MILE LIGHTS UNDER LOAD - RECORD.

3.9.7	FOG HORN
1	VISUALLY INSPECT EXTERIOR FITTINGS FOR LOOSE OR BROKEN MOUNTS, EROSION OR DAMAGE.
2	ENERGISE 2 MILE FOG HORN AND ENSURE IT IS FULLY OPERATIONAL.
3	OPEN FOG HORN CONTROL BOX AND SIMULATE A FAILURE BY REMOVING THE FUSE.
4	ENSURE THE 1/2 MILE STANDBY UNIT IS OPERATIONAL WITH NO DEFECTS EVIDENT.
5	CHECK THAT THE ALARM PANEL INDICATES ANY FAILURE OF THE 2 MILE FOG HORN.

3.9.8	BALLAST SYSTEM
1	CHECK RIG PM RECORDS FOR MAINTENANCE OF THIS EQUIPMENT (STRAINER REQUIRES TO BE CLEANED ANNUALLY AS PER PM STANDARD).

3.9.9	THRUSTER
1	LOWER THE PRIME MOVER BOTTOM BEARING COVER AND INSPECT BEARING FOR PITTING AND BRINELLING. RECORD AXIAL CLEARANCE.
2	REMOVE THE LOWER COVER PLATE OF JACKSHAFT COUPLING, INSPECT O-RING SEAL AND TEETH CONDITION.
3	OPEN THE VOID CHAMBER (BALLAST TANK) AND INSPECT THE KING PIN AREA (TANK SHOULD BE DRY). DO NOT ENTER UNTIL VENTILATED AND VERIFIED SAFE.
4	CHECK OPERATION OF BILGE PUMP BY FILLING THE SUMP FROM THE SEAWATER SYSTEM.
5	REPORT ON DATE AND CONDITION OF LAST AZIMUTH GEARBOX OIL ANALYSIS.
6	REPORT ON DATE AND CONDITION OF LAST STRUT OIL ANALYSIS. TAKE A SAMPLE FOR ANALYSIS.
7	CHECK CONNECTION OF THE MOTOR EXCITER SPACE HEATERS AND IF OPERATIONAL.
8	MEGGER EXCITER WITH 500V MEGGER TO GROUND AND RECORD.
9	CHECK KING PIN SLIP RING ASSEMBLY BRUSHES FOR PROPER ALIGNMENT AND SEATING, BRUSH WEAR AND TENSION.
10	MEGGER MOTOR WITH 1000V MEGGER AND RECORD FIELD AND ARMATURE.
11	FUNCTION TEST THE ALARMS OF THE SCR DRIVE.
12	FUNCTION TEST LEVEL SWITCHES ON HYDRAULIC OIL TANK KING PIN GEARCASE.
13	RUN THRUSTER SLOWLY THROUGH ITS POWER RANGE.
14	CHECK MOTOR BEARINGS FOR EXCESSIVE NOISE AND TEMPERATURE.
15	VISUALLY CHECK FOR EXCESSIVE BRUSH ARCING FROM AZIMUTH DRIVE MOTOR.
16	TEST ALL LOCK-OUT FUNCTIONS.

3.9.10	BALLAST PUMP
1	INDIVIDUALLY PUMP FROM SEA WITH EACH BALLAST PUMP, RECORD FLOW AND PSI FOR EACH PUMP. RECORD RIG DRAFT.
2	CHECK SHAFT SEAL AREA FOR LEAKAGE (MECHANICAL SEAL).
3	CHECK WITH ELECTRICIAN AND TAKE ACTUAL MOTOR LOAD AMPERAGE READINGS AT A KNOWN DRAFT. COMPARE WITH PREVIOUS READINGS. PUMP OVERHAUL NECESSARY IF OVERALL DROP OFF OF 30%.

3.9.11	ACTUATOR: AIR BALLAST VALVE
1	WITH BALLAST SEA VALVE OPEN, SHUT CIRCUIT BREAKER OFF - VALVE SHOULD CLOSE SIMULATING POWER FAILURE.
2	CHECK TO SEE IF THE EMERGENCY AIR VESSEL WILL HOLD ITS PRESSURE IN CASE RIG AIR PRESSURE IS LOST. RECORD PSI AT START AND AFTER HALF HOUR.
3	CHECK THE NUMBER OF TIMES THE SEA SUCTION CAN BE OPENED AND CLOSED BEFORE RUNNING OUT OF AIR.

3.9.12	BALLAST VALVES
1	OPERATE ALL BALLAST VALVES MANUALLY AND VERIFY CORRECT OPERATION; ENSURE VALVES RETURNED TO CORRECT POSITION.
2	VERIFY THE BALLAST SEA SUCTION IS NOT LEAKING.
3	VERIFY CORRECT OPERATION OF THE BALLAST OVERBOARD DISCHARGE CHECK VALVES.
4	VERIFY CORRECT OPERATION OF PUMP BILGE CHECK VALVE.
5	OPEN AND CLOSE THE MANUALLY OPERATED BLOCK VALVE THAT CONNECTS THE TWO BALLAST MANIFOLD LINES. VERIFY ALL INDICATOR LIGHTS OPERATE PROPERLY.

3.9.13	VALVE: CLA-VAL 8"
1	CHECK OPERATION OF ALL FOUR VALVES FROM THE TDC CONSOLE.
2	OPEN CONTROL PANEL, CARRY OUT MANUAL OVERRIDE IN BOTH OPEN AND CLOSED POSITIONS.
3	CHECK THE ENTIRE CONTROL SYSTEM FOR LEAKS.
4	CHECK OPERATION OF THE KENETROL VENT VALVES.

3.9.14	CHECK VALVE - CENTERLINE
1	CHECK MAINTENANCE RECORDS TO ENSURE THAT EACH VALVE IS BEING PULLED AND INSPECTED/REPAIRED ANNUALLY.

3.9.15	TANK GAUGING SYSTEM
1	OPERATE EVERY MOTOR OPERATED VALVE ON THE BALLAST DISPLAY CONSOLE FOR ONE COMPLETE CYCLE WHILE CHECKING FOR LEAKING SEALS, UNUSUAL NOISES OR DEFICIENCIES.
2	VERIFY THAT EACH MOTOR OPERATED VALVE INDICATOR LIGHT OPERATES PROPERLY ON THE CONSOLE.
3	TRANSFER BALLAST CONTROL TO BACKUP PANEL AND OPERATE EACH MOTOR OPERATED VALVE.
4	VERIFY EACH MOTOR OPERATED VALVE AND PUMP INDICATOR LIGHT OPERATES AT BACKUP PANEL.
5	TRANSFER BALLAST CONTROL BACK TO MAIN CONSOLE. ENSURE SYSTEM IS ON THE CORRECT SIGNAL POSITION.

3.9.16	SECONDARY DEBALLAST SYSTEM
1	START THE PUMP AND RECORD THE ACTUAL MOTOR LOAD AMPERAGE AND PRESSURE READING AT A KNOWN DRAFT. COMPARE TO PREVIOUS READINGS.
2	CHECK PUMPING SYSTEM FOR ANY OTHER OPERATIONAL FAULTS.
3	MEGGER TEST AND RECORD INSULATION READING, COMPARE WITH PREVIOUS READING.

3.9.17	BILGE SYSTEM
1	VERIFY PROPER AUTOMATIC OPERATION OF THE BILGE PUMPS BY ACTIVATING THE FLOAT MANUALLY TILL THE PUMP RUNS.
2	VERIFY AT ALL STATIONS THAT RUNNING LIGHTS AND AUDIBLE ALARMS ARE OPERATING PROPERLY.
3	CHECK WITH CONTROL ROOM THAT THE PRIMARY HIGH LEVEL ALARM IS ACTIVATED WHEN RAISED PAST STARTING LEVEL.
4	CHECK BILGE SUCTION CHECK VALVE FOR BLOCKAGE.
5	CHECK SHAFT SEAL FOR LEAKAGE (MECHANICAL SEAL).
6	CHECK WITH ELECTRICIAN AND TAKE ACTUAL MOTOR LOAD AMPERE READINGS AT A KNOWN DRAFT. PUMP OVERHAUL NECESSARY IF AMPERE READINGS DECREASED BY 25%.

3.9.18	REACH RODS
1	CHECK MAINTENANCE RECORDS TO ENSURE THAT ALL REACH RODS ARE BEING EXERCISED ON A MONTHLY BASIS.

3.9.19	ACOUSTIC POSITION INDICATOR
1	RUN SYSTEM SELF TEST AND RECORD ANY DEFICIENCIES.

3.9.20	ANCHOR WINDLASS
1	CHECK PHYSICAL CONDITION OF GEARBOX OIL. CHECK AND REPORT ON LAST ANALYSIS PLUS DATE TAKEN.
2	INSPECT ENTIRE WINDLASS ASSEMBLY FOR SIGNS OF DAMAGE, WEAR, EXCESS RUST AND CORROSION.
3	INSPECT CHAIN SEAL RUBBER FACING FOR DAMAGE OR EXCESSIVE WEAR IN ONE AREA.
4	GAUGE LOAD CELL GAP, SHOULD BE 3/4" TO 1/2".
5	REMOVE GEARBOX INSPECTION COVER AND CHECK GEAR TRAIN FOR CONDITION AND UNIFORM WEAR.
6	INSPECT BULLGEAR TRAIN FOR CONDITION AND UNIFORM WEAR.
7	INSPECT BACK STOPPING BRAKE ASSEMBLY FOR DAMAGE AND BRAKE PAD WEAR.
8	CHECK RECORDS OF FLEX COUPLING LUBRICATION.
9	CHECK MECHANICAL BRAKE ADJUSTMENT.
10	CHECK EMERGENCY PAY-OUT OF CHAIN.
11	CHAIN WASH DOWN WORKING.
12	CHECK BRAKE BLOCKS WEAR (REPLACE WHEN THICKNESS IS 5/8" OR LESS).
13	CHECK OPERATION OF HAND BRAKE. CHECK WINDLASS CONTROL CONSOLE FOR LOOSE TERMINATION, INSULATION DAMAGE, BURNED-OUT LAMPS, MOISTURE.

3.9.21	WINDLASS FOOTAGE COUNTER
1	VERIFY THAT REMOTE FOOTAGE READ OUT CORRESPONDS WITH THE MECHANICAL READ OUT.
2	VERIFY TENSION READ-OUTS AND RECORD LOCAL AND REMOTE.

3.9.22	WINDLASS MOTOR
1	TAKE FINAL MEGGER READINGS, MOTOR FIELD AND ARMATURE, RECORD AS FOUND.

3.9.23	CHAIN LOCKER SEAL
1	BEFORE STARTING OPERATION - PHONE CONTROL ROOM. VERIFY THE "OPEN" AND "CLOSED" INDICATION IS OPERATING.
2	CHECK OPERATING AIR PRESSURE. GAUGE SHOULD READ BETWEEN 90 AND 110 PSI.
3	CHECK TO SEE THAT THE 'SET' INDICATOR IS ILLUMINATED ON THE INDICATOR PANEL AND THE SEALS ARE CLOSED.
4	OPEN THE CHAIN LOCKER SEALS BY PRESSING THE 'OPEN' PUSH BUTTON.
5	OPERATE THE OPENING AND CLOSING FUNCTIONS AND RECORD THE TIME TO OPEN AND CLOSE THE DOORS.
6	CHECK FOR LEAKING AIR LINE HOSE AND THAT DOORS ARE SEALED PROPERLY (CHECK FOR MISALIGNMENT OF THE PROFILED RUBBER SEALS AND THE ANCHOR CHAIN).

3.10	RIG SPECIAL OPERATIONS

3.10.1	COILED TUBING
1	PRESSURE CONTROL EQUIPMENT CERTIFICATION PAPER/HISTORY
2	IS THE BOP CONFIGURATION SUFFICIENT TO ALLOW FOR ALL FORESEEABLE CIRCUMSTANCES? (CLOSING WELL WITH CTU, CLOSING ON BHA, CLOSING WITH NOTHING IN THE WELL; BOP CONTROLS WITHIN SAFE ZONE OR SECONDARY SET REQUIRED)
3	ARE SCHEMATICS OF THE WELLHEAD/BOP/DOWNHOLE TOOLS MAINTAINED IN THE CT CABIN?
4	IS A DETAILED BOP TEST PROCEDURE IN PLACE?
5	OPERATOR/SUPERVISOR WELL CONTROL CERTIFICATION AND RELEVANT COURSES
6	HAVE APPROPRIATE COILCADE SIMULATIONS BEEN RUN?
7	COIL TUBING LIFE RECORDS/LIMITS
8	HAVE CRANE CAPACITIES BEEN REVIEWED FOR ADEQUACY?
9	IS SURFACE PIPING EQUIPMENT GOING THROUGH PERIODIC INSPECTION AND CERTIFICATION?
10	SAFETY SLINGS/CHAINS IN PLACE
11	RIG UP ACCORDING TO POLICY: INJECTOR HEAD JACKED-UP WHILE WORKING UNDER IT, KILL LINE, NEW RING GASKET, NO PRESSURE LINE BELOW CTU
12	HAS IT BEEN SERVICED AND INSPECTED PER STANDARD?
13	HAS LOCATION BEEN VIEWED/SURVEYED FOR EQUIPMENT LAYOUT?
14	HAVE COMBUSTIBLE GAS AND H2S MONITORS BEEN SPECIFIED?
15	ARE THERE ANY PLANS FOR A "TRIAL" RIG-UP OF EQUIPMENT, EITHER IN THE YARD OR AT THE WELLSITE?
16	HAVE THE APPROPRIATE PROCEDURES BEEN DEVELOPED FOR: FIRE AND EVACUATION, PRESSURE DEPLOYMENT, WELLHEAD/BOP LEAK, WELL CONTROL, SEVERE FLUID LOSSES, MEDICAL EMERGENCY, DRILLS, SAFETY MEETINGS, WELLSITE SECURITY, TEMPORARY SUSPENSION OF OPERATIONS, CT LEAK/FAILURE?
17	ARE THERE ADEQUATE MEANS OF COMMUNICATION BETWEEN CT OPERATOR AND THE CREW?
18	ARE SAFETY MEETINGS HELD AND ATTENDED BY ALL PERSONNEL AT THE WELLSITE?
19	IS A COPY OF THE COMPANY SLPM AT THE WELLSITE?
20	IS A COPY OF THE WELL CONTROL MANUAL AT THE WELLSITE?
21	IS THE EQUIPMENT LAYOUT/SETUP SAFE AND EFFICIENT?
22	ARE THE NECESSARY GAS SENSORS IN PLACE AND FUNCTIONING?
23	ARE THESE SENSORS FREQUENTLY TESTED AND CALIBRATED?
